# Technical Debt

## Inter-Service Protocol Inconsistency

- [ ] **AC internal APIs use HTTP/JSON without shared contracts**: All other inter-service communication (GC↔MC, GC↔MH, MC↔MH) uses gRPC with proto-defined contracts in `proto/internal.proto`. AC is the exception — its internal APIs (`/api/v1/auth/internal/meeting-token`, `/api/v1/auth/internal/guest-token`, `/api/v1/auth/token`) are HTTP/JSON with request/response structs defined independently on each side. This caused a production-visible bug: GC's `MeetingTokenRequest` defined `home_org_id: Option<Uuid>` while AC's expects `home_org_id: Uuid` (required), resulting in 422 errors at runtime. No compile-time check catches these mismatches. Options: (a) migrate AC internal APIs to gRPC and add to `internal.proto`, or (b) create a shared `ac-api-types` crate that both AC and GC import. The `common::token_manager` (OAuth token fetch) is already shared but the meeting/guest token request types are not. Locations: `crates/gc-service/src/services/ac_client.rs` (GC's structs), `crates/ac-service/src/models/mod.rs` (AC's structs).

- [ ] **`RejectionReason` lacks an `INVALID_REQUEST` variant**: `proto/internal.proto:189-194` defines `RejectionReason` as `UNSPECIFIED|AT_CAPACITY|DRAINING|UNHEALTHY`. When MC's `AssignMeetingWithMh` rejects a malformed request (e.g., empty `mh_assignments` or empty `MhAssignment.grpc_endpoint`), the handler at `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/mc_service.rs:248-251` maps the `McError::InvalidArgument` to `RejectionReason::Unhealthy`, which is misleading — MC is healthy, GC sent bad data. GC consumers may interpret this as a transient MC issue and retry, which won't help. Add `REJECTION_REASON_INVALID_REQUEST` and route invalid-argument paths to it. Low frequency (GC contract violations should never happen), but the right semantic for observability and correct GC behavior under bug conditions.

- [ ] **Pre-existing pnpm-audit HIGH findings on `nx@20.3.0 → minimatch@9.0.3`** (surfaced 2026-05-06 during devloop `2026-05-06-client-proto-codegen-pipeline` Gate 2 audit; confirmed unchanged from start commit `223aa6dc` via `git stash + pnpm install + pnpm audit`, NOT introduced by that devloop). `pnpm audit --audit-level=high` reports 3 HIGH advisories — all minimatch ReDoS variants: GHSA-3ppc-4f35-3m26 (repeated wildcards with non-matching literal), GHSA-7r86-cg39-jmmj (matchOne combinatorial backtracking via multiple non-adjacent GLOBSTAR segments), GHSA-23c5-xmqv-rm74 (nested *() extglobs catastrophic backtracking). Single transitive path `.>nx>minimatch`; only consumer is `nx@20.3.0` which landed in browser-client-join task #1. **Threat model**: dev/CI-only — Nx uses minimatch for project-graph globbing on developer-authored `project.json`/`nx.json` glob patterns, NOT user-controlled input; not browser-bundled. Practical exploit requires repo write access (committing a malicious glob), at which point graver options exist. **@security accepted defer (Option 1) on 2026-05-06**. Two resolution paths: (a) bump `nx@20.3.0` to a version with patched minimatch (workspace-wide change, possible API surface; nx@22.x has API changes vs. 20.x); (b) add `pnpm overrides` to root `package.json` pinning `minimatch>=9.0.7` (workspace-wide but minimal blast radius; sets a precedent for the "do we use pnpm overrides for remediation" convention). Owners: operations + infrastructure (workspace-wide dep + lockfile convention decisions). Constraint for browser-client-join task #17 (`ci-client.yml`): the workflow's `pnpm audit --audit-level=high` step (per task #17 plan) will currently report these 3 HIGH findings — task #17 should either (i) include the bump/override and resolve here, or (ii) configure the audit gate to allowlist these specific advisory IDs with rationale, or (iii) run audit non-blocking and add a follow-up. See `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-06-client-proto-codegen-pipeline/main.md` § Tech Debt References.

- [ ] **Pre-existing `buf lint` STANDARD-rule cleanup on `proto/*.proto`** (surfaced 2026-05-06 during devloop `2026-05-06-client-proto-codegen-pipeline` Gate 2 verification; confirmed unchanged from start commit `223aa6dc` via `git stash`, NOT introduced by that devloop). `pnpm exec buf lint` against `proto/` produces 21 findings collapsing to 5 pattern categories: (1) per-package directory layout (~6 findings — STANDARD expects `proto/dark_tower/internal/internal.proto` + `proto/dark_tower/signaling/signaling.proto` rather than the current flat `proto/*.proto`), (2) version-suffixed package names (~2 — `dark_tower.internal` should be `dark_tower.internal.v1`; wire-breaking since fully-qualified message names embed package), (3) multi-package directory (~2 — resolved together with #1), (4) paired RPC request/response naming (~9 — STANDARD requires `RegisterParticipant` / `RegisterParticipantResponse` to be `RegisterRequest` / `RegisterResponse` or service-prefixed; affects all 5 internal services), (5) one-to-one request/response uniqueness (~2 — `HeartbeatResponse` reused across `FastHeartbeat` and `ComprehensiveHeartbeat` violates STANDARD's per-RPC unique-response rule). **Constraint inherited by browser-client-join task #17** (CI workflow): `proto-gen:lint` Nx target was wired in task #7 as runnable but currently exits non-zero against these 21 findings; the `buf lint` CI gate cannot be flipped to ENFORCED until this cleanup lands or the rules are configured-out with rationale. Three resolution paths: (a) full cleanup — move files + rename packages + rename RPC types — wire-breaking, requires coordinated proto + Rust + TS change spanning every consumer of `proto-gen`, likely warrants a `/debate` first; (b) move files only (categories 1+3) and add `buf:lint:ignore` annotations for 2+4+5 with rationale; (c) configure `proto/buf.yaml` `lint.except: [...]` to disable the rules entirely with design-intent commentary. Owner: protocol (file moves + rename judgments) + global-controller / meeting-controller / media-handler (RPC consumer impact). See `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-06-client-proto-codegen-pipeline/main.md` § Tech Debt References for full breakdown.

- [ ] **Dead `accepted: bool` field in three registration response messages**: `RegisterMHResponse` (`proto/internal.proto:231-235`), `RegisterMCResponse` (`:90-95`), and `RegisterMeetingResponse` (`:133-135`) all carry a `bool accepted = 1` field. Producer sites hardcode it `true` in every path: `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/mh_service.rs:176` (MH registration), `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/mc_service.rs:257` (MC registration), and the MH-side `RegisterMeeting` handler. The only error path in each is a typed `Err(Status::internal | ...)`; no conditional sets `accepted=false`. Consumer sites (`crates/mh-service/src/grpc/gc_client.rs:152-179, :296`; parallel sites in MC) have real branching logic that handles the unreachable `accepted=false` case and log-then-error. Surfaced during ADR-0032 Step 2 review when security noticed `mh_gc_registration_total{status=success}` was emitted unconditionally on `Ok(response)` regardless of `accepted`; initial framing was a P1 metric-labeling bug, but investigation showed the latent-not-active shape: the metric would be wrong *if* rejections happened, but GC never rejects. Resolution: remove the field from all three messages and delete the unreachable consumer-side branching. Constraints: (a) proto3 wire compat — a two-step rollout (reserve field + keep always-true first, delete in next release) avoids new-server × old-client skew issues where old clients would read the missing field as proto3 default `false` and fail registration. (b) 3 messages × 3 producers × 3 consumers is cross-service scope — protocol specialist ownership, likely wants a small /debate first to ratify the two-step. (c) Downstream: delete `gc_integration.rs::test_gc_client_registration_rejected` (tests an impossible state). Not urgent — metric bug cannot fire in production absent a GC-side code change that doesn't exist. Schedule after ADR-0032 Steps 3-5 complete so protocol changes don't overlap with MC/AC/GC metric-test backfills.

- [ ] **Add a referent-durability rule to `docs/protocol/CONVENTIONS.md`**: proposed rule — *a stored reference whose referent can be invalidated without the artifact itself being rewritten must either be revalidated at every use, or not cross the artifact boundary at all.* The checkable test a future author applies is **"can this referent stop resolving while the artifact stays byte-identical?"** Surfaced 2026-08-13 at devloop `2026-08-13-run-story-seams-and-hermetic-tests` Gate 1, while choosing the field set for `dt-story list-tasks`.

  **Lead with the positive instance — the codebase already independently arrived at the discipline.** `run-story.sh` persists a devloop-output slug to `$slug_file` and never trusts it on read: `:331-334` re-checks `[ -f "docs/devloop-outputs/${s}/main.md" ]` before setting `continue_slug`, and `:429-431` repeats the same check before writing it, falling back to `continue_slug=""` and a fresh start when it fails. That is a stored `String` reference whose referent can stop resolving (directory removed, or never committed) and the runner handles it correctly: **revalidate at use, never trust at read**. This matters more to a conventions doc than the two negative instances do — two excluded fields argue a hazard exists, whereas a third site where the discipline was reached independently is evidence the rule *describes existing practice* rather than imposing new ceremony, which is the difference between a convention people follow and one they route around. It also supplies the remedy half that "don't emit it" alone lacks: for a reference that genuinely must cross the boundary, `:331-334` is the worked example.

  **The two negative instances, each invisible to the argument that found the other.** In `crates/dt-story/src/manifest.rs`, one nine-field struct: (1) `escalation` (`:54`) holds a `$RUN_DIR` path under `${DEVLOOP_TMP:-/tmp/devloop}` (`run-story.sh:136-138`) — the manifest is git-tracked and durable, the referent vanishes with the container, so a completed story permanently cites evidence that no longer exists; (2) `commit` (`:52`) holds a git sha and is *not* path-valued — `run-story.sh:493-497` declines to populate it in a comment stating the mechanism outright ("amending changes the sha, so it can't be recorded inside the commit it refers to") and `:500` amends, so any sha recordable there is stale by construction. Neither field has a reader in `run-story.sh`, `preflight-story.sh` or `validate-story-manifest.sh`; the only references to `commit` anywhere are inside `dt-story` itself (`main.rs:55,196,206`, `engine.rs:136,145,192`, `manifest.rs:52`). A path-durability argument does not see `commit`; an amend-invalidates-sha argument does not see `escalation`.

  **The load-bearing half is that the rule must test a property, not match a shape.** All three references are `String`-typed, so references are invisible to the type system and shape-matching ("path-valued") is the failure mode — it would have caught `escalation`, missed `commit`, and missed the slug entirely. Both manifest fields are excluded from the `list-tasks` projection landing in that devloop, with the reason recorded in the projection struct's doc comment and held distinct from the projection's two other exclusions (`specialist` — no named consumer; `prompt` — uncontrolled value per R-2 defect 5) so each stays independently checkable; collapsing them into "don't emit extra stuff" would lose that `prompt` and `commit` fail for unrelated reasons and would need unrelated fixes.

  **Post-task-4 re-verification — MADE by `protocol` at task 4's Gate 3, 2026-08-17, against the tree as built** (staged by `operations`; this paragraph is the owner's claim, not the staging). Every instance below was re-read in the post-task-4 tree and the runner's two call sites executed, not inferred from the plan: `run-story.sh:294` still derives `RUN_DIR` from `${DEVLOOP_TMP:-/tmp/devloop}`, `:475` still passes that path as `--log`, and **neither** `complete` call site (`:1312`, `:1334`) passes `--commit`. Task 4 reshaped the manifest, so all three instances were re-read against the new tree. `escalation` is **unchanged** as a negative instance (still an ephemeral `$RUN_DIR` path under `${DEVLOOP_TMP:-/tmp/devloop}`). `commit` is **unchanged** as a negative instance, and now has a sibling worth noting: the runner still declines to populate it, and its slug derivation deliberately reads the *commit range* rather than recording a sha, so the amend-invalidates-sha argument is untouched. **`slug` is a new, THIRD kind** — the first reference the manifest *admits*, on the rule's own remedy branch: it travels with its referent (written in the same commit that contains the git-tracked `docs/devloop-outputs/<slug>/`), but a later commit deleting or renaming that directory invalidates it while the story file stays byte-identical, which is this rule's literal test. So it is admitted **on condition that consumers revalidate at use**, and they do — `/close-story` re-checks `main.md` exists before constructing a path, mirroring `run-story.sh`'s handling of `$slug_file`.

  **That strengthens the rule rather than complicating it**, and it is what this entry said it was waiting for: until now the only positive instance was *external* to the manifest, so the entry could only argue the discipline exists elsewhere. The manifest now carries a reference admitted under the rule's own remedy, with the remedy implemented at the consumer — which is the worked example a conventions doc needs before generalising beyond `dt-story`. **The contingency is discharged: the rule survived task 4 and is now ready to promote.** What remains open is only the `CONVENTIONS.md` text itself, owned by `protocol`.

  **One precision a future author will need, because the obvious reading is wrong.** `slug` is the first of these references that is **not** a bare `String` — it is a `Slug` newtype whose `TryFrom<String>` runs in the deserializer. Do **not** read that as the type system having absorbed this rule. The newtype encodes the **character class** (a shape), not the **durability property**; a `Slug` is as capable of pointing at a deleted directory as a `String` is. So the load-bearing half above — *references are invisible to the type system, so test the property, not the shape* — is **untouched by the newtype**, and the two must not be conflated when the rule is written into `CONVENTIONS.md`. The newtype is what makes `slug` safe to *project* (rule 2 in `main.rs`'s `TaskSummary` doc: never project an uncontrolled value); revalidation at use is what makes it safe to *store*. Two different hazards, two different mechanisms, and each one's fix is invisible to the other — the same structure as the `escalation`/`commit` pair that produced this entry.

  **Originally contingent on surviving story task 4** (`docs/user-stories/2026-08-11-story-runner-hardening.md` R-8), which reshapes the manifest schema — re-verify all three instances against the post-task-4 tree before promoting the rule, and only then generalise beyond `dt-story`. The generalisation is worth making: every artifact boundary in this repo that carries a reference has the same hazard, and all three known instances sit in the story workflow because that is where durable planning documents meet ephemeral run state. Owner: protocol (rule + `CONVENTIONS.md` text); originated with infrastructure (the `commit` instance, the property-not-shape narrowing, and the positive instance).

## Cross-Service Duplication (DRY)

### From DRY Reviewer (Ongoing)

- [ ] **The repo has TWO DNS-label rule families; story R-7 task #3 guarded one and left the other's 4 sites unguarded** (surfaced 2026-08-15 by dry-reviewer at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-15-layer7-per-run-org/`, task #3 of the story-runner-hardening story). **Family A — org subdomain** (the `{0,61}` spelling, which bounds the length INSIDE the pattern; the literal is deliberately not reproduced here, because `docs/TODO.md` is inside the guard's sweep and quoting it would red the pinned occurrence count — as it did while this entry was being written, which is the guard demonstrating it is not vacuous): 7 sites, now byte-identity-ENFORCED by `scripts/guards/simple/validate-subdomain-regex-sync.sh`. **Family B — slug / Kind cluster name**, the same DNS-label concept over a different value space, spelled with `*` instead of `{0,61}` and the 63-char bound carried as a SEPARATE check: `crates/devloop-helper/src/error.rs:104-160` (`ValidSlug::new` — hand-rolled byte loop, doc comment states the pattern as `^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?$`, length via `s.len() > 63`), `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:59` and `infra/kind/scripts/teardown.sh:17` (byte-identical `[[ ! "${CLUSTER_NAME}" =~ ^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?$ ]] || [[ ${#CLUSTER_NAME} -gt 63 ]]`), and `infra/devloop/devloop.sh:88` (`TASK_SLUG`, same regex, **no length bound at all**). **Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication** — Family B governs cluster names and filesystem path components, not org subdomains, so collapsing the two families would be wrong (different value spaces, different trust boundaries); per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked rather than fix-or-defer. **The actual risk is a reader's inference**: a repo that now ships a loud, self-tested drift guard for Family A reads as though DNS-label rules are guarded, and Family B's three shell copies + one Rust copy are not — `devloop.sh:88`'s missing length bound is already a live divergence from its two siblings. **Fix when acted on**: (a) decide whether `ValidSlug` is the canonical Family-B home (it is the only one that is already a named, testable helper) and have the shell sites either call it or be covered by a second enumerated-site table in the existing guard — the guard's `SITES` array generalises to a second family at near-zero cost; (b) settle whether `devloop.sh`'s `TASK_SLUG` genuinely wants no length bound or is simply missing one. **Explicitly NOT to be "fixed" by merging Family A and Family B into one pattern.** **Defer trigger**: any devloop touching `ValidSlug`, the Kind cluster-name validators, or adding a 5th Family-B site. Owner: infrastructure (+ dry-reviewer to re-check family separation at that point).

- [ ] **`DEVLOOP_TEST` seam-sentinel idiom + the git-containment predicate have no shared bash home — 7 and 2 sites respectively** (surfaced 2026-08-13 by dry-reviewer Gate 1/3 review of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-13-run-story-seams-and-hermetic-tests/`, task #1 of the story-runner-hardening story). Two idioms, **one shared root cause**, which is why they are filed together: `scripts/workflow/run-story.sh` deliberately sources no shared bash helper, and the only candidate home (`scripts/lang/_common.sh`) carries layer-lifecycle + STATUS-emission machinery the runner must not inherit. (a) **Seam sentinel**: `[[ "${DEVLOOP_TEST:-}" == "1" && -n "${OVERRIDE:-}" ]]` now appears at 7 production sites — `scripts/layer7.sh` (3: the seam block, the env-test command seam, the browser-suite command seam), `scripts/layer-all.sh`, `scripts/lang/_audit_gate.sh`, `scripts/workflow/run-story.sh`'s seam block, and (new 2026-08-15, story R-7 task #3) `scripts/guards/simple/validate-subdomain-regex-sync.sh`'s `SUBDOMAIN_GUARD_ROOT` scan-root seam. **Line numbers deliberately dropped** — they were stale within one devloop (`layer7.sh` grew 215 lines, moving two of the three), and `docs/**` bare line cites are guard-banned anyway; grep for the idiom instead — plus the function form (`__test_sentinel_active` + `__any_override_present` + per-override resolvers) unique to `scripts/audit-suppressions-check.sh:56-100`, which earns itself across four overrides. The *exact-match* semantics are the load-bearing part (`DEVLOOP_TEST=0` must NOT enable overrides — see the `audit-suppressions-check.sh:35-36` warning) and today they travel by comment, not by code. (b) **Git-containment predicate** (~5 lines: resolved root is its own `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` **and** `-ef`-differs from the real one) is now at 2 sites, `run-story.sh`'s seam block and `run-story.test.sh`'s suite-side pre-flight. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — nothing in a shared home was reimplemented, so per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. **Explicitly pre-cleared at Gate 1 and NOT to be "fixed" by collapsing (b) to one site**: the suite-side copy must not source the runner's function, because a test that borrows its safety from the artifact it verifies takes the operator's clone down on the exact run meant to catch a broken seam. Same expression, two trust purposes. **Fix when acted on**: a small `scripts/lang/_seam_sentinel.sh` (name it for the concern, not `common`/`util` — ADR-0034 §Neutral) exposing `seam_sentinel_active`, `seam_refuse_override_without_sentinel <var>...` and `seam_assert_contained_root <candidate> <real>`, sourced by the runner and by whichever of the 7 sites migrate. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: consumer #1 (`audit-suppressions-check.sh`) is an always-run security gate outside this task's changeset, and a new shared-bash-home decision for `scripts/workflow/` is a design call in its own right; the next devloop adding a 7th sentinel site, or any devloop already refactoring `audit-suppressions-check.sh`, is the natural moment. **DEFER TRIGGER FIRED 2026-08-15**: the 7th site landed (the drift guard's scan-root seam, added by story R-7 task #3, which needed the seam to drive its own guard's failure branches). Task #3 did not act on it — hoisting a shared bash home touches an always-run security gate outside that changeset — but the stated trigger condition is now met and this is a scheduling call for the Lead rather than a still-dormant entry. Owner: infrastructure (+ security to re-verify the exact-match semantics survive the hoist).

- [ ] **Four hermetic `env -i` test-runner functions with no common signature** (surfaced 2026-08-13 by dry-reviewer Gate 3 review of the same devloop). `run_with_cache` (`scripts/lang/_test_helpers.sh:67`), `run_la` (`scripts/layer-all.test.sh:75`), `run_layer7` (`scripts/layer7.test.sh:120`) and `run_story` (`scripts/workflow/run-story.test.sh:382`) each scrub the environment and invoke one target script. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication, and **filed with a standing recommendation NOT to unify as-is**: the genuinely shared part is three tokens (`env -i PATH="$PATH" HOME="$HOME"`), while the *output contracts* diverge irreconcilably — `run_with_cache` returns an rc on stdout, `run_la` sets four globals (`LA_OUT`/`LA_ERR`/`LA_DT`/`LA_RC`), `run_layer7` takes output paths as arguments, `run_story` sets six globals and parses a `--` argv separator. A unified signature parameterized over all of that is strictly more code than it saves, and the fifth consumer would want a sixth shape. This entry exists so the pattern is *counted* rather than re-litigated per devloop: it fails the two-consumer test on the **signature** half, not the count half. **Revisit trigger**: a 5th consumer that fits an existing output contract unchanged (i.e. genuine convergence rather than a 5th shape), or a decision to standardise self-test output contracts across `scripts/**/*.test.sh` — the latter being the only version of this worth doing. Owner: test (+ infrastructure).

- [ ] **Fail-loud "parse env var → positive integer, reject 0" config block now repeats across GC (×4) + MC (×1) — cross-service at N=2 services (surfaced 2026-08-06 by dry-reviewer Gate 2 review of task #64, roster-leave-latency)**: MC's new `MC_QUIC_MAX_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` parse (`crates/mc-service/src/config.rs:379-397`) is the same skeleton GC already carries four times: `RATE_LIMIT_RPM` (`crates/gc-service/src/config.rs:282`), `MC_STALENESS_THRESHOLD_SECONDS` (`:309`), `TELEMETRY_PROXY_MAX_BYTES` (`:359`), `TELEMETRY_PROXY_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE` (`:383`) — `if let Some(s) = vars.get(KEY) { let n = s.trim().parse::<u64>().map_err(|e| ConfigError::<Variant>(..))?; if n == 0 { return Err(..) } n } else { DEFAULT }`. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — no shared/`common` helper was reimplemented (config parsing is per-service by design per the DRY INDEX; `common/src/config.rs` has no such helper), so per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer, and it does NOT block task #64. **Caveat for the extractor (small shared surface)**: the truly-common part is only the parse + trim + zero-reject (~4 lines); each call site uses a *different* service-local `ConfigError` variant and bespoke message (MC: `InvalidValue`; GC: `InvalidRateLimit`/`InvalidMcStalenessThreshold`/`InvalidTelemetryProxyMaxBytes`/`InvalidTelemetryProxyRateLimit`), so a hoist must be generic over the error constructor — e.g. `common::config::parse_positive_u64(vars, key) -> Result<Option<u64>, ParseError>` with each caller mapping the `ParseError` to its own variant, or a closure-parameterized `parse_positive_u64_or(vars, key, default, |msg| ConfigError::X(msg))`. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: requires a `crates/common` addition plus retrofitting 4 pre-existing GC sites (cross-service coordination + a deliberate signature/home decision), beyond task #64's MC-only changeset. Natural moment: the next GC config-touching devloop, or a small observability/common-owned follow-up once a 3rd service adds a sibling. Owner: dry-reviewer to propose; global-controller + meeting-controller co-consumers; common home. Related but distinct from the R-55 OTel four-knob config-scaffold entries above (those hoist the OTel-specific block; this is the generic positive-int primitive underneath).
- [ ] **sdk-core wire-path constants (`REGISTER_PATH`/`LOGIN_PATH`/`MEETINGS_PATH`) are module-private, so the browser-E2E fixtures re-encode the same path literals (surfaced 2026-08-04 by dry-reviewer Gate 3 review of task #18, Playwright E2E harness)**: `packages/web-app/e2e/fixtures.ts` hardcodes `/api/v1/auth/register` + `/api/v1/auth/user/token` (as `waitForResponse` predicates in `signUpViaUi`/`signInViaUi`) and the `/api/v1/auth/` + `/api/v1/meetings` prefixes (in `assertTokenOnlyJoinTraffic`), while sdk-core owns the same literals as module-private consts — `packages/sdk-core/src/http/AuthApiClient.ts:24-25` (`REGISTER_PATH`/`LOGIN_PATH`) and `packages/sdk-core/src/http/MeetingApiClient.ts:26` (`MEETINGS_PATH`). Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — nothing importable exists to reuse (the consts are not exported through the closed barrel `packages/sdk-core/src/index.ts`), and per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. Two nuances for the extractor: (a) the fixture is an *observer* of UI-driven traffic, not a re-issuer — it duplicates path knowledge, not the request/wire mapping (that stays single-sourced: `bootstrapMeeting` consumes `MeetingApiClient` directly); (b) assertion (e)'s structural no-auth-calls guard arguably *wants* an independent literal (a test asserting absence shouldn't derive the forbidden prefix from the SUT's own constant — adjudicate at extraction time). Fix: export the three path consts (or a `WIRE_PATHS` object) from the sdk-core barrel and consume them for the `waitForResponse` predicates at minimum; sdk-core edit is client-owned. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: requires a client-owned `packages/sdk-core/**` edit, which task #18's Gate 1 scope decision explicitly excludes (no client reviewer on roster; sdk-core in the NOT-touched list); task #19 (pipeline integration, client-adjacent) or the next sdk-core http-surface change is the natural moment. Drift today fails loudly (`waitForResponse` timeout naming the stale path), so the exposure is confusion-cost, not silent breakage. **Task #19 adjudication (2026-08-05, dry-reviewer pre-adjudicated at Gate 1)**: NOT extracted — same no-client-reviewer constraint as #18, and #19 added no new wire-path literals (the negative specs observe paths only through the already-centralized `fixtures.ts` helpers); defer trigger is now "next sdk-core http-surface change". Owner: client (+ test as fixture consumer).

- [ ] **Per-service `*-logs.json` Grafana dashboards are a 4-way byte-identical-modulo-service-token clone-set — extraction/templating candidate (surfaced 2026-08-06 by dry-reviewer Gate 3 review of task #62, MH logs+SLO dashboards)**: with `mh-logs.json` landed, `infra/grafana/dashboards/{ac,gc,mc,mh}-logs.json` are all 374 lines and structurally identical after masking the `mh`/`mc`/`ac`/`gc` + service-name tokens (verified at review: the only cross-file diffs are `app="<svc>-service"` label positions, panel/dashboard titles, `uid`, and one description string). This is the deliberately-accepted per-service static-JSON pattern (one set per service, no cross-service templating) and per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception is TODO-tracked, NOT a fix-or-defer finding — but the logs flavor has now crossed the rule-of-three clone threshold (4 near-identical copies) and there is already a templating precedent in-tree: `infra/grafana/dashboards/_template-service-overview.json`. Concrete maintenance-cost evidence: the security-surfaced `level`-var Loki-stream scoping fix (this file, Observability Debt, task #62) must edit all four logs dashboards in lockstep — exactly the lockstep-edit class templating removes. **Scope note**: this applies to the *logs* set only — the `*-slos.json` set genuinely DIVERGES per service (277/486/843/981 lines; different SLO panels per service) and is NOT a clone-set, so do NOT fold SLOs into this. `*-overview.json` already has the `_template-service-overview.json` template as its extraction home. **Fix when acted on**: add a `_template-service-logs.json` (mirroring the existing overview-template precedent) parameterized on the service token/label/title/uid, and generate or lint the four `*-logs.json` against it so a change lands once. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: cross-service (touches all four service dashboards + needs a templating/generation mechanism decision that the project has only partially made — overview has a template, logs/slos do not) and beyond task #62's MH-only changeset; the next devloop editing any `*-logs.json` in lockstep (e.g. the `level`-var scoping fix) is the natural moment to land the template first. Owner: observability (dashboard content) + infrastructure (kustomize/generation wiring).

- [ ] **No drift guard between Prometheus rules files and the `docs/observability/alerts.md` inventory — expr/`for`/threshold are dual-encoded and only manually synced (surfaced 2026-08-03 by dry-reviewer Gate 2 review of task #16, GC telemetry obs artifacts)**: every entry in `docs/observability/alerts.md` restates its alert's PromQL expr, `for:` duration, severity, and threshold prose from `infra/docker/prometheus/rules/{gc,mc,mh,otel}-alerts.yaml` — a classic two-places encoding with no guard (`scripts/guards/simple/validate-alert-rules.sh` validates rules files only; it never opens alerts.md). Current state, verified programmatically at this review: **GC is 20/20 in perfect sync** (18 pre-existing + the 2 task-#16 telemetry alerts, whose inventory PromQL blocks are byte-identical to the rules exprs after YAML dedent — the verbatim-identity discipline was agreed with @implementer at task #16 Gate 1 precisely to keep a future mechanical guard feasible); but the pairing convention is **GC-only in practice** — MC has 15 rules vs only 4 inventory entries, MH 15 rules vs 0 entries, so inventory drift/absence already exists outside GC. Extraction opportunity (guard, not code), NOT true duplication — nothing was reimplemented in task #16; the dual encoding is the inventory's designed role (oncall-readable catalog) and per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. **Fix**: a small Layer-B guard (extend `validate-alert-rules.sh` or a sibling `validate-alert-inventory-sync.sh`) that for each `alert:` in each rules file (a) requires a `#### <AlertName>` heading in alerts.md, (b) compares the dedented `expr:` block against the entry's ```` ```promql ```` block byte-for-byte, and (c) compares the `for:` duration against the entry's `` `for: <dur>` `` line — failing loudly on drift or missing entry (the ~15-line dedent+compare already exists as a worked prototype in the task-#16 dry-review transcript). **Rollout decision required**: enforcing repo-wide immediately would fail on the 26 missing MC/MH entries — either backfill MC/MH first, or land the guard scoped to GC (+ a coverage allowlist that new services must join), and treat MC/MH backfill as its own docs task. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: guard authorship is outside task #16's docs/alerts changeset and needs the MC/MH-backfill-vs-scope decision; the next devloop touching any `*-alerts.yaml` or alerts.md should land the guard before adding more dual-encoded entries. Owner: observability (inventory + conventions home) with infrastructure (guard pipeline).

- [ ] **`bundle-content.test.ts` prod-bundle-scan harness duplicated across `sdk-core` + `web-app`** (surfaced 2026-07-05 by dry-reviewer Gate-3 review of the task #15 sdk-svelte/web-app devloop). Two packages now carry a structurally parallel build-artifact contract test that (a) runs a real production `vite build` in `beforeAll` via `pnpm exec vite build --mode production`, (b) recursively walks `dist/` (`listFiles`/`walk` — identical logic, renamed), and (c) scans every emitted file for a set of forbidden build-time tokens. `packages/sdk-core/tests/bundle-content.test.ts` scans for `serverCertificateHashes` + `__DEV_TRUST_FINGERPRINT__` (R-14 dev-trust tree-shake proof); `packages/web-app/tests/bundle-content.test.ts` scans for those two PLUS `__darktower_test__` + `__E2E_HOOKS__` (R-29 E2E-bus DCE proof). The forbidden-token SETS differ per package (correct — each asserts its own dead-code surfaces), but the walk+build+scan HARNESS is the same. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication (the sdk-core copy is inline in its own private test file — there is no exported shared helper that was reimplemented) — per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer, and this is only the 2nd occurrence (extract-on-third-use not yet tripped). Fix when a 3rd package needs the same contract: a small node test helper (candidate home `packages/test-utils/` node sub-path, e.g. `assertDistExcludesTokens(pkgRoot, forbiddenTokens)` that owns the `vite build` + recursive walk + scan) consumed by all three, each passing only its own token list. **Constraint**: `sdk-core`'s copy is path-pinned by the dt-guard `crates/dt-guard/src/ts_dev_trust.rs` (state 2→3 expects that exact test path) — any extraction must preserve sdk-core's test file path or update that guard in lockstep. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: cross-package (touches sdk-core, outside task #15's changeset) + needs a test-utils node-helper home decision + the dt-guard path pin; the next SDK package (or task) adding a prod-bundle-exclusion assertion should fold all three onto the shared helper. Owner: client (+ test for the test-utils home; + dry-reviewer to re-verify path pin).

- [ ] **`MockMeetingSession` test double repeated in `sdk-svelte` + `web-app` component tests** (surfaced 2026-07-05 by dry-reviewer Gate-3 review of the task #15 devloop; jointly adjudicated with @test as `packages/test-utils/` owner — decision: keep-local now, extraction candidate). `packages/sdk-svelte/src/__tests__/helpers/MockMeetingSession.ts` and `packages/web-app/src/__tests__/helpers/MockMeetingSession.ts` each subclass sdk-core's exported `TypedEventEmitter<MeetingSessionEventMap>` and add a public `fire()` over the protected `emit`, so **no dispatch/emitter logic is reimplemented** (the whole point of exporting `TypedEventEmitter`). The genuinely shared kernel is ~5 lines (`extends TypedEventEmitter` + `state = Idle` + `fire`); the two doubles otherwise DIVERGE — web-app's carries a `join()`/`disconnect()` + `joinCalls`/`disconnectCalls` spy surface the join view exercises, while sdk-svelte's is a pure `BoundMeetingSession` (state + on only). Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer, and it is currently only the 2nd occurrence (under the rule-of-three bar). **Why keep-local now** (@test, R-39 owner): promoting today would force a union "god-double" carrying join/disconnect spies that sdk-svelte's lightweight pure-bind test never uses, and would invert the "adapter tests own their fixtures" locality for a 5-line kernel. **Extraction design when a 3rd consumer arrives** (@test hint): promote a MINIMAL base `MockMeetingSession` (emitter + `state` + `fire`) to `packages/test-utils/` that each consumer EXTENDS for its own spy surface — not a single double carrying the union. **Defer trigger**: third consumer (likely a future sdk-core `session` test or an E2E-support helper). Owner: test (test-utils home) + client (consumers).
- [ ] **Container-build-hygiene bash idioms scattered across `setup.sh` + `devloop.sh`** (surfaced 2026-06-30 by dry-reviewer Gate 1/3 review of the envtest-infra-reliability devloop; pre-existing — that devloop adds one more copy of the runtime-selector idiom via the new `check_build_disk_space`, but correctly consumes `build_image`'s `CONTAINER_CMD` by arg-pass rather than re-deriving, so it does NOT widen the duplication). Two repeated idioms with no shared SoT: (a) the **`KIND_EXPERIMENTAL_PROVIDER → podman|docker` runtime selector** appears in `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:281-285` (`build_image`) and `:301-304`, with a Rust analogue `detect_container_runtime()` (`crates/devloop-helper/src/commands.rs:267`) — and the two bash copies carry *inverted unset-defaults* (build_image → `docker`, the other → also docker; the originally-planned guard would have defaulted `podman`, the divergence the Gate-1 review caught). (b) the **`OLD_IMAGE_ID`/`rmi` superseded-image cleanup** is a 5-line block triplicated: `setup.sh:286-290` (`build_image`, service images) + `infra/devloop/devloop.sh:59-62` and `:419-422` (two literal copies for the `darktower-dev:latest` dev-container image). Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication (no shared helper reimplemented; bash files, no `crates/common` home) — per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. Fix: a small sourced `infra/lib/container-cmd.sh` (or similar) exposing `resolve_container_cmd()` and `rebuild_image_replacing_old <tag> <ctx> [dockerfile]`, consumed by both scripts — collapsing the selector to one default-of-record and the rmi block to one definition. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: cross-file (setup.sh + devloop.sh, two scripts owned together but exercised on different paths) and needs a new shared-bash-lib location decision; the next devloop touching the runtime-selection or image-cleanup idiom in either script should fold it in. Owner: infrastructure (+ operations for devloop.sh).

- [ ] **Dependency version-spec drift across Cargo.toml files (not inherited via `[workspace.dependencies]`)** (surfaced 2026-06-07 by dry-reviewer Gate 3 review of task #48 audit-cleanup; pre-existing — task #48 touches no Cargo.toml). Two version-spec single-sourcing gaps: (a) **`reqwest`** is pinned verbatim — `reqwest = { version = "0.12", default-features = false, features = ["json", "rustls-tls"] }` — in 6 crates (`crates/{ac-service,ac-test-utils,gc-test-utils,gc-service,env-tests,common}/Cargo.toml`) and is absent from root `Cargo.toml` `[workspace.dependencies]`, so a version/feature bump must edit 6 copies in lockstep (the exact failure mode #48's vitest single-pin avoided). (b) **`uuid`** drifts: `[workspace.dependencies]` pins `1.10` but `crates/env-tests/Cargo.toml:33` independently pins `1.11`. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication (no shared helper reimplemented) — per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. Fix: promote `reqwest` to `[workspace.dependencies]` and switch the 6 crates to `reqwest = { workspace = true }`; reconcile `uuid` env-tests pin to inherit `workspace = true` (or bump the workspace pin to 1.11 if the newer minor is needed). **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: out of #48's scope (advisory cleanup, zero Cargo.toml edits); the next reqwest- or uuid-touching Cargo.toml change should fold this in. Owner: infrastructure.

Therefore adding `accessToken` now would be a guard edit with no triggering surface (and CATEGORY_A changes need their own security-sign-off cohort). - [ ] **env-tests `gc_client.rs::MeetingResponse` field-set divergence from server (R-53 follow-up, 2026-05-23)**: `crates/env-tests/src/fixtures/gc_client.rs::MeetingResponse` has an `org_id` field that `crates/gc-service/src/models/mod.rs::MeetingResponse` does NOT have, and the GC version has `updated_at` that the fixture does NOT have. Pre-existing divergence — present on `HEAD~1` before this devloop. The R-53 camelCase migration neither caused nor fixed it; it just re-spelled both field names to camelCase consistently. Fix is one of: (a) bring fixture in sync with server (drop `org_id`, add `updated_at`), (b) bring server in sync with fixture (add `org_id`, drop `updated_at` — unlikely; `org_id` is presumably gone for a reason), or (c) explicitly document the fixture as a permissive superset for compatibility with older payloads. Surfaced 2026-05-23 by paired-global-controller Gate 2 review of devloop `2026-05-23-camelcase-wire-migration`.
- [ ] **Shared register/login `json!` body builder for AC integration tests (R-53 follow-up, 2026-06-03)**: `crates/ac-service/tests/integration/user_auth_tests.rs` hand-rolls 27 email/password POST-body literals: **14 register** bodies (`json!({"email": ..., "password": ..., "displayName": ...})` — 11 registration tests, with `test_register_duplicate_email` and `test_register_same_email_different_orgs` each posting two) plus **13 login** bodies (`json!({"email": ..., "password": ...})` to `/api/v1/auth/user/token`). The shared substructure is the `{email, password}` core; register layers `displayName` on top. A single builder owning that core (e.g. `login_body(email, password)` + `register_body(email, password, display_name)` that delegates to it, or one builder with an optional display-name) covers all 27 sites — scoping the helper register-only would leave the 13 login bodies hand-rolled and exposed to the same copy-paste class. The register half is the exact pattern that let the R-53 camelCase migration regress silently — `display_name` → `displayName` had to be flipped in every register literal in lockstep (task #46 did precisely this). The login half did NOT regress under R-53 (login `UserTokenRequest` is camelCase-derived but only `email`/`password`, which are wire-identical, so login tests were unaffected) — it is folded in for completeness of the email/password core and future-proofing, not because login has the same demonstrated regression history. Pre-existing pattern across both halves; task #46 only re-spelled the key inside the existing register literals, did not introduce the duplication. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — no shared/`common` helper was reimplemented, so per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked rather than fix-or-defer. Likely target: test-local body builders in `user_auth_tests.rs` or `crates/ac-service/tests/common/`. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: extracting now would rewrite all 27 register+login bodies — including the many the task #46 regression fix would not otherwise touch — co-mingling a broad cleanup with the stale-wire-key fix and ballooning the diff's blast radius beyond the regression. Each literal also currently doubles as the test's at-a-glance input spec (empty `displayName`, invalid email, too-short password, wrong password); a builder with per-field overrides partially obscures that, so the extraction wants its own small cleanup devloop where the readability trade-off can be designed deliberately. NOTE: the golden-lock tests added in task #46 (`auth_handler.rs` struct lock + `user_auth_tests.rs` HTTP lock) deliberately do NOT share a body/golden helper — that is intentional dual-scope duplication (the duplication is the cross-scope protection), out of scope for this extraction. Surfaced 2026-06-03 by dry-reviewer Gate 1 review during task #46 work.
- [ ] **Shared wire-key-set test helpers (`wire_key_set` / `assert_no_snake_keys` / AC `wire_keys`) reimplemented inline across AC + GC (R-53 wire-lock follow-up, 2026-06-08)**: The wire-shape lock tests added by task #46 (AC) and task #49 (GC) all need the same two trivial serde_json idioms — (a) collect the top-level JSON object key-set into a `BTreeSet<String>`, and (b) assert no serialized key contains `_` (all-camelCase / no-surviving-snake check). This logic is now reimplemented in at least seven spots across two crates: AC `crates/ac-service/src/handlers/auth_handler.rs::tests::wire_keys` (named key-set helper) PLUS `assert_no_snake_keys` (named snake-loop helper, added task #51 — verbatim mirror of the GC one, its own comment cites "for AC/GC lock consistency"), AC `crates/ac-service/src/models/mod.rs::tests` (inline key-set + inline snake-loop in `test_service_token_response_wire_shape`, the snake-loop added task #51), GC `crates/gc-service/src/models/mod.rs::tests::{wire_key_set, assert_no_snake_keys}` (named helpers, both idioms — task #49), GC `crates/gc-service/src/handlers/me.rs::tests::test_me_response_wire_shape_stays_camel` (inline key-set + inline snake-loop — task #49), and GC `crates/gc-service/tests/meeting_create_tests.rs::test_create_meeting_wire_shape_golden_lock` (inline key-set + inline snake-loop at HTTP-integration scope — task #49). The AC↔GC `assert_no_snake_keys` is now a verbatim 2-crate copy (auth_handler.rs:665 ↔ gc-service/models/mod.rs:987). Within GC's `models/mod.rs` the two helpers ARE shared across all 8 struct locks (good); within AC, task #51 made `assert_no_snake_keys` shared across the AC user-flow locks (good intra-crate reuse); the residual duplication is (i) GC `me.rs` and the GC HTTP golden-lock not reusing them because they live in different modules / a different compilation unit (the `tests/` integration crate cannot see `src` test-private fns), and (ii) AC and GC each carrying their own copy of BOTH idioms. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — no shared/`common` helper was reimplemented (none exists yet); per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. Each idiom is ~6 lines of pure serde_json with no domain logic, so the consolidation only pays off once it's a single cross-service SoT both crates import. Likely target: `crates/common/src/observability/testing.rs` (already the home of cross-service `MetricAssertion`) gaining `pub fn wire_key_set(&Value) -> BTreeSet<String>` + `pub fn assert_no_snake_keys(&Value, ctx: &str)`, consumed by AC + GC `src` (lib) tests and `tests/` integration tests alike. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: needs cross-crate coordination (a `common` public-API addition consumed by both AC and GC, touching files outside task #49's GC-only changeset) and a deliberate decision on the helper's home + signature; folding it into task #49 would balloon the diff into AC + common and co-mingle a cross-service refactor with the GC stale-wire-key regression fix. The dual-SCOPE lock duplication itself (struct-level lock vs HTTP-integration golden lock asserting the same key-set) is NOT part of this — that cross-scope redundancy is intentional protection per the 2026-06-03 entry above and must stay. Next AC- or GC-side wire-lock-touching change, or a third service growing wire-shape locks, is the natural moment to land the shared helper. Owner: dry-reviewer to propose; global-controller + auth-controller co-consumers; the `common` addition is GSA-adjacent only as a test util (not a wire/crypto primitive). Surfaced 2026-06-08 by dry-reviewer Gate 2 review of devloop `2026-06-08-gc-integration-tests-camelcase-task-49`. **Deepened 2026-06-20 by task #51** (`2026-06-19-reverse-oauth-snake-carveout-task51`): the AC user-flow flip added a verbatim `assert_no_snake_keys` copy to `auth_handler.rs` (mirroring GC) + an inline snake-loop to `models/mod.rs`, bringing the AC-side idiom set to full parity with GC's — so AC and GC now each carry their own complete copy of BOTH helpers. Per ADR-0019 this stayed TODO-tracked (not folded into task #51) because the consolidation target is a cross-crate `common` public-API addition, out of scope for a single-crate AC wire flip; co-mingling it would have ballooned the GSA-co-signed cluster diff into `common` test-util surface. The 2-crate verbatim `assert_no_snake_keys` copy is now the strongest signal that the next wire-lock-touching change should land the `common` SoT.
- [ ] **Per-service R-55 OTel `main.rs` init + Config four-knob scaffold now has 4 identical siblings (AC #25, GC #26, MH #27, MC #6) — hoist trigger firmly met (2026-07-03; N=4 confirmed 2026-07-06)**: AC (`crates/ac-service/src/config.rs` + `main.rs`, task #25) and GC (`crates/gc-service/src/config.rs` + `main.rs`, task #26) each independently carry the byte-for-byte-identical R-55 scaffold: (a) the four `Config` fields `otel_enabled: bool` (env `OTEL_ENABLED`, default `false`, explicit-boolean gating)/`otel_endpoint: String` (env `OTLP_ENDPOINT`, default `""`)/`otel_sample_rate: f64` (env `OTEL_SAMPLE_RATE`, default `1.0`, PARSE-ONLY — range owned solely by `init_otel`)/`environment: String` (env `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT`, default `"development"`); (b) the `otel_config(&self) -> Option<common::observability::otel::OtelConfig>` gating method (`Some` iff `otel_enabled`); (c) the fail-fast `otel_enabled && otel_endpoint.trim().is_empty()` validation; (d) the identical `main.rs` ordering — `Config::from_env()` before the tracing-subscriber init, `init_otel` gated on `config.otel_config()`, named `_otel_guard` held to end of `main`, `.with(otel_layer)` composed FIRST onto the bare `registry()`. AC's task #25 DRY breadcrumb had explicitly predicted "NOT #26 GC" for this hoist trigger, but that reasoning was about GC's *different*, pre-existing `otel_collector_endpoint` field (task #10, the `:4318` browser-telemetry-proxy bare base) — it did not anticipate GC's task #26 *also* adding this separate four-knob R-55 shape on top. With AC + GC both landed, this is genuinely the 2nd sibling, not speculative. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication (each is a fresh implementation mirroring an established pattern by design, not a reimplementation of shared code) — per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. Likely target: a `common` helper (e.g. `common::observability::otel::OtelServiceConfig` parse-from-vars helper returning the 4 fields + `otel_config()` equivalent, consumed by each service's `Config::from_env()`) plus a documented `main.rs` init-ordering snippet/example (the ordering itself can't be a function — `tracing_subscriber::registry().init()` is a one-shot macro-like builder chain each service must write inline — but the `Config`-side four-field parse block CAN be hoisted). **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: cross-service (touches AC + GC to retrofit, plus MC #6 and MH #27 are about to add 2 more copies) and needs a deliberate signature/home decision beyond either single-service devloop's scope; the next of MC #6 or MH #27 (whichever lands first) is the natural moment — by then there will be 3-4 near-identical copies, past due to hoist. Owner: observability (concept) + the specialist landing MC #6 or MH #27 (retrofit). **Update 2026-07-06 (task #6 Gate 2)**: MH #27 and MC #6 have now BOTH landed the same scaffold → N=4 (AC/GC/MH/MC), firmly past the hoist trigger. All four are strict-identical again — note MC #6's initial copy had DRIFTED (case-sensitive `OTEL_ENABLED` + silent non-numeric `OTEL_SAMPLE_RATE` fallback, behind a comment falsely claiming "Mirrors AC/GC/MH exactly"); caught at Gate 2 by DRY + Code-Quality review and fixed in-PR (case-insensitive-trim + reject-invalid via `InvalidOtelConfig`), restoring the byte-identical premise this hoist depends on. The hoistable part is the `Config`-side four-field parse block (a `common::observability::otel` parse-from-vars helper) — NOT the `main.rs` one-shot subscriber chain — now ripe against 4 concrete identical shapes; retrofit is its own devloop (observability home + scheduling specialist). Surfaced 2026-07-03 by dry-reviewer Gate 1 review of task #26 (GC OTel wiring); flagged pre-plan, confirmed against the landed diff at Gate 3.

- [ ] **AC `test_issue_user_token_timing_attack_prevention` is wall-clock flaky under parallel `#[sqlx::test]` load (surfaced 2026-06-03)**: `crates/ac-service/src/services/token_service.rs:~2997` asserts the timing difference between two auth paths stays under a relative threshold to prove constant-time behavior. It passes deterministically in isolation (and single-threaded) but fails non-deterministically when run inside the full parallel `cargo test -p ac-service` suite, where CPU/DB contention from many concurrent `#[sqlx::test]` cases inflates wall-clock variance (observed e.g. "512ms (57.0% of 898ms)"). Pre-existing — reproduced on the pristine baseline via `git stash` during task #46; NOT introduced by the camelCase fix (task #46 touched only test wire keys + added serde lock tests, no timing-path code). Same root-cause family as the broader sqlx parallel DB-contention flakiness (`testing/mod.rs:226`) flagged by test on `test_register_same_email_different_orgs` / `test_register_user_without_ip_address`. Likely fix: make the timing assertion robust to host load (e.g. compare medians over N iterations with outlier trimming, or gate the strict threshold behind a serial/`--test-threads=1` or dedicated-runner marker) rather than a single-shot relative-percentage check. Mitigation today: run AC tests per-binary single-threaded. Surfaced 2026-06-03 during task #46 work (out of scope for that fix's verdict; tracked here for a CI-flakiness paper trail).
- [ ] **Shared reqwest client-builder for GC telemetry forwarder vs AC client (task #10 follow-up, 2026-06-21)**: `crates/gc-service/src/services/telemetry_forwarder.rs` and `crates/gc-service/src/services/ac_client.rs` share an ~8-line `Client::builder().timeout(...).connect_timeout(...).build().map_err(GcError::Internal)` block. The bodies otherwise diverge: `ac_client` injects an OAuth bearer + parses a JSON `TokenResponse` in `handle_response`; the telemetry forwarder forwards raw OTLP bytes with no auth and only inspects status. Extraction opportunity (Pattern-B), NOT true duplication — per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: extracting now would couple two clients with different auth + response semantics for an 8-line builder; the next GC service growing a third reqwest client (or a feature/version bump to the builder) is the moment to land a shared `gc-service/src/services/http_client.rs` builder helper. Surfaced 2026-06-21 by @dry-reviewer plan review of task #10 (GC telemetry proxy). Owner: global-controller.
- [ ] **Shared `AppState` builder for GC in-crate integration tests — `TelemetryState::from_config` setup repeated across 5 test files (task #10 follow-up, 2026-06-21; 5th sibling added by task #5 CORS, 2026-07-07)**: Adding the required `telemetry` field to GC's `AppState` forced the same ~6-line `TelemetryState::from_config(config.otel_collector_endpoint.clone(), config.telemetry_proxy_max_bytes, config.telemetry_proxy_rate_limit_per_minute).map_err(...)?` + `AppState { …, telemetry }` block into every test that hand-rolls an `AppState` directly instead of going through the shared `TestGcServer` harness: `crates/gc-service/tests/meeting_tests.rs:142` and `:237`, `crates/gc-service/tests/meeting_create_tests.rs:113`, `crates/gc-service/tests/auth_tests.rs:97` (4 verbatim copies), plus `crates/gc-service/tests/cors_integration.rs:42` (`build_test_router`, added by task #5) — the full `Config::from_vars` + `TelemetryState::from_config` + `TokenReceiver` + `AppState{}` + `routes::build_routes` assembly (5th copy). The shared harness `crates/gc-test-utils/src/server_harness.rs:106` already absorbed the block for its own consumers — the residual duplication is only in the in-crate tests that construct `AppState` themselves rather than via `TestGcServer`. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — no shared/`common` helper was reimplemented (this is new same-shape test setup forced by a struct field addition); per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. Likely target: a small `gc-test-utils` `AppState`-builder (e.g. `build_test_app_state(pool, config, mc_client, token_receiver) -> Arc<AppState>` that owns the `TelemetryState::from_config` call), consumed by the 5 in-crate files; or migrate those tests onto `TestGcServer` where feasible. **Note the CORS sibling needs two extra harness capabilities before it can migrate**: (a) env-var injection — `TestGcServer::spawn` hard-codes its var map with no `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, so a `spawn_with_vars(vars)`/`assemble_state(vars, pool)` seam is needed to drive the allowed-origin case; and (b) a DB-free `oneshot`/lazy-pool drive mode — `cors_integration.rs` deliberately avoids `TestGcServer`'s live `tokio::spawn`ed server because the per-thread `MetricAssertion` recorder can't observe emissions routed through a spawned server thread (same per-thread-recorder trap that disqualified the harness for these tests at task #5 Gate 1). A clean consolidation extracts the shared state-assembly core so both `spawn` and a DB-free oneshot path reuse it. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: routed to the DRY lane by @code-reviewer during task #10 review as a cross-test-file cleanup; folding it in would touch 5 pre-existing test files beyond any one changeset and wants a deliberate decision on the builder signature/home (the files differ in their mock-MC, token-receiver, and now CORS-var setup, so the shared part is only the telemetry+AppState assembly). Next GC test-harness-touching change is the natural moment to land the builder. Surfaced 2026-06-21 by @dry-reviewer Gate-2 delta re-review of task #10; 5th sibling + harness-capability notes added 2026-07-07 by @dry-reviewer Gate-3 review of task #5 (CORS). - [ ] **Framed-protobuf send-path + read-loop over a WebTransport bidi stream — extract on second use for MH (task #13 → task #14, 2026-06-25)**: `SignalingClient` (`packages/sdk-core/src/signaling/SignalingClient.ts`) introduces the canonical "frame + encode + trace-inject + write" send path (`#sendClientMessage`, `:300` — `injectIntoClientMessage` → `toBinary(ClientMessageSchema)` → `encodeFrame` → `writer.write`) and the matching "read chunk → `FrameDecoder.push` → `fromBinary(ServerMessageSchema)` → dispatch" bidi read loop (`#runReadLoop`, `:321`). Task #14 (MediaTransport / `MhClientMessage` → MH) needs the byte-identical shape against the MH envelope schemas. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — there is only ONE consumer today (SignalingClient → MC); the four reused primitives (`framing/length-prefix.ts`, `telemetry/tracePropagation.ts::injectIntoClientMessage`, `@bufbuild/protobuf` codec) are themselves SoT and are consumed as-is, nothing reimplemented. Per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. The two methods were deliberately kept **cohesive and liftable** for exactly this: their only MC-specific couplings are the schema constants (`ClientMessageSchema` / `ServerMessageSchema`) and the `#dispatch` / `#terminate` handoffs — SignalingClient-specific concerns (event names, proto→`SignalingError` mapping, the `dt_client.join` span lifecycle) live OUT of them in `#dispatch` / `#handleErrorMessage` / `#raise` / `#settle*`, so the lift is a parametrize-over-schema + inject-a-handler refactor, not a rewrite. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: pre-generalizing into a shared `framedChannel` module (e.g. `sendFramedMessage(writer, schema, message)` + `pumpFramedMessages(readable, schema, onMessage, onFramingError)` under `packages/sdk-core/src/signaling/` or `src/transport/`) before #14's MH schema exists is speculative generality — the schema-generic signature, the framing-error surfacing seam (callback vs. event), and the span-ownership boundary can only be designed correctly against the second consumer's real shape (the implementer floated such a module at Gate 1 and we agreed to defer it). Task #14 is the second consumer and the natural moment to lift `#sendClientMessage` / `#runReadLoop` into that shared helper, consumed by both `SignalingClient` and `MediaTransport`. Owner: client. Surfaced 2026-06-25 by @dry-reviewer Gate-2 review of devloop `2026-06-25-sdk-core-signaling-client`; pointer: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-06-25-sdk-core-signaling-client/main.md`.
- [ ] **Per-service R-55 OTel `main.rs` wiring + config-parse scaffold (AC #25 establishes; MC #6 / MH #27 will mirror) (2026-06-25)**: Task #25 is the FIRST per-service `init_otel` wiring. AC is a clean thin caller of the common helper (`crates/common/src/observability/otel.rs::init_otel`) — no reimplementation of the propagator/sampler/resource-attrs/guard — but it introduces a per-service scaffold that the next two backend-span-export services will copy near-verbatim: (a) the three-knob config parse in `crates/ac-service/src/config.rs::from_vars` (`OTEL_ENABLED` bool + `OTLP_ENDPOINT` String + `OTEL_SAMPLE_RATE` f64-parse-only + `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT`, plus the `otel_enabled && endpoint.is_empty()` cross-field gate), (b) the `Config::otel_config() -> Option<common::…::OtelConfig>` gating method, and (c) the `main.rs` glue (match on `otel_config()` → `init_otel(...).await?`, split into `(Some(layer), Some(guard))`, compose `otel_layer` first onto the bare `registry()`, hold the named `_otel_guard` to end of `main`). Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer, and it CANNOT be extracted at N=1 (you'd be designing the shared helper against one imagined shape). **Scope note**: GC #26 is NOT a clean sibling — GC's OTel config is the `otel_collector_endpoint` 4318/HTTP browser-telemetry-proxy shape (different knobs, different port/purpose), so the three-knob scaffold's true siblings are MC #6 and MH #27 only. **Already resolved and explicitly OUT of this extraction debt**: the `OTEL_SAMPLE_RATE` `[0.0,1.0]` bound is single-owned by `init_otel` (otel.rs:212) — config-side is parse-only per the team-lead's 2026-06-25 freeze (option X); do NOT let a future hoist re-introduce a per-service range check. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: the moment the SECOND three-knob sibling lands (#6 MC or #27 MH) and the real duplication surface is concrete, the #6/#27 reviewer should hoist a shared `common::observability::otel` config-parse + wiring helper (e.g. a `parse_otel_env(&vars) -> OtelEnvConfig` + a `compose_otel(registry, OtelInit) ` or builder seam) consumed by AC/MC/MH; folding it now would be premature against a single instance. Owner: dry-reviewer to propose at #6/#27; auth-controller + meeting-controller + media-handler co-consumers; observability owns the common helper home. Surfaced 2026-06-25 by @dry-reviewer Gate-2 review of task #25 (AC OTel wiring). **Update 2026-07-03 (task #27 Gate 1)**: confirmed MC #6 has NOT landed (`crates/mc-service/src/config.rs` has no `OTEL_*` reader, `main.rs` has no `init_otel` call) — MH #27 is the SECOND three-knob sibling per the defer trigger above, so the hoist is now ripe on the real-shape evidence (2 instances: AC #25 + MH #27). Not folded into task #27 itself — the implementer is a thin consumer mirroring AC's shape, and designing `parse_otel_env`/`compose_otel` under task #27's own review clock would broaden its diff into `crates/common` + a re-touch of AC, which the DRY Exception's "requires cross-service coordination" deferral pattern still covers. Next candidate: a small follow-up devloop (owner: observability, common home) once MC #6 also lands, so the extraction is designed against all three real shapes rather than two. Surfaced by @dry-reviewer Gate 1 review of task #27 (MH OTel wiring).
- [ ] **Inline `HashMap<String, String>`-as-`Extractor` idiom for envelope trace fields — MH lands first, MC's R-57 (task #6) will need the sibling (2026-07-03, task #27; corrected 2026-07-04 at Gate 2)**: Task #27 (MH) wires `webtransport/connection.rs::handle_connection()` to extract inbound W3C trace context from `MhClientMessage::{trace_parent, trace_state}` (proto tags 20/21) and attach it as the span parent. **Correction from the original Gate-1 note**: the landed code does NOT add a bespoke `Extractor` impl — it builds a `HashMap<String, String>` carrier (skipping proto3-default-empty fields) and calls the global propagator's `.extract(&trace_carrier)`, reusing `opentelemetry`'s existing blanket `Extractor` impl for `HashMap<String, String>` (the same technique `otel_grpc.rs`'s own unit tests already use). So there is no new `Extractor` TYPE to hoist — the residual duplication is just the ~9-line "build a `HashMap` from two proto string fields, call the global propagator, `set_parent`" idiom itself. `ClientMessage` (MC-bound) carries the identical `trace_parent`/`trace_state` field pair (`proto/dark_tower/signaling/v1/signaling.proto:354-364`) and MC's R-57 task (#6, not yet landed — `crates/mc-service/src/webtransport/connection.rs` only zero-inits these fields in test fixtures today) will need the byte-identical idiom. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — MH #27 consumes `common::observability::otel_grpc`'s propagator/bounds machinery as-is and does not reimplement it; the idiom has exactly ONE consumer today and is arguably too small (9 lines, no branching beyond two `is_empty()` guards) to need its own named helper vs. just copy-pasting the idiom a second time. Per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. Likely target, if it proves worth hoisting once MC's copy exists: a tiny `common::observability::otel_grpc::extract_context_from_trace_fields(trace_parent: &str, trace_state: &str) -> opentelemetry::Context` wrapping the carrier-build + global-propagator-extract, consumed by both MH's and MC's connection handlers (leaving the `set_parent` call, which is span-plumbing, at each call site). **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: MC's R-57 (task #6) is the natural second consumer — worth confirming at that point whether 2 call sites justify a named helper over the copy-paste, given how small the idiom is. Owner: dry-reviewer to propose at MC task #6; media-handler + meeting-controller co-consumers; observability owns the `common` helper home if hoisted. **Update 2026-07-06 (task #6 Gate 2)**: MC #6 landed the 2nd consumer — but instead of copy-pasting the inline idiom, it EXTRACTED the helpers into a named local module `crates/mc-service/src/webtransport/trace.rs` (`extract_parent`/`reparent_current_span`/`inject_current_context`, all over the GLOBAL propagator + the two `PROPAGATED_HEADERS` keys), consumed at every MC WT call site. It ALSO added the OUTBOUND inject half MH never had (populates `ServerMessage.{trace_parent,trace_state}` via `inject_context` into a `HashMap` injector — NO hand-rolled W3C string, verified at Gate 2). So the idiom is now N=2 for extract (MH inline + MC `trace.rs`) and N=1 for inject (MC only); the hoist target is UPGRADED from the old extract-only `extract_context_from_trace_fields` to a SYMMETRIC inject+extract pair over the two-field carrier, mirroring the already-shipped `common::observability::otel_http::{inject,extract}_trace_context` (the `http::HeaderMap` sibling of exactly this pattern). Kept local this PR per the Gate-1 DRY adjudication (a `common` hoist adds a cross-crate boundary beyond a wiring task); revisit when a 3rd consumer or an MH retrofit schedules the symmetric helper. (Superseded item (b) of the task-#6 consolidated breadcrumb below — canonical here.) Surfaced 2026-07-03 by @dry-reviewer Gate 1 review of task #27 (MH OTel wiring); corrected 2026-07-04 by @dry-reviewer Gate 2 review against the landed diff.
- [ ] **`SpanLayer` — tower ambient-span workaround for tonic `Interceptor`/span-timing, MH-only today (2026-07-04, task #27 Gate 2)**: Task #27 adds `crates/mh-service/src/grpc/span_layer.rs::SpanLayer` (a bare `tower::Layer`/`Service` pair) because `MediaHandlerServiceServer::with_interceptor(server_interceptor())` alone does NOT reparent the handler span: tonic's `Interceptor::call()` runs synchronously/eagerly inside `Service::call()`, before the handler's own `#[instrument]` span is created (that happens only when the response future is later polled), so `Span::current()` is `Span::none()` at extraction time and `set_parent()` silently no-ops. `SpanLayer` creates+enters a bare per-request span before the interceptor runs and `.instrument()`s the response future so the reparented span stays active through the handler's span creation. Verified NOT a duplicate of anything existing: grepped `crates/common` + AC/GC/MC for `tower::Layer`/`Instrument`/an ambient-span pattern — GC's `grpc/auth_layer.rs` and MC's `grpc/auth_interceptor.rs` are tower Layers too but solve JWT auth (already tracked as its own near-identical-layer extraction candidate under "gRPC Auth (Cross-Service)" in the dry-reviewer INDEX), not span timing; GC's inbound gRPC server (`main.rs`) does not use `otel_grpc::server_interceptor()` at all yet (no R-56 inbound wiring on GC), so GC hasn't hit this bug. Extraction opportunity (Pattern-A future-SPOT), NOT true duplication — self-contained, ~90 LoC, no `crates/common` changes. **The underlying bug is NOT MH-specific**: any service accepting inbound gRPC AND wiring `otel_grpc::server_interceptor()` behind a `tonic::transport::Server` will hit the identical eager-interceptor-vs-late-span timing gap. MC's own inbound gRPC servers (GC→MC `McAssignmentService`, MH→MC `McMediaCoordinationService`) will need the same fix whenever MC wires its own R-56 inbound extraction (no task number yet — MC's OTel wiring, task #6, is main.rs/config.rs scaffold only per the entry above; inbound interceptor wiring on MC's *own* gRPC server is a separate, not-yet-scheduled step). Likely target once a second consumer exists: hoist `SpanLayer` verbatim into `common::observability::otel_grpc` (it takes no MH-specific state — no config, no dependencies beyond `tower`/`tracing`) so both MH and MC's inbound servers `.layer(SpanLayer)` from one definition, and so the bug fix + its regression test live in the same module as `server_interceptor()` itself (arguably its correct home even at N=1, but hoisting speculatively before a second consumer's `Server::builder()` call site exists risks guessing at the wrong seam, e.g. if MC's server needs the layer to also carry auth-adjacent state). **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: N=1 — MC has no inbound `server_interceptor()` wiring yet to confirm the hoisted shape is actually shared as-is. Owner: dry-reviewer to propose when MC wires inbound R-56 extraction on its own gRPC server; media-handler + meeting-controller co-consumers; observability owns the `common` module if hoisted. **Update 2026-07-06 (task #6 Gate 2) — MC did NOT become the 2nd consumer; `SpanLayer` stays N=1 MH-only, DOWNGRADE to informational**: MC #6 wired inbound `server_interceptor()` on its own gRPC servers (GC→MC `MeetingControllerService`, MH→MC `MediaCoordinationService`) and hit the exact same eager-interceptor-vs-late-span gap — but resolved it with the OFF-THE-SHELF `tower_http::trace::TraceLayer::new_for_grpc()` as the outermost layer (matching GC's `main.rs`), NOT by copying `SpanLayer`. Verified MC's `McAuthService` carries the identical `BoxBody` body-type constraint as GC's `GrpcAuthService`, so the `.layer(TraceLayer).layer(auth_layer)` order drops in cleanly (zero new dependency). Net: GC + MC both use `TraceLayer::new_for_grpc()`; MH's `SpanLayer` is the intentional odd-one-out (its module docs deliberately choose a bare attribute-less anchor span over TraceLayer's full request span — a legitimate MH-local design, not a defect). The "any inbound-`server_interceptor()` service needs `SpanLayer`" premise is therefore VOID — TraceLayer is the proven zero-new-dep fix. No longer a hoist candidate; only revive at N=2 if a future service both needs the bare-anchor semantics AND copies `SpanLayer` verbatim. Surfaced 2026-07-04 by @dry-reviewer Gate 2 review of task #27 (MH OTel wiring); flagged for review by @team-lead at Gate 2 kickoff.

- [ ] **GC Claims struct duplicates common::jwt::ServiceClaims**: `crates/gc-service/src/auth/claims.rs` defines its own `Claims` identical to `common::jwt::ServiceClaims`. Should migrate to `pub type Claims = common::jwt::ServiceClaims;` (same pattern as AC). **Blocks GrpcAuthLayer extraction** (see GrpcAuthLayer entry below) — extraction to `common::grpc` requires unifying on `ServiceClaims` first, and that migration must also refactor GC's HTTP `validate_user()` path.
- [ ] **AC private MeetingTokenClaims/GuestTokenClaims duplicate common types**: `crates/ac-service/src/handlers/internal_tokens.rs:235-264` defines private `MeetingTokenClaims`/`GuestTokenClaims` structs that are structurally near-identical to `common::jwt::MeetingTokenClaims`/`common::jwt::GuestTokenClaims`. AC should migrate to use the common types. **Deepened 2026-08-06 by devloop `2026-08-06-roster-display-names` (dry-reviewer Gate 3)**: the roster-display-name carrier fix added a `display_name` field to BOTH `MeetingTokenClaims` copies in lockstep — `common::jwt::MeetingTokenClaims` (as `#[serde(default) ] pub display_name: String`, Debug-redacted) and the AC-local serialize-only struct at `internal_tokens.rs` — so the two now share one MORE field that must be kept wire-agreeing by hand (they already agreed on the `display_name` wire key: common uses serde-default field naming, AC-local emits the same key, no `#[serde(rename)]` on either). This is exactly the drift surface this entry warns about: the deserialize side defaults-on-missing while the serialize side always emits, so a future field added to only one copy would silently diverge. Per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this stayed TODO-tracked (not fix-or-defer) — consolidating the two structs would touch the JWT signing + MC validation surface, well beyond a carrier fix. No THIRD copy was introduced by this devloop (the MC/MH/`mc-test-utils` edits are field additions to struct-literals of the *common* type, i.e. consumers, not new definitions). The consolidation target is unchanged: migrate the AC-local serialize path onto `common::jwt::MeetingTokenClaims` (its field types differ — AC-local uses `String` for `participant_type`/`role`/`home_org_id` vs common's enums/`Option`, so the migration must reconcile those), collapsing this and entry #54 (`sign_meeting_jwt`/`sign_guest_jwt`) together. - [ ] **`mock_token_receiver` test helper duplication (4 copies in MC)**: `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/gc_client.rs` (tests), `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/mh_client.rs` (tests), `crates/mc-service/tests/gc_integration.rs`, `crates/mc-service/tests/register_meeting_integration.rs` — same "transient `watch::Sender` + `TokenReceiver::from_test_channel`" shape. The integration variant uses `Box::leak` instead of `OnceLock` but the intent is identical. Candidate for extraction to `mc-test-utils`.
- [ ] **GrpcAuthLayer extraction (GC/MC/MH)**: GC, MC, and MH all now have async JWKS-based two-layer auth (`GrpcAuthLayer`/`GrpcAuthService`, `McAuthLayer`/`McAuthService`, `MhAuthLayer`/`MhAuthService`) that are structurally near-identical tower Layer/Service implementations with the same 5-step validation chain (structural fast-path, JWKS crypto, scope check, `service_type` routing, claims injection). All three include identical `classify_jwt_error()` helpers and `failure_reason` metrics. Remaining differences: `REQUIRED_SCOPE` constant value (`service.write.{gc,mc,mh}`), Layer 2 path routing table (GC maps 2 gRPC services, MC maps 2, MH maps 1), tracing targets (`{gc,mc,mh}.grpc.auth`), metric prefix (`{gc,mc,mh}_`). Extract to `common::grpc::GrpcAuthLayer` parameterized by scope, routing table, and metric prefix. **Blocker**: GC still uses local `auth::Claims` while MC/MH use `common::jwt::ServiceClaims` — see the "GC Claims struct duplicates common::jwt::ServiceClaims" entry above. Unify the claims type first, then extract. **Also carry into extraction**: the `caller_type_rejected_total{actual_type}` label takes `claims.service_type` directly from the JWT, which is technically unbounded if AC ever issues a token with a novel `service_type`. Cross-service parity (all three services behave identically) is intact today; the extraction should add a match-and-allowlist at the recording site (known values → as-is; everything else → `"unknown"`). Locations: `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/auth_layer.rs:GrpcAuthLayer`, `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/auth_interceptor.rs:McAuthLayer`, `crates/mh-service/src/grpc/auth_interceptor.rs:MhAuthLayer`.

- [x] **Dead MhAuthInterceptor (MH)**: Removed in ADR-0003 MH auth alignment (2026-04-15). `MhAuthLayer` now provides two-layer auth with service_type routing.
