- [ ] **`IWebTransport` interface canonical-home decision (test-utils ↔ sdk-core, R-13/R-39)**: `packages/test-utils/src/contracts/IWebTransport.ts` declares a minimal `IWebTransport`/`WebTransportBidirectionalStream`/`WebTransportCloseInfo` shape so `MockWebTransport` has something to implement. test-utils ships first (browser-client-join task #8); sdk-core ships in task #9 with the canonical production interface (R-13). Current strategy is **Pattern A — parallel structural declarations**: when sdk-core lands its `IWebTransport`, `MockWebTransport` will satisfy both via TypeScript structural typing. No circular dep, no dev-dep edge from sdk-core's prod build. Decision to revisit at task #9 review time: (a) keep Pattern A indefinitely (cheap while the shape is stable), or (b) flip to test-utils dev-depending on sdk-core's canonical declaration. Trigger for (b): meaningful shape evolution (e.g., adding stream-prioritization, congestion-control hints, or a third optional field) that risks the two declarations drifting silently. Surfaced during browser-client-join task #8 DRY review (2026-05-06). **Task #9 resolution (2026-06-21): kept Pattern A.** sdk-core landed the canonical `IWebTransport` at `packages/sdk-core/src/transport/IWebTransport.ts` with the identical shape; no dep edge either direction. The drift forcing-function is now live: an explicit `const transport: IWebTransport = mock;` (sdk-core interface ← `@darktower/test-utils` `MockWebTransport`) in `packages/sdk-core/src/transport/__tests__/BrowserWebTransport.test.ts` fails to compile if sdk-core's interface grows a member the mock lacks. The residual gap (test-utils copy gaining a field sdk-core lacks) stays one-directional, as noted. Revisit trigger (b) unchanged; likely consolidates with the `MetricsSink` entry below at task #12.

- [ ] **`MetricsSink` interface canonical-home decision (test-utils ↔ sdk-core, R-24/R-39)**: `packages/test-utils/src/contracts/MetricsSink.ts` declares the minimal `MetricsSink` + `MetricLabels` contract that `InMemoryMetricsSink` (R-39, this PR) implements. The interface contract is **co-owned with observability** per user-story §Design line 256. sdk-core (task #12, R-24) will ship the canonical `MetricsSink` + production impls (`OtelMetricsSink`, `ConsoleMetricsSink`, `NoopMetricsSink`). Same Pattern A reasoning as the `IWebTransport` entry above: parallel structural declarations, no circular dep. Decision to revisit at task #12 review time, **paired with the `IWebTransport` entry** — likely both interfaces consolidate together. Trigger for "extract to sdk-core, test-utils consumes": adding a fourth method (e.g., `histogramWithExplicitBuckets`), changing `MetricLabels` shape, or introducing a base-trait/factory pattern that benefits from one source of truth. Subset-filter assertion semantics (`InMemoryMetricsSink.assertCounter` etc.) intentionally mirror `crates/common/src/observability/testing.rs:681,769,861` `MetricAssertion::with_labels(...)`; cross-language symmetry is not duplication. Surfaced during browser-client-join task #8 DRY review (2026-05-06). **Task #12 resolution (2026-06-24): kept Pattern A.** sdk-core landed the canonical `MetricsSink` + `MetricLabels` at `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/MetricsSink.ts` (named convention author per ADR-0024 §6.5 Pattern B) plus the four sinks. The intended consolidation (test-utils `export type`-re-exports sdk-core) was attempted but **REVERTED**: it adds a `test-utils → @darktower/sdk-core` package edge, and since sdk-core already devDepends test-utils (test doubles), Nx rejects the resulting build-task cycle (`sdk-core:build → test-utils:build → sdk-core:build`, verified via `nx run-many -t build`). So both copies stay as parallel structural declarations, now drift-locked by a compile-time conformance test `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/__tests__/contractParity.test.ts` (asserts the two interfaces are mutually assignable — `tsc` fails on drift). Re-evaluate (b) "extract to one source" only if the Nx graph can be restructured to break the cycle (e.g. splitting test doubles off sdk-core's build inputs), or on a shape change that the parity test alone cannot hold.

- [ ] **`ts-name-guard-dt-client` should ALSO check `sink.counter('dt_client_*')` literal CALL SITES (dt-guard, task #13/#14 follow-up)**: today the guard (`crates/dt-guard/src/ts_metric_naming.rs`) only checks string literals passed to OTel `meter.createCounter/createHistogram/createGauge(...)` factory calls. Task #12 introduced the `// dt-metric-name-dynamic: <reason>` escape hatch so the canonical generic `OtelMetricsSink` — which forwards a runtime-validated VARIABLE name to `meter.createX(name)` — opts out (it validates via `assertClientMetricName(name, mode)` on the line above; the marker rescues ONLY the non-literal case, never a bad literal — enforced by `ts_metric_naming::tests::bad_literal_with_marker_present_still_hard_fails`). BUT the actual `dt_client_*` metric NAME LITERALS will live at the **emission call sites** in #13/#14 (`sink.counter('dt_client_join_attempts_total', ...)`), which the guard does NOT scan today (they are `.counter(` not `.createCounter(`). **Action (observability-policy + infrastructure-tooling, co-owned per ADR-0034)**: when #13/#14 add those call sites, extend the guard to also validate the first string-literal arg of `.counter(`/`.histogram(`/`.gauge(` on a `MetricsSink` so a bad literal at the emission site is caught statically (the runtime guard catches it too, but CI should catch typos pre-merge). The dynamic-name marker covers the generic sink internals only; it is NOT a license to skip literal-call-site checking. Surfaced 2026-06-24 at task #12 Gate-2 (Layer 3 name-guard conflict resolution).

- [ ] **Client SDK OTel bundle-size budget (sdk-core, task #12)**: task #12 added 7 `@opentelemetry/*` runtime deps to `@darktower/sdk-core` (its first runtime deps) for the client telemetry scaffolding (R-19/R-24). They are **externalized** from sdk-core's own bundle (Vite `rollupOptions.external = [/^@opentelemetry\//]`), so sdk-core's `dist/index.mjs` stays small (~21.7 KB, unchanged from pre-OTel) and emits bare `import '@opentelemetry/...'`. **The size cost lands on the bundling CONSUMER** (`@darktower/web-app`, task #15+), which will pull the OTel SDK + OTLP-proto exporter + protobuf transformer into its app bundle. No budget is enforced yet. **Action (operations, by web-app bundle-analysis at task #15+)**: measure the OTel contribution to the web-app production bundle, decide whether to (a) accept it, (b) lazy-load telemetry behind a dynamic `import()` so it is off the join critical path, or (c) trim the dep set (e.g. drop `sdk-trace-web` if the root-span helper can use `@opentelemetry/api` + `core` alone). Pinned exact versions: api 1.9.1, core/resources/sdk-metrics/sdk-trace-web 2.8.0, exporter-metrics-otlp-proto 0.219.0, semantic-conventions 1.41.1. Surfaced 2026-06-24 by @operations at task #12 Gate-1 (deliberate deferred decision).

- [ ] **Name-guard prelude shared between `ConsoleMetricsSink` + `OtelMetricsSink` (sdk-core telemetry, task #12)**: `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/ConsoleMetricsSink.ts` and `OtelMetricsSink.ts` share an identical guard prelude — the `readonly #mode: GuardMode` field, the `constructor(mode: GuardMode = 'throw')` default, and the `if (!assertClientMetricName(name, this.#mode)) return;` first line in all three of `counter`/`histogram`/`gauge` (6 guard-line sites + 2 copies of the mode plumbing across the two classes). Genuinely parallel structure, but held at extraction-candidate (NOT extracted now) because: (a) N=2 within-package classes, not cross-service — below the ADR-0019 ≥3 threshold; (b) a shared `abstract GuardedMetricsSink` base would abstract over ~2 trivial fields and a one-line guard call, borderline-not-worth-the-indirection at N=2; (c) `NoopMetricsSink` deliberately skips the guard (emits nothing) so a common base could not cover all three production sinks anyway — the guarded subset is only Console+Otel. **Extraction trigger**: a 3rd name-guarded sink lands (e.g. a batching/buffering sink, or a sampling wrapper), at which point a `GuardedMetricsSink` base (holds `#mode`, exposes a `protected guard(name): boolean`) pays for itself. Surfaced 2026-06-24 at task #12 Gate-2 @dry-reviewer review.

- [ ] **`wait_for_busy` test-helper busy-wait pattern (devloop-helper, 6 sites)**: `crates/devloop-helper/src/main.rs` `concurrency_tests` module repeats the same 8-line busy-wait at 6 call sites: `for _ in 0..100 { if h.ctx.snapshot_busy().is_some() { break; } std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20)); } assert!(h.ctx.snapshot_busy().is_some());`. Iter-1 had 1 site; iter-2 (2026-05-03 helper-concurrency, process-group cancel + `cancel_pending`) added 5 more — clears the ADR-0019 ≥3-4-site threshold for an extraction opportunity. Extraction candidate: `fn wait_for_busy(ctx: &Context)` (or method on `TestHelperWithCtx`) returning `BusyHint` so callers can inline `let bh = wait_for_busy(&h.ctx);` and drop the assert. Low priority (test-only, single crate, behavior is correct) — surface on next material edit to `concurrency_tests`. Surfaced 2026-05-03 by @dry-reviewer iter-2 verdict.
- [ ] **`trace_parent`/`trace_state` envelope-level field pair (3 envelopes in `proto/signaling.proto`)**: Browser-client-join Task #2 added flat `string trace_parent = 20; string trace_state = 21;` (W3C Trace Context, RFC 9287) at envelope level on `ClientMessage`, `ServerMessage`, and `MhClientMessage`. The user story design explicitly chose flat fields over a `TraceContext { string trace_parent = 1; string trace_state = 2; }` sub-message because (a) the wrapping cost (1-2 bytes per envelope) repeats on every signaling message, (b) generated SDKs see one extra layer of nesting per envelope access, and (c) the field tags 20/21 are fixed by spec. Worth revisiting if a third trace-context field (e.g. `baggage` per W3C Baggage spec) is ever added — at that point the cost-benefit of a sub-message flips because (i) tag-space management gets tighter and (ii) the per-field doc-comment surface grows linearly with envelopes × fields. Until then, flat is correct. Surfaced during browser-client-join Task #2 DRY review (2026-05-03).

- [ ] **Layer-runner loop duplicated between `scripts/layer-all.sh` and `scripts/verify-completion.sh` (Wave 1 #1, ADR-0033)**: `scripts/layer-all.sh:32-51` and `scripts/verify-completion.sh:102-113` both implement the per-layer-invoke + tee + `parse_status_line` loop (~12 lines each). The `--layer full` path in verify-completion already delegates to layer-all (verify-completion.sh:89-92), so duplication is bounded to the quick/standard branch. Wave-2-or-later refactor: add `--layers <list>` option to layer-all.sh and have verify-completion.sh invoke it for the quick/standard subsets too. Both files are correct as-is; this is a structural deduplication to land before a third caller emerges. Surfaced during ADR-0033 Wave 1 #1 DRY review (2026-05-08).

- [ ] **GH Actions setup steps duplicated across `ci.yml` and `ci-client.yml` (task #17, 2026-06-23)**: `ci-client.yml` (new) and `ci.yml` share 5–6 identical step blocks: `actions/checkout`, `pnpm/action-setup@v4 version: 10.33.2`, `actions/setup-node@v4 node-version: '22' cache: pnpm`, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `bufbuild/buf-setup-action@v1`, and `Swatinem/rust-cache`. Inside `ci-client.yml` alone the pnpm+Node+install block is repeated across three jobs (lint, unit, component). GitHub Actions has no native cross-workflow step sharing without composite actions. Extraction candidate: `.github/actions/setup-client/action.yml` composite action for the pnpm+Node+install trio (used 3× within `ci-client.yml`, potentially reusable by `ci.yml`); the Rust+dt-guard build block is lint-job-only so lower priority. Composite extraction also centralises the Dependabot version-bump surface (currently 3 jobs in `ci-client.yml` + `ci.yml` = 4 copies of the `pnpm/action-setup` version). **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: N=2 workflows and N=3 jobs; correct as-is; composite action adds indirection with minimal payoff at this scale. Natural trigger: a third client-tier workflow (e.g. E2E browser CI) or a fourth job in `ci-client.yml`. Owner: infrastructure. Surfaced 2026-06-23 by @dry-reviewer Gate 1 review of task #17.

- [ ] **Nx `targetDefaults.lint` command not extracted — per-package `project.json` lint duplicates (task #17, 2026-06-23)**: Task #17 adds `tsc --noEmit && eslint . && prettier --check .` to `sdk-core/project.json` and `test-utils/project.json` lint targets (N=2 copies). `nx.json::targetDefaults.lint` currently sets `inputs`+`cache` only, not a default `command`. A default `options.command` in `targetDefaults` could replace both per-package copies for TS-source packages; `proto-gen` (buf lint) would still need its own override. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: only 2 TS-source packages today; per-package entries remain readable and idiomatic; `targetDefaults` default would be invisible when reading individual configs. Natural trigger: a third TS package joins the workspace. Owner: infrastructure. Surfaced 2026-06-23 by @dry-reviewer Gate 1 review of task #17.

- [ ] **`lang/<X>/changed.test.sh` locality-self-test harness duplicated (Wave 1 #2, ADR-0033)**: `scripts/lang/rust/changed.test.sh` and `scripts/lang/ts/changed.test.sh` (added Wave 1 #2) share a verbatim ~30-line harness body — `assert_rc()`, `run_with_cache()` (mktemp + `env -i` cache injection + `printf` rc), `PASS`/`FAIL`/`FAILURES` accounting, and the trailing per-failure dump + summary printer. Only the per-language predicate-cases differ (the lines calling `assert_rc` with paths). Same N-instance parallel-anchor pattern as the `lang/<X>/audit.sh` security-comment block (ADR-0033 §11 convention). Likely target: `scripts/lang/_changed_test_harness.sh` exposing `assert_rc` + `run_with_cache`, plus a tiny driver that takes the language name for the summary banner. Defer until Wave 2 #5 (TS test wrappers) or a third lang's `changed.test.sh` lands — current 2-instance shape is below the threshold where extraction reduces complexity, and the parallel anchor is already easy to read. Surfaced during ADR-0033 Wave 1 #2 DRY review (2026-05-08).

- [ ] **`scripts/lang/ts/{compile,fmt,lint,test}.sh` shared prelude — `_nx_affected.sh` extraction (Wave 2 #5, ADR-0033)**: The four TS runtime wrappers (`scripts/lang/ts/compile.sh`, `fmt.sh`, `lint.sh`, `test.sh` — added Wave 2 #5 / task #36) share an identical ~7-line prelude: shebang + one-line verb comment + `set -euo pipefail` + `IFS=$'\n\t'` + `__here` resolution + `source _common.sh` + `BASE_SHA="$(...)"` resolution, before the single line `run_and_emit "nx-<target>" pnpm exec nx affected -t <target> --base="$BASE_SHA" "$@"`. Total redundancy: 4 × ~7 lines = ~28 lines. The Rust counterparts (`scripts/lang/rust/{compile,fmt,lint}.sh`) share only a 4-line prelude (no `BASE_SHA` resolution), which is why Rust did not warrant extraction. Likely target: `scripts/lang/ts/_nx_affected.sh` exposing `run_nx_target <target> <reason-prefix> [args...]` that internally resolves `BASE_SHA` and calls `run_and_emit`. **Defer triggers**: (a) a 5th Nx-driven TS wrapper lands (e.g., `e2e.sh` in task #15 or a `coverage.sh` follow-on), OR (b) a second Nx-consuming language directory is added (would justify lifting from `lang/ts/_nx_affected.sh` to `lang/_nx_affected.sh`). Current 4-instance shape is borderline; held at deferred because parallel-structure-with-Rust + N=4 + low individual prelude weight keeps the wrappers self-describing for the 3am debugger (ADR-0033 §6 trade-off framing). (A prior draft of this entry cited a 4×-duplicated `TODO(post-#36)` comment as additional consolidation pressure; that pressure was retired at Gate 3 by relocating the marker into `scripts/lang/_get_base_ref.sh` + `/work/TODO.md` per code-reviewer F1, so the extraction trigger now depends purely on (a) or (b).) Surfaced during Wave 2 #5 DRY review (2026-05-11).

- [ ] **`tests/common/test_state.rs` per-service test scaffolding (AC + MC + GC, three-crate sibling)**: `crates/ac-service/tests/common/test_state.rs` (AC, ADR-0032 Step 4 — `make_app_state`, `seed_signing_key`, `seed_service_credential`, `TEST_CLIENT_SECRET`), `crates/mc-service/tests/common/mod.rs` (MC, ADR-0032 Step 3 — equivalent per-service AppState/Config builders + DB seeding helpers), and `crates/gc-service/tests/common/jwt_fixtures.rs` (GC, ADR-0032 Step 5 — JWT signing fixtures: `TestKeypair`/`TestUserClaims`/`TestServiceClaims`/`build_pkcs8_from_seed`) all house in-crate test scaffolding. Three-crate sibling pattern matches the per-service Cat B precedent (`record_token_refresh_metrics`). Likely consolidation target if a fourth caller emerges: extend the per-service `*-test-utils` crate if the helpers stay service-private (most likely — each service has a distinct `AppState`/JWKS topology), or promote to a workspace-level `crates/test-utils-common` if a test-helper genuinely needs cross-service share. Evaluate when 4th caller emerges. Surfaced during ADR-0032 Step 4 @dry-reviewer Finding 2; GC sibling added in Step 5.

- [ ] **GC dashboard panel for `gc_meeting_join_*` `participant` axis (ADR-0032 Step 5)**: The `participant=user|guest` label was added to the `gc_meeting_join_total` / `gc_meeting_join_duration_seconds` / `gc_meeting_join_failures_total` family in Step 5. Existing panels in `infra/grafana/dashboards/gc-overview.json` (`sum by(status)`, `sum by(error_type)`, success-rate ratio) work unchanged because Prometheus aggregates over the new label by default. Estimated ~30 LoC to `gc-overview.json` (one new panel section). Deferred from Step 5 per @team-lead authorization (ADR-0032 Step 5 plan-stage scope decision).

Production handler integration pending — see `crates/gc-service/src/repositories/mod.rs:21` "will be used in meeting join handler". Block on: ADR-0033 weak-form typed-label-source rule landing first. Surfaced during ADR-0032 Step 5 by @test (Path A reclassification + F1 finding 2026-04-27).

- [ ] **GC `gc_caller_type_rejected_total{actual_type}` cardinality clamping (ADR-0032 Step 5 finding F1)**: `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/auth_layer.rs:241` reads `claims.service_type` directly into the label without allowlist-clamping to {`meeting-controller`, `media-handler`, `global-controller`}. AC's JWKS-issuance trust boundary makes this low-severity (only AC-signed tokens reach the rejection branch, and AC emits only the 4 enumerated values), but a forged or off-spec token would inject an arbitrary string as a Prometheus label. Fix: clamp at the emission site with a `match` over known values, defaulting to `"unknown"`. ~5 LoC. Surfaced during ADR-0032 Step 5 @observability F1 finding.

- [ ] **TS production-file path filter — `no-secrets-in-ts.sh` + `no-pii-in-logs-ts.sh` (Wave 2 #6, ADR-0033)**: Both guards carry a byte-identical 16-line inline filter (constraint-#6 directories + type-decl + test/fixture path excludes) at `scripts/guards/simple/ts/no-secrets-in-ts.sh:58-75` and `no-pii-in-logs-ts.sh:64-81`. `name-guard-dt-client.sh:68-84` uses a related-but-distinct shape (positive include-list prefix gate `^packages/(sdk-core|web-app)/src/` first, then a SUBSET of the same excludes), so it is NOT a 3rd caller for extraction purposes (rule of three with asymmetry — the predicate is different in kind, not degree). **Defer trigger**: a 4th simple/ts/ guard that needs the EXACT same byte-identical filter (e.g., `no-eval-in-ts.sh`, `no-debug-statements-ts.sh`). **Likely target**: `filter_ts_production_paths()` in `scripts/guards/common.sh` taking a file list on stdin/argv, emitting filtered files on stdout. **Why not a variadic `filter_test_code_ts <file> [extra-patterns...]`**: that signature would invite misuse by include-list callers (like `name-guard-dt-client`) and paper over the include/exclude asymmetry. Structural alignment requirement (same var names `FILE_LIST`/`f`/`CHANGED`, same regex anchor, same comment shape pointing at sibling) was honored by the implementer per @code-reviewer's review framework, so future extraction is mechanical. Surfaced 2026-05-12 by ADR-0033 Wave 2 #6 DRY review.

- [ ] **Cross-stack `CANONICAL_SERVICES` mirror — Rust ↔ Bash (ADR-0034 Wave 1 F-DRY-3 residue, 2026-05-19)**: Wave 1 collapsed the 3 Rust mirrors of `{ac, gc, mc, mh}` into the canonical `crates/dt-guard/src/common/services.rs::CANONICAL_SERVICES` const + `SERVICE_METRIC_PREFIX_RE` derived from it. The 5th mirror — `scripts/guards/common.sh:343 CANONICAL_SERVICES` (bash associative array) — remains a separate stack. Cross-stack collapse to a single SoT requires either (a) bash codegen from Rust (e.g., a `dt-guard emit-services-shell` subcommand that prints the array on demand, sourced by `common.sh`), (b) a TOML/JSON intermediary read by both stacks at startup, or (c) a `validate-services-sync.sh` meta-guard that fails when the two enumerations diverge. Adding a new service still requires updating Rust + Bash separately until then. **Defer trigger**: a 5th service joins (likely to surface the divergence as a real bug rather than hypothetical), OR a Rust-side refactor renames a service prefix (the bash mirror would silently keep the old name). Surfaced 2026-05-19 by ADR-0034 Wave 1 DRY review F-DRY-3 cross-stack residue.

- [ ] **Cross-stack exclusion/slug-derivation parity — Bash `_gate2_binding.sh` ↔ Rust `cross_boundary_scope.rs` (task #51 Gate-2 authority gate, 2026-06-09)**: `scripts/lang/_gate2_binding.sh::gate2_is_excluded` deliberately mirrors `crates/dt-guard/src/cross_boundary_scope.rs::is_symmetric_exclusion` (`:64-84`) entry-for-entry — `docs/TODO.md` exact, `docs/specialist-knowledge/*/INDEX.md` single-segment, `docs/user-stories/*.md` depth-2 — and `gate2_derive_slug` / `gate2_staged_complete_slug` mirror `find_active_main_md` (`:192-202`). Same cross-stack-no-single-SoT class as the `CANONICAL_SERVICES` Rust↔Bash entry below: the two stacks cannot share executable code (the pre-commit hook is coreutils-only by design — it must NOT depend on a built dt-guard binary, see `_gate2_binding.sh:16-22`), so the parity is maintained by hand. The ONE intentional divergence is documented and correct: gate2 widens `docs/devloop-outputs/**` tree-wide (vs the Rust precedent's self-referential single-main.md exemption) because Gate-3 write-back would otherwise self-invalidate the binding (`_gate2_binding.sh:106-111`). NOT true duplication — no shared helper was reimplemented (the Rust fn is a guard-policy function, not a sourceable bash SoT); per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. **Risk if it drifts**: a Rust-side change to `is_symmetric_exclusion` (e.g. adding/removing an exempt path, or tightening `is_user_story_path`) would silently desync the gate2 binding's universe from the cross-boundary-scope guard's, so a file could be bound by one and exempt by the other. The `scripts/guards/simple/selftest-gate2-verdict.sh` (j)-block asserts the bash predicate's direction (MUST-BE-BOUND vs MUST-BE-EXCLUDED smuggling paths) but does NOT cross-check it against the Rust precedent at build time. **Defer trigger / consolidation options**: (a) a `dt-guard emit-exclusions-shell` subcommand that prints the predicate for `_gate2_binding.sh` to source (mirrors option (a) of the `CANONICAL_SERVICES` entry) — but this would reintroduce the built-binary dependency the hook intentionally avoids, so it is only viable if the hook is allowed a graceful binary-absent fallback; (b) a `validate-gate2-exclusion-parity` meta-guard (Rust-side test, runs in the bash-4 pipeline not the hook) that asserts the bash predicate and the Rust fn agree over a shared fixture-path corpus — this is the lower-risk option (keeps the hook coreutils-only, catches drift in CI). Recommended: option (b) when either side's exclusion set next changes, OR if a third consumer of the exclusion semantics emerges. Owner: dry-reviewer to propose; infrastructure (gate2 owner) + code-reviewer (cross_boundary_scope owner) co-consumers. Surfaced 2026-06-09 by dry-reviewer Gate 2 review of task #51 (`docs/devloop-outputs/2026-06-09-gate2-authority-verdict-gate/main.md`).

- [x] **`MacroKind`-derived alternation in `crates/dt-guard/src/metric_macros.rs`** (ADR-0034 Wave 1 E-DRY-1, surfaced 2026-05-19; resolved 2026-05-22 in Wave 2 #45). Wave 2 collapsed the three-way encoding onto `MacroKind::ALL` as the single source of truth: `impl MacroKind { pub const ALL: &'static [Self] = &[...]; }` at `crates/dt-guard/src/metric_macros.rs:42-93`; `LABEL_MACROS` + `DESCRIBE_MACROS` + `MACRO_NAME_ALTERNATION` all derived from `MacroKind::ALL` at `:99-127`; the regex statics `MACRO_INVOCATION_RE` + `MACRO_INVOCATION_WITH_FIRST_ARG_RE` consume `*MACRO_NAME_ALTERNATION` so adding a new variant auto-extends every consumer. Defer-trigger (fourth macro family / cargo-feature-gating) closes without intervention. Resolution pointer: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-20-wave2-bash-guards-dt-guard/main.md` (Wave-2 in-loop fix-up).

- [x] **8-way wrapper-prelude duplication in `scripts/guards/simple/*.sh` dt-guard wrappers (ADR-0034 Wave 1 E-DRY-2, 2026-05-19)**: Resolved 2026-05-20 in `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-19-wave1-python-guards-dt-guard/` in-loop fix-pass (landed early; user accepted the "tracking overhead exceeds fix cost + defer trigger unlikely to fire as a fix-event" framing). Extracted shared prelude to `scripts/guards/simple/_dt_guard_wrapper.sh` (non-executable so `run-guards.sh:180` `[[ -x ]]` gate skips it). Each of the 8 wrappers collapses to a 3-line `source ... <subcommand>` invocation; helper computes SCRIPT_DIR/REPO_ROOT/DT_GUARD + binary-missing check + `exec "$DT_GUARD" <subcommand> --root "$REPO_ROOT"`. Smoke-tested: STATUS=OK path via `validate-doc-citations-no-line-numbers.sh` → exit 0; binary-missing path via `DT_GUARD=/nonexistent` → `STATUS=FAIL REASON=dt-guard-binary-missing` + exit 1; full guard suite 31/31 pass.

- [x] **`scripts/guards/run-guards.sh` per-guard timeout case-classifier duplicated across verbose / non-verbose branches (Wave 1 #11 test F1)**: Resolved 2026-05-19 in `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-19-wave1-python-guards-dt-guard/` in-loop fix-pass. Extracted `classify_guard_exit(exit_code, captured)` shell function at `scripts/guards/run-guards.sh:132`, called from both verbose branch (`:189`, empty captured) and non-verbose branch (`:196`, `$OUTPUT`). Load-bearing `|| true` sentinel + comment block + capture-form preserved verbatim. Smoke-tested with 10s-sleep fake guard vs 1s timeout → exit 124 → `STATUS=FAIL REASON=guard-timeout-validate-sleepy` correct in both modes.

- [ ] **Per-toolchain cache-volume + env-var pattern in `infra/devloop/devloop.sh` `podman run` (3 instances, browser-client-join task #42)**: After task #42 lands, the `podman run -d --name "$DEV_CONTAINER"` block at `infra/devloop/devloop.sh:516-532` carries 3 sibling cache-volume mounts paired with toolchain env vars: `-v cargo-registry:/tmp/cargo-home/registry` + `-v cargo-git:/tmp/cargo-home/git` + `-e CARGO_HOME=/tmp/cargo-home` (cargo, two volumes one var), and `-v pnpm-store:/tmp/pnpm-store` + `-e npm_config_store_dir=/tmp/pnpm-store` (pnpm, one volume one var). The shape is "shared download cache as a named volume + toolchain env var pointing at it"; the user-story §"Surfaced Feedback Log" explicitly endorses this as the cross-toolchain canonical for devloop dev containers. **Defer trigger**: a 3rd toolchain joins (likely candidates: uv/pip cache for Python tooling if Python ever lands in the workspace; a Java/Maven/Gradle local repo if a JVM consumer of the proto contracts emerges; a Go module cache if Go services are added). **Likely target**: a small bash helper in `infra/devloop/devloop.sh` (or a sourced fragment) that registers `(volume-name, container-path, env-var-name, env-var-value)` tuples and emits the corresponding `-v`/`-e` `podman run` args, OR — if the cardinality stays at 3 toolchains and only the `podman run` call needs them — a literal block kept as-is. Current 3-instance shape is below the abstraction threshold because (a) the cargo pair (registry + git) is structurally distinct from the pnpm singleton, so a tuple helper would have to model the 1-or-2-volumes-per-toolchain variability, and (b) the env-var names diverge per toolchain (`CARGO_HOME` vs. `npm_config_store_dir`) so no single var-name convention emerges. Held at deferred — three sibling lines next to each other in `podman run` args is the right level of explicitness. Surfaced 2026-05-14 by browser-client-join task #42 DRY review (R-62, ADR-0033 Wave 2 follow-up).

- [ ] **`tracing_subscriber::registry()` init block duplicated across all four service `main.rs` (browser-client-join task #24)**: `crates/ac-service/src/main.rs:27-33`, `crates/gc-service/src/main.rs:56-62`, `crates/mc-service/src/main.rs:78-84`, `crates/mh-service/src/main.rs:52-58` each carry a near-identical 6-line `tracing_subscriber::registry().with(EnvFilter::try_from_default_env().unwrap_or_else(|_| "<svc>=<level>,tower_http=debug".into())).with(fmt::layer().json()).init()` block. Once the four service-wiring tasks (#25 AC, #26 GC, #6 MC, #27 MH) land, each will additionally `.with(otel.layer)` from the common OTel helper (`crates/common/src/observability/otel.rs::init_otel`) before `.init()`, so the duplication grows from 6 to 7 lines per service. **Defer trigger**: a 5th service joins the workspace, OR a 3rd common layer (e.g. a metrics-events layer) needs composing into the same Registry stack. **Likely target**: `common::observability::install_default_subscriber(svc_name, default_filter, otel_layer)` that handles registry construction + `.init()`; services pass their per-service default filter (`ac_service=debug` for AC, `mc_service=info` for MC, etc.) and the helper-produced OTel layer. **Not done in task #24** because (a) the per-service EnvFilter defaults diverge (`ac_service=debug` vs. `mc_service=info`) and a wider design pass is needed to decide whether the default belongs in the helper signature, an env-var fallback chain, or the per-service `Config`; (b) the helper would expand task #24's scope from "OTel SDK + interceptor" to "subscriber stack", which conflicts with the layer-return contract that explicitly leaves Registry composition to callers (`crates/common/src/observability/otel.rs:69-70`); (c) the helper has to land first as it does today before any subscriber consolidation has a stable layer-return shape to depend on. Surfaced 2026-05-23 by browser-client-join task #24 DRY review (R-54, R-56).

- [ ] **The "org exists and is active" lookup is implemented three times across two services, and one of the three carries the exact defect story R-6 just removed from the others** (surfaced 2026-08-14 by dry-reviewer at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/`, task #2 of the story-runner-hardening story). Three sites: (a) `crates/ac-service/src/repositories/organizations.rs:31 get_by_subdomain` — `WHERE subdomain = $1 AND is_active = true`, returning `Option<Organization>`, so organization-missing and organization-inactive collapse into one `None`. This is **the identical mechanism** R-6 removed from GC's creation CTE, still live in AC, and it is production-reachable: `crates/ac-service/src/middleware/org_extraction.rs:113` is its only caller (ADR-0020 subdomain extraction). (b) `crates/ac-service/src/repositories/organizations.rs:60 get_by_id` — already solves the distinction correctly (no `is_active` filter; `is_active` carried on the struct so the caller can tell missing from inactive) but is `#[allow(dead_code)]` with zero callers. So AC contains both the bug and its fix, and the fix is the dead one. (c) `crates/gc-service/src/repositories/meetings.rs` — GC now reads the org columns inline in the creation CTE with no Rust struct at all, deliberately (see below). **Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication** — nothing in a shared home was reimplemented (`crates/common/` has no organization model or lookup of any kind), so per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked rather than fix-or-defer. **Explicitly NOT to be "fixed" by having GC call AC's repository**: separate crates, `AcError` vs `GcError`, and GC's lookup is fused into an atomic CTE whose single-statement property is the load-bearing security claim (`meetings.rs` module header) — extracting it to a shared function would either break that atomicity or export a meetings-specific CTE as if it were a generic org lookup. **Fix when acted on**: the AC-side half stands alone and is the valuable half — give `get_by_subdomain` a three-way outcome the way GC's `MeetingRefusal` now does, and either wire up `get_by_id` or delete it. Whether an `Organization` model belongs in `crates/common/` is a separate and much weaker question: two consumers, one of which (GC) deliberately wants no struct. **Defer trigger**: any devloop touching AC's org-extraction middleware or adding a second GC consumer of org state. Owner: auth-controller (+ dry-reviewer to re-check whether a shared model is warranted at that point).

- [ ] **`McRejectionReason` is mapped to strings by two parallel `match` statements, one exhaustive and one not** (surfaced 2026-08-14 by dry-reviewer at Gate 1 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/`). `crates/gc-service/src/services/mc_assignment.rs:288-299` matches the same enum twice in the same function: once to `(status, rejection_reason)` metric labels (exhaustive, five arms) and once to a client-facing `reason_str` (four arms ending in `_ =>`). Adding a variant therefore updates the metric label but silently inherits the generic `"No meeting controllers available"` message — the label and the message disagree, and only the label half reds at compile time. This is the "parallel string tables" shape that GC's new `MeetingRefusal` deliberately avoids: story R-6 established the in-crate convention of one `&'static str` method on the cause enum (`MeetingRefusal::metric_label()`), mirroring `GcError::error_type_label()`, with the two-taxonomy junction pinned by a test (`refusal_maps_to_its_error_variant` in `errors.rs`). `McRejectionReason` predates that convention and is now the only GC cause-enum not following it. **Not a same-owner sibling of R-6's mechanism** — R-6's defect is a zero-row query result encoding several unrelated preconditions; this is the *fix pattern* applied inconsistently elsewhere — which is why it was TODO-tracked at Gate 1 rather than folded into task #2. **Fix when acted on**: give `McRejectionReason` a `metric_label()` and a `client_message()`, both exhaustive, and delete the `_ =>` arm so a new variant is a compile error at both sites. ~12 LoC plus join-path test surface; `services/mc_assignment.rs` was outside task #2's changeset. **Defer trigger**: the next devloop adding an `McRejectionReason` variant or touching MC assignment rejection handling. Owner: global-controller.

- [ ] **gRPC input validation duplication**: MC and MH services duplicate validation logic (~100 lines). Locations: `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/mc_service.rs`, `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/mh_service.rs`
- [ ] **Heartbeat interval constants**: Defined in 3 places with different names. Locations: `mc_service.rs`, `mh_service.rs`, `meeting-controller/gc_client.rs`

## Port Constant Scattering (Kind / K8s / Env-Tests)

- [ ] **MC/MH port constants duplicated across 6+ files with no single source of truth**: Host ports (4433, 4434, 4435, 4436), NodePorts (30433, 30434, 30435, 30436), and observability ports (9090/30090, 3000/30030, 3100/30080) are hardcoded independently in `infra/kind/kind-config.yaml`, `infra/services/{mc,mh}-service/service.yaml` (NodePort values), `infra/services/{mc,mh}-service/{mc,mh}-{0,1}-configmap.yaml` (advertise addresses), `infra/services/{mc,mh}-service/configmap.yaml` (bind addresses), `infra/docker/{mc,mh}-service/Dockerfile` (EXPOSE + bind defaults), `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh` (print output), and `crates/env-tests/src/cluster.rs` (ClusterPorts::default). Changing a port in one file without updating all others causes silent breakage. Consider extracting port assignments to a shared config (e.g., `ports.env` sourced by setup.sh, referenced by Kustomize configMapGenerator). The new `kind-config.yaml.tmpl` (ADR-0030) correctly uses envsubst placeholders for its hostPorts, avoiding this issue for devloop clusters. Low priority — manual workflow only. **Update (R-37, task #4)**: the AC/GC host-side E2E ports are now also scattered — static host ports 8443 (AC) / 8444 (GC) and NodePort containerPorts 30082 (AC) / 30180 (GC) are hardcoded in `infra/kind/kind-config.yaml` (new AC/GC `extraPortMappings`), `infra/kind/kind-config.yaml.tmpl` (30082/30180 under `${HOST_PORT_*}`), `infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/services/{ac,gc}-service/nodeport.yaml`, `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh` (`print_access_info`), and the committed client config (`packages/web-app/vite.config.ts` proxy targets `127.0.0.1:8443/8444`). Same single-source-of-truth opportunity as MC/MH above.

- [ ] **AC and GC encode `DATABASE_URL` in two independently-authored places with nothing asserting they agree (2026-08-14)**: Surfaced during review of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/main.md` (owner @database; **pre-existing, not introduced by that diff**). GC's connection string is a literal in `infra/services/gc-service/secret.yaml:14` (`postgresql://…postgres.dark-tower.svc.cluster.local:5432/dark_tower`); AC's arrives via `secretKeyRef` → `ac-service-secrets` (`infra/services/ac-service/statefulset.yaml:42-46`), a Secret not tracked in this repo. Both services read the **same** `organizations` table, defined once in `migrations/20250118000001_initial_schema.sql:5` — so this is one logical database with two authored values and no guard on their agreement (CLAUDE.md: "when two places encode the same value, they will drift").

  **Why it matters concretely**: if the two ever diverge, AC mints a validly-signed token naming an org row GC cannot see, and GC returns `500 ORGANIZATION_NOT_PROVISIONED` — the referential-integrity branch added by R-6. That branch is otherwise reachable only by out-of-band mutation, so a config divergence would present as data corruption. The failure is silent until a meeting-create request happens to exercise it.

  **Already operator-visible, which is why this is worth closing rather than just knowing**: `docs/runbooks/gc-deployment.md` now instructs a deploy-time operator to check whether GC and AC point at the same database *before* reaching for a rollback. That turns an unguarded config invariant into a manual triage step performed under time pressure — the runbook is the compensating control, not the fix.

  **Shape of the fix**: derive both from one source (a shared Secret, or a Kustomize `configMapGenerator`/`secretGenerator` consumed by both services) rather than authoring them separately; failing that, a validation-pipeline check asserting the two resolve equal. Same defect class as the port scattering above. Owner: infrastructure + database. Defer trigger: next infra touch to either service's Secret wiring, or any report of `org_not_provisioned` in a non-test environment.

- [ ] **Auto rollout-restart on cert-secret recreate in `setup.sh`**: `scripts/generate-dev-certs.sh` uses 14-day WebTransport leaf validity (Chrome's `serverCertificateHashes` cap), so expiry is a routine dev event. On an already-running cluster, `setup.sh` regenerating an expiring leaf + recreating the `mc/mh-service-tls` Secret does NOT restart the pods serving the old leaf (`kubectl apply` is a no-op on unchanged Deployment specs). **The remaining work is the automation only**: add the rollout-restart to `setup.sh`'s secret-recreate path. Deliberately deferred out of R-36 — it's a behavior change firing on ALL runs including fresh setup, so it carries regression surface and needs its own test cycle. The operator-facing manual sequence is **documented** in `docs/runbooks/client-dev-local.md` §5 F7 and printed by `generate-dev-certs.sh`'s expiry hint; do not restate it here. Owner: operations / infrastructure. Low priority — dev-loop only. (Was mis-owned to "R-49 dev runbook (task #20)"; task #20 produced prose and discharged the documentation half only — a runbook cannot deliver a `setup.sh` behavior change. Re-owned 2026-07-29 by task #20.)

## Env-Test Resilience & Runbook Validation

- [ ] **`test_all_services_have_logs_in_loki` (`30_observability.rs:116`) hard-asserts Loki readiness with NO retry** (surfaced 2026-06-26 by task #56's first live Layer-7 run). The test does `assert!(cluster.is_loki_available().await, …)` (a single HTTP `GET /ready`) at test start; if Loki isn't warm yet — which it often isn't in the seconds right after a cluster bring-up — the test fails cold even though Loki comes up moments later (verified: a re-run with Loki warm passes). The crate already HAS `src/eventual.rs::assert_eventually` + `ConsistencyCategory::LogAggregation` (45s budget) — the body of this test uses it, but the upfront availability gate doesn't. Fix (either, @observability to decide at pickup): **(a) RETRY** — wrap the `is_loki_available()` gate in `assert_eventually(ConsistencyCategory::LogAggregation, …)` so it tolerates warmup (makes a cold-but-coming Loki GREEN); **(b) CONDITIONAL-SKIP** — skip `test_all_services_have_logs_in_loki` when `is_loki_available()` is false/None, honoring the crate's declared optional-Loki auto-skip intent (makes a genuinely-ABSENT Loki a clean SKIP, not a FAIL). (a) and (b) are complementary: (a) fixes the cold-start flake, (b) covers genuine absence; both together make absent-Loki non-failing. This is the ONLY thing that makes absent-Loki not a loud Phase-2 FAIL — neither task #56's Phase-1e soft-poll nor any classifier can (a classifier would reopen reverse-masking; ruling #3). Owner: env-tests / observability (@observability co-owns per task #56 Gate-1). Out of task #56's scope (that task fills the pipeline wiring, not the env-tests crate); task #56's Layer 7 correctly surfaced this as a loud `STATUS=FAIL`, no masking. Defer trigger: the next env-tests / observability touch, or a recurring cold-start flake in Layer 7. (Compensating control: task #56 may add an observability-endpoint readiness poll to layer7.sh Phase-1e — see that devloop's §Accepted Deferrals — which would convert this from a Phase-2 test flake into a Phase-1 wait.)
- [ ] **Counter-delta baseline capture not robust to stale-series inflation across a pod rollout** (surfaced 2026-08-05, task #19 Gate-2 L7 attempt-2: `26_mh_quic.rs::test_mc_media_connection_update_increments_participant_mh_status_metric` flaked with "did not increase above baseline 5 within 60s (last observed: 1)" — full diagnosis in `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-04-e2e-negative-specs-pipeline/main.md` §Issues Encountered). Mechanism: the `sum(...)` instant-vector baseline is captured while old-pod series are still inside Prometheus's staleness window (Layer-7 Phase-1c `rebuild-all` still draining), those series expire mid-assertion, and the fresh pods' counters can never exceed the inflated baseline → false-negative 60s timeout (loud, never a false pass). Affects BOTH counter-delta helper families: the Rust ones in `crates/env-tests/tests/26_mh_quic.rs` (`assert_participant_mh_status_increases_past`, `mh_notification` twins) and the deliberate TS mirrors in `packages/web-app/e2e/mcMetrics.ts` (`pollUntilSumAbove` consumers; header there carries the matching caveat). The concrete browser-suite exposure is the Layer-7 RETRY path (@test, task #19 review): attempt N's spec increments the counter on the then-current MC pod; attempt N+1's Phase-1c rebuild rolls the pods; the retry's baseline read can include the old pod's soon-to-expire series — a guaranteed 60s timeout on exactly the attempt that burns the final budget. Fix shapes to evaluate at pickup: settle-wait until the target set matches current pods, or a per-current-pod query (`sum by` over live instances), applied consistently to both languages. Owner: env-tests / observability (+ test for the mcMetrics.ts mirror). Defer trigger: next env-tests crate touch or a recurring L7 counter-delta flake.
- [ ] **`26_mh_quic.rs::mc_participant_mh_status_counter` masks Prometheus query failure as a 0.0 reading** (`Err(_) => return 0.0`; same surfacing context as the entry above, flagged 2026-08-05 by task #19/@operations as a fail-loudly/never-mask violation). A Prometheus outage mid-poll reads as "counter is 0" — indistinguishable from a real zero, misdirecting triage toward the service under test. The TS mirror (`packages/web-app/e2e/mcMetrics.ts::promInstantSum`) deliberately THROWS on transport/query failure (global-setup already proved Prometheus healthy, so a failure there is a real problem) — the Rust helper should match that contract; audit sibling helpers in the crate for the same `unwrap_or(0.0)`-on-error shape while there. Owner: env-tests / observability. Defer trigger: next env-tests crate touch or a recurring L7 counter-delta flake.
- [ ] **Resilience tests** (`40_resilience.rs`): Pod restart recovery, network partition handling, DB connection loss. Should cover AC (StatefulSet rollout), GC (Deployment rolling), MC (graceful drain with GC coordination). NetworkPolicy canary tests already exist and are real.
- [ ] **Runbook validation tests** (`90_runbook.rs`): Validate documented operational procedures actually work. Coverage needed: AC key rotation (Scenario 2 in `ac-service-incident-response.md`), MC graceful drain before scaling (`mc-deployment.md` §2), MC registration/heartbeat with GC (`mc-deployment.md` §7), GC token refresh from AC, scale-up with load distribution verification. DB backup/restore is CloudNativePG responsibility, not service-level.
- [ ] **No guard covers `devloop-validation.md` STATUS-rank-ladder ↔ `_common.sh::__status_rank` drift** (surfaced 2026-08-05, task #22 Gate-3 by @operations). The runbook §3 manually restates the aggregation-rank numbers (`SKIPPED-NO-VERB`(0)…`UNKNOWN`(8)) that live authoritatively in `scripts/lang/_common.sh::__status_rank` (0-8). This is a single-source-of-truth duplication (CLAUDE.md: "add a guard that fails validation on drift"): the ladder had already silently drifted (pre-task-#56 numbers omitting `SKIPPED-NO-CLUSTER`/`PRECONDITION_FAILURE`), was found by eye during @observability's task #22 review, fixed on L101/L104/L449 — and the fix's first pass MISSED a sibling on L423 (caught only by @operations re-review). Both the drift and the missed sibling are direct consequences of there being no automated check. Fix (task-sized, NOT a trivial in-tree edit — deferred at task #22): a new `dt-guard` subcommand (ADR-0034) that parses the runbook's numeric `rank N` mentions + the §3 ladder line and diffs them against `__status_rank`'s case arms, failing validation on any mismatch (extend to the enum ORDER, not just presence). Owner: operations (validation-pipeline guards). Defer trigger: next `dt-guard`/guard-pipeline touch, or the next time a rank-ladder edit lands in the runbook.

## Env-Test Self-Sufficiency

- [ ] **AC org provisioning endpoint**: Add an admin/internal API to AC for creating organizations. Env-tests should create their own test org via this endpoint instead of depending on pre-seeded data in `infra/docker/postgres/init.sql`.
- [ ] **Remove init.sql seed data**: Once the AC provisioning endpoint exists, remove the `devtest` org/user seed logic from `infra/docker/postgres/init.sql` and update env-tests (20, 21, 23) to self-provision via the API.
- [-] **Env-test portability — remove localhost/port hardcoding**: Partially resolved. `ClusterPorts::from_env()` (ADR-0030) now reads `ENV_TEST_AC_URL`, `ENV_TEST_GC_URL`, `ENV_TEST_PROMETHEUS_URL`, `ENV_TEST_GRAFANA_URL`, `ENV_TEST_LOKI_URL` with localhost defaults. Remaining: (1) remove `mc_webtransport_url` from `ClusterConnection` — MC/MH endpoints should come from GC join responses, (2) tag the invalid-token negative test as local-only or discover MC endpoint dynamically.

## Bootstrap Data Provisioning (SQL seeding → admin APIs)

*One pattern, several instances: bootstrap state the product should own is written by raw SQL from `setup.sh`, so test suites reach around the services to arrange state, exercise paths no real client can take, and duplicate rules (subdomain shape, cap semantics, credential scopes) that a service should enforce once. Sequencing constraint across all of them: calling an admin API needs an admin credential, and credentials are themselves SQL-seeded, so none escapes seeding until the credential API lands first.*

- [ ] **AC org provisioning API**: `setup.sh:705` has carried `TODO: Replace with AC org provisioning API` at the `seed_demo_org` INSERT for some time; `provision_run_org()` (added 2026-08-15 for per-run layer-7 org isolation) is a second instance and is marked as a stopgap in place. Both write `organizations` rows via `kubectl exec … psql`, which additionally requires the dev container to hold a cluster-admin kubeconfig for what should be an API call. Would also give org **deprovisioning** a home — see the Organization Lifecycle entry, which is blocked on a separate FK problem. Note for planners: during the 2026-08-11 story-runner planning a specialist proposed exactly this and the lead scoped it out as unnecessary, on the incorrect reasoning that existing SQL seeding made an API a non-dependency. It is not a dependency of *provisioning working*; it is the difference between a test fixture and a product capability.
- [ ] **AC admin API for service credentials**: Add `POST /api/v1/admin/service-credentials` to AC for registering/updating service credentials (client_id, scopes, service_type). Idempotent upsert. Currently service credentials are seeded via raw SQL in `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:seed_test_data()`, which has caused bugs (missing `internal:meeting-token` scope for GC) because credential config is decoupled from the code that requires it.
- [ ] **Per-service credential registration Jobs**: Each service (GC, MC, MH) should own its credential registration via a K8s Job in its Kustomize base. The Job calls the AC admin API to register the service's client_id and required scopes. Deploy pipeline ensures AC is ready before downstream services deploy. This way, adding a new scope (e.g., `internal:meeting-token`) is part of the same PR that adds the endpoint requiring it.
- [ ] **Remove setup.sh SQL seeding**: Once the admin APIs and registration Jobs exist, remove the raw SQL seeding from `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh` and `infra/docker/postgres/init.sql` — credentials (`seed_test_data`) **and** organizations (`seed_demo_org`, `provision_run_org`, and the `dt_psql` helper they share). Removing the org path also removes layer 7's need for a cluster-admin kubeconfig to provision.

## Organization Lifecycle

- [ ] **Org deprovisioning is unsupported — deleting an organization fails on a FK violation**: `organizations`' children cascade (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), but the cascade reaches `users` and stops: `auth_events.user_id REFERENCES users(user_id)` has no `ON DELETE` clause (`migrations/20250122000001_auth_controller_tables.sql:59`) and `auth_events` carries no `org_id`, so the delete aborts. Two consequences worth knowing before this is picked up: (1) a retention decision is needed first — audit rows probably should outlive the user, so `ON DELETE SET NULL` rather than cascade; (2) GC maps a missing org to **500** (`MeetingRefusal::OrganizationNotProvisioned`) on the reasoning that `org_id` comes from a signed JWT claim, so an org that does not exist is a provisioning bug rather than a lifecycle state — if deprovisioning ships, revisit that mapping, since a valid token for a deleted org becomes normal and 401/403 fits better. Surfaced 2026-08-15.

## Client Architecture

- [ ] **Evaluate HTTP/3 for AC**: AC currently serves HTTP/1.1 (TCP-based). For consistency with GC, evaluate adding HTTP/3 support. Low priority — client is protocol-agnostic via `fetch()`. Follow-up to ADR-0003.

- [ ] **Roster shows a departed participant for 30–60s on tab-close (leave-latency fix #64 incomplete)**: Manual test 2026-08-08 — closing a browser tab leaves the participant's name in other participants' rosters for 30–60s before it clears. Task #64 added `MC_QUIC_MAX_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 10s, `crates/mc-service/src/config.rs:54`) as the crash/network-loss fallback, but the observed 30–60s ≠ the configured 10s — which means the config value is almost certainly **not wired into the actual QUIC endpoint** (the underlying wtransport/quinn library default ≈30s is firing instead): a plumbed-but-not-wired gap in the same shape as #63 (config field + validation + parse tests exist, but the last wire to the transport is missing). **Verify**: grep where `quic_max_idle_timeout_seconds` is *consumed* by the endpoint builder. Deeper gap: a tab-close should trigger **prompt** departure via a WebTransport close event (~1s), not any idle timeout — the fix is (1) client sends an explicit leave on `pagehide`/`beforeunload` (reliable — do not depend on the browser's implicit connection-teardown timing), (2) MC handles the transport-close event immediately (broadcast ParticipantLeft), (3) idle timeout as last-resort fallback, actually wired this time. **Test-vs-reality gap**: #60's teardown E2E uses Playwright `context.close()` (likely a cleaner teardown than a user tab-close) and asserts *eventual* departure (60s poll), not promptness — so it passed while the real path is slow; the fix's test must assert **bounded** latency via a mechanism that matches a real tab-close. Owner: meeting-controller + client (+ test). Surfaced 2026-08-08 manual test.

- [ ] **`SignalingClient.#terminate` teardown-robustness — emit before `#teardown`** (slug: `signaling-client-terminate-emit-before-teardown`, **Owner: client**). In `packages/sdk-core/src/signaling/SignalingClient.ts`, `#terminate` calls `this.emit('error', …)` on the join-settled branch BEFORE `this.#teardown()`, so a throwing consumer `error` listener skips teardown and strands the transport/reader (`#terminated` already latched → no recovery). Same shape as the `MeetingSession.disconnect` issue fixed in task #14 (symmetric ~2-line fix: run the settle/emit in `try`, `#teardown()` in `finally`). **Pre-existing** — task #14's diff only renamed `#emit`→`emit`, not the ordering — so scoped out of that devloop. Surfaced 2026-06-29 by browser-client-join task #14 (operations reviewer, Gate 3).

## R-14 Transition

- [ ] **R-14 contract test transition — `packages/sdk-core/tests/bundle-content.test.ts`** (forcing-function obligation, scoped to browser-client-join task #9). When task #9 lands `packages/sdk-core/`, the implementer MUST land a Vitest contract test at the canonical path `packages/sdk-core/tests/bundle-content.test.ts` asserting that the prod `vite build --mode production` artifact does NOT contain the literal string `serverCertificateHashes` (R-14 dev-only WebTransport fingerprint trust path must be tree-shaken out under `__DEV_TRUST_FINGERPRINT__=false`). **Enforcement**: `scripts/guards/simple/ts/no-dev-trust-path-in-prod-bundle.sh` mechanically forces this — when `packages/sdk-core/` exists but the canonical test does not, the guard fails CI at state 2 with a message naming this canonical path. Once both exist, the guard moves to state 3 (Vitest test in Layer 4 carries the real check) and optionally state 4 (stale-dist tripwire). #9's devloop may then choose to delete or repurpose the guard. **No additional Nx wiring required**: `scripts/lang/ts/test.sh:10` already runs `nx affected -t test:unit test:component`; precedent `packages/test-utils/project.json`. **If #9's implementer prefers a different test filename**, update `no-dev-trust-path-in-prod-bundle.sh`'s expected path in the same devloop (one-line edit) — the canonical-path hardcoding is the explicit cost of the forcing function. Surfaced 2026-05-12 by devloop-outputs/2026-05-12-ts-guards-task37. **Task #9 resolution (2026-06-21): landed** at the canonical path on the `test:component` tier (real `vite build --mode production` + dist scan); the contract test also asserts `__DEV_TRUST_FINGERPRINT__` is absent (token must be substituted away, not left live), per @semantic-guard.

- [ ] **`sdk-core` framing codec property-based (fast-check) coverage — deferred to R-41 suite** (slug: `sdk-core-framing-fast-check`, browser-client-join task #9). Task #9 landed example-based framing tests in `packages/sdk-core/src/framing/__tests__/length-prefix.test.ts` (encode round-trip, partial reads across chunks, multi-frame chunks, max-frame + zero-length + truncated-prefix rejection, BE byte layout). ADR-0028 §182/184 calls for property-based testing (fast-check) of the frame codec — random payloads / random chunk-split round-trips. Deferred to the R-41 test-suite task to avoid pulling `fast-check` into this foundation package early. **Pickup at R-41 planning**: add `fast-check` devDep to `packages/sdk-core`, add a property test asserting `FrameDecoder(push(randomSplits(encodeFrame(p)))) === p` for arbitrary `p` and arbitrary chunk boundaries, plus a property that no chunk sequence grows `bufferedByteLength` past `4 + MAX_MESSAGE_SIZE`. Owner: test. Surfaced 2026-06-21 by browser-client-join task #9 (test reviewer Gate-1).

- [ ] **`@vitest/coverage-v8` provider not installed — blocks R-41 ≥90% coverage gate** (slug: `vitest-coverage-v8-tooling`, **Owner: test** — coordinate with infrastructure on the workspace-wide devDep). `packages/sdk-core/vitest.config.ts` and `packages/test-utils/vitest.config.ts` both declare `coverage: { provider: 'v8', ... }`, but `@vitest/coverage-v8` is not installed anywhere in the workspace, so `vitest run --coverage` errors `Cannot find dependency '@vitest/coverage-v8'`. Deliberately NOT added in task #9 (Lead scope decision 2026-06-21): it is a workspace-wide test-tooling devDep that would widen `pnpm-lock.yaml` beyond the additive sdk-core subtree and break the clean Mechanical/infrastructure lockfile classification. The Layer-3 `test-coverage` guard is presence-based and already passes; this only blocks the *measured* ≥90% gate. **Pickup at R-41**: add `@vitest/coverage-v8` (pinned, matching the `vitest ^4.1.8` major) as a workspace devDep with infra ack on the wider lockfile, then enforce the 90% threshold in the coverage config / CI. test-utils shares the identical gap and should be wired in the same change. Surfaced 2026-06-21 by browser-client-join task #9 (test reviewer Gate-2).

- [ ] **Root ESLint + Prettier + svelte-check `pnpm lint` toolchain** (slug: `root-eslint-prettier-svelte-check-toolchain`, **Owner: infrastructure**, status: to be scheduled). R-9 calls for "ESLint + Prettier + svelte-check wired into a root `pnpm lint` task." Today every package's `lint` target is `tsc --noEmit` (the repo has no ESLint/Prettier setup), and svelte-check needs Svelte (a later task). Standing up the root toolchain is a monorepo-bootstrap change — root `package.json` devDeps + shared `.eslintrc`/`.prettierrc` + a flip of every existing package's `project.json` `lint` target — which is infrastructure-owned per the R-8 bootstrap precedent. **Deliberately out of scope for browser-client-join task #9**: that task wired only sdk-core's own `tsconfig` + a real `tsc --noEmit` `lint` Nx target (so `nx run-many -t lint` covers the package). **Pickup**: schedule as an infrastructure task; coordinate the per-package `lint`-target migration and add svelte-check once Svelte lands. Surfaced 2026-06-21 by browser-client-join task #9 (client scope call, Gate-1).

## Dashboard Presentation Debt (ADR-0029)

- [ ] **AC overview duplicate "Tokens Issued" stat panel**: Panel id=39 (Traffic Summary row) and panel id=5 (Overview row) both show `sum(increase(ac_token_issuance_total{...}[$__range]))`. Remove panel id=5. Location: `infra/grafana/dashboards/ac-overview.json`

## Observability Debt

- [ ] **No system-overview (single-pane-of-glass) dashboard**: only per-service dashboards exist (`infra/grafana/dashboards/{ac,gc,mc,mh}-overview.json` + `errors-overview.json`; `_template-service-overview.json` is a per-service template, not system-wide). Add a cross-service overview: a curated RED-method top line (rate / errors / duration per service) + key business SLIs (meetings active, join success rate, participants active) + optionally the most important client metrics (see the client-governance entry below). Two design constraints: (a) **ownership is cross-cutting → observability-owned**, NOT any single service — ADR-0031 makes per-service dashboards service-owned, but a system overview crosses boundaries; (b) **curate, don't dump**, and avoid drift from the per-service panels (share the query source or treat it as an explicit curated derived view). Owner: observability. Surfaced 2026-08-08.

- [ ] **Client metrics have plumbing but no governance — dashboards, alarms, AND a coverage guard all missing (ADR-0032 analog for the client); needs a `/debate`**: the GC telemetry proxy (R-2: `POST /api/v1/telemetry/v1/{metrics,traces}`) and its proxy-side ingest metrics (#16: `gc_telemetry_ingest_total` etc.) exist, but the client metrics that flow *through* it have **no Grafana dashboards, no alerts, and — critically — no coverage guard**. Service metrics are governed by ADR-0032 (every metric must have a test + dashboard + catalog entry, enforced by `validate-application-metrics` / metric-coverage guards); the client has none of that machinery. When the deferred client-metrics catalog is built out (browser-client-join stubbed it), without a guard client metrics will drift into the exact silent-failure state ADR-0032 fixed for services — a browser-emitted metric with no dashboard/alert/test, invisible until an incident. **Not a copy of the Rust guard**: client metrics travel a different path (browser → GC proxy → OTel collector → Prometheus, not a service scrape) and live in TS, so the guard must be telemetry-path-aware and `ts`-lang-aware (ties into the ADR-0033 polyglot pipeline — the `ts` lang wants its own metric-coverage guard). Needs a `/debate` (cross-cutting: observability + client + infrastructure + polyglot-guard) to set the client-metric governance pattern, mirroring ADR-0032. Owner: observability + client + infrastructure. Surfaced 2026-08-08.

- [ ] **Dashboard-set completeness guard — no canonical service should silently ship a partial dashboard trio (task #62 finding)**: task #62 found MH shipped only `mh-overview.json` for weeks — `mh-logs.json`/`mh-slos.json` were never created, and no guard caught the gap. `scripts/guards/simple/validate-kustomize.sh` enforces dashboard↔kustomize coverage bidirectionally (every JSON in `infra/grafana/dashboards/` is listed in `infra/grafana/kustomization.yaml` and vice-versa), but nothing asserts that each canonical service HAS the full `overview`+`logs`+`slos` trio — a service with only an overview passes every existing guard. **Action**: add a guard asserting every service in the canonical set {ac, gc, mc, mh} has all three of `<svc>-overview.json`, `<svc>-logs.json`, `<svc>-slos.json` under `infra/grafana/dashboards/`. Derive the service list from a single source (e.g. the crate/service table or an existing manifest), NOT a hardcoded literal, per SSoT — and fail loudly on any missing member. **Related guard gap (task #62, @test)**: the `dashboard-panels` guard (`crates/dt-guard/src/dashboard_panels.rs`) validates metric existence + kind + rate-wrapping, but does NOT validate panel label keys/values against the metric catalog — a panel referencing a real metric with a nonexistent label (e.g. `mh_jwt_validations_total{status="success"}` instead of `result="success"`) passes the guard yet renders "No data" in Grafana (the same silent-broken-dashboard-through-the-gate class that motivates the completeness guard above). Extend the guard to also check panel label keys/values against `docs/observability/metrics/<svc>-service.md`. Owner: observability (with infrastructure for guard wiring). Surfaced 2026-08-06 during task #62 by @observability + @test.
- [ ] **`level` template-var in `*-logs.json` uses empty Loki stream — scope to `{app="<svc>-service"}` across all four services (task #62 / security finding)**: the `level` (Log Level Filter) template variable in the per-service logs dashboards (`{ac,gc,mc,mh}-logs.json`) ships with `stream=""`, so it enumerates log levels across ALL apps in Loki rather than the dashboard's own service. `mh-logs.json` (task #62) was intentionally kept byte-consistent with the `mc-logs.json` reference to avoid diverging one service from the set. **Action**: scope the `level` var's query to `stream="{app=\"<svc>-service\"}"` (mirroring the already-scoped `pod` var) on ALL FOUR logs dashboards in one change, so the pattern stays uniform. Pure query-scoping refinement (Loki label-values query var; no untrusted free-text input, no `regex` reflecting input) — small, cross-service, mechanical. Owner: observability (with security). Surfaced 2026-08-06 during task #62 by @security (plan review); deferred by @team-lead to keep the {ac,gc,mc,mh} set consistent rather than scoping MH alone.
- [ ] **MH SLO dashboard gauges reference alert-hard-limits / unratified targets for handshake + token-refresh latency (task #62 finding)**: `infra/grafana/dashboards/mh-slos.json` (task #62) grounds four of six gauges in documented SLOs or live alert boundaries, but two latency gauges lack a ratified SLO: (a) **WebTransport handshake p99** — the catalog defines no handshake SLO; the gauge's 1s red boundary is borrowed from the `MHWebTransportHandshakeSlow` warning alert, which is defined on **p95** (the gauge shows **p99**, so it can turn red before that alert fires — a deliberate quantile mismatch, no p99 alert exists). (b) **Token refresh p99** — no documented latency SLO; thresholds (2.5s/5s) are derived from the histogram bucket ceiling (5s), mirroring `mc-slos.json`. **Action**: when MH SLOs are ratified in ADR-0011 (the `mh-alerts.yaml` header already notes MH has no error-rate SLO defined yet), reconcile these two gauge thresholds to the ratified targets and add the matching alert(s) (a p99 handshake burn alert, a token-refresh latency alert) so dashboard and alerting agree. Owner: observability (with operations for alert authoring). Surfaced 2026-08-06 during task #62 by @observability + @operations.
- [ ] **GC `normalize_endpoint()` has no telemetry arm — telemetry requests conflate into `endpoint="/other"` on `gc_http_*` (browser-client-join Task #16 → GC follow-up)**: `normalize_endpoint()`/`normalize_dynamic_endpoint()` (`crates/gc-service/src/observability/metrics.rs:182-228`) recognize only `/`, `/health`, `/metrics`, `/api/v1/me`, and the `/api/v1/meetings*` family; the telemetry proxy routes `/api/v1/telemetry/v1/{metrics,traces}` (`crates/gc-service/src/routes/mod.rs:170-175`) normalize to `"/other"`. **Consequence**: the pre-handler telemetry rejects that never reach `gc_telemetry_ingest_total` — 401 (route-layer auth) and the DoS-backstop 413 (body > 2× max_bytes) — are only observable on `gc_http_requests_total{endpoint="/other"}`, conflated with every other unrecognized path, so the catalog's size-rejection UNION and the Scenario 10/11 HTTP-split diagnosis steps are approximate (each carries an explicit caveat referencing this entry). **Action**: add two static match arms for the telemetry routes (bounded literals, no cardinality risk — mirror the existing `/api/v1/meetings` static-arm pattern) + extend `test_normalize_endpoint_known_paths`; then remove the `endpoint="/other"` caveats from `docs/observability/metrics/gc-service.md` (honesty block + `error`-status note), `docs/runbooks/gc-incident-response.md` Scenarios 10-11, `docs/observability/alerts.md` (both new entries), and the `GCTelemetryProxyHighRejectionRate` description. Est. <20 LoC. Owner: global-controller (with observability). Surfaced 2026-08-03 during task #16 by @implementer (observability); deferral approved by @team-lead (decision C — barred by task #16's no-instrumentation constraint, not effort).
- [ ] **MC emits no log event on the `MediaConnectionUpdate` success path — `statuses_count` is unreachable from logs at any `RUST_LOG` (browser-client-join Task #20 → MC observability)**: `handle_media_connection_update` (`crates/mc-service/src/webtransport/connection.rs:663-724`) carries `#[instrument(name = "mc.media_connection_update", fields(connection_id, statuses_count = update.statuses.len()))]`, but emits **no event on the success path** — its only log is a `debug!` on the actor-gone error branch (`:715-721`), which is itself invisible under the deployed `RUST_LOG=info,mc_service=debug` because the target `mc.webtransport.connection` does not prefix-match `mc_service`. Since `tracing_subscriber::fmt::layer().json()` (`crates/mc-service/src/main.rs:120`) writes one line per **event** and no `.with_span_events(...)` is configured, the span exists only as context on events that never occur. **Consequence**: `statuses_count` — the per-join media fan-out width, and the single richest completeness datum MC has — cannot be read from logs at any log level. The `mc_participant_mh_status_total{state}` counter (`crates/mc-service/src/actors/participant.rs:478`) is the only available substitute, and it is strictly weaker: it increments *inside* the per-entry loop, so an **empty** fan-out produces no increment at all and is indistinguishable from "the client never sent an update" — precisely the case (GC returns empty `media_servers`, client still reaches `joined`, MC still logs `Join succeeded`) that most needs an observable. **Action**: add an `info!` on the success path of `handle_media_connection_update` carrying `statuses_count` (and `connection_id`), target `mc.webtransport.connection` so it is visible at the deployed `info` global. ~3 LoC; the span field already computes the value. Then update `docs/runbooks/client-dev-local.md` §4.2, which currently documents the counter-delta workaround and explicitly warns readers **not** to reach for the span field — that warning can be removed once the event exists. Owner: observability (with meeting-controller — the edit is in MC's WebTransport connection handler). Surfaced 2026-07-29 during task #20 by @observability: the runbook draft cited a `jq` filter on the span name as "the completeness signal", which returns empty output on a healthy join — a phantom signal in a document that trains readers to treat absence as evidence. Caught at review; the runbook now uses the counter delta.
- [ ] **R-56 inbound HTTP trace-context extraction for AC (browser-client-join Task #25 → GC #26 landed → AC follow-up)**: AC has no inbound gRPC boundary, so R-56's Tonic server-interceptor has no attach point; task #25 wired only the global W3C propagator (registered for free by `init_otel`). **UPDATE (task #26, 2026-07-03)**: the shared `common` HTTP-transport layer now exists — `crates/common/src/observability/otel_http.rs` (`--paired-with=observability`), framework-agnostic `HeaderInjector`/`HeaderExtractor` over `&http::HeaderMap` + `inject_trace_context`/`extract_trace_context` functions, mirroring `otel_grpc.rs`'s pattern. The producer side is also landed: GC's `ac_client.rs` now injects `traceparent`/`tracestate` on every GC→AC HTTP call (task #26 surface (b)), so AC's inbound requests carry a real header to extract, not a hypothetical one. **Remaining action**: AC itself still needs to wire `otel_http::extract_trace_context` at its own inbound HTTP boundary (axum middleware, same shape as GC's `middleware/otel.rs`) — that consumption is NOT part of task #26's scope (GC-only devloop) and remains open. **Security constraint (@security, unchanged)**: the extraction call reads only `traceparent`/`tracestate` — never `authorization` or any bearer header, and never copies header values into span attributes (enforced structurally by `otel_http.rs`'s write-only `HeaderInjector`/keyed-only `HeaderExtractor`, confirmed by @semantic-guard). Owner: auth-controller (consumption) + observability (layer, already landed). Surfaced 2026-06-25 (task #25); shared-layer blocker resolved 2026-07-03 (task #26).
- [ ] **AC + GC `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT` hardcoded `development` in the prod base configmap (browser-client-join Task #25 / #26 → prod OTel graduation)**: `infra/services/ac-service/configmap.yaml` and `infra/services/gc-service/configmap.yaml` both set `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT: "development"` in the prod base; the Kind overlay only flips `OTEL_ENABLED` to `true`. OTel is dev-only + default-off (`OTEL_ENABLED=false` in base) today, so zero runtime impact — but once prod OTel graduates (`OTEL_ENABLED=true` in a prod overlay), prod telemetry would be mistagged `deployment.environment=development` for BOTH services. **Action**: prod OTel graduation MUST overlay-patch `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT` (e.g. `production`) for every service that lands this pattern (AC done, GC done as of task #26; MC/MH will add the same footgun when #6/#27 land — carry this caveat forward to those tasks too). The prod-overlay `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT` patch must cover ac + gc + mc (and mh once #27's base carries it).
- [ ] **TS metric emission policy — Case A vs Case B (ADR-0033 follow-up)**: ADR-0033 (Polyglot Validation Pipeline) is silent on whether the TypeScript client emits its own application metrics (Case B — browser RUM / OpenTelemetry web SDK to a collector) or is purely a metrics consumer that reports server-side via API (Case A — server emits, no TS-side metric guards needed). The decision drives whether `validate-metric-coverage.sh` and `validate-application-metrics.sh` need to extend to TS file globs, whether `lang/ts/changed.sh` should treat metric-emission sites specially, and whether ADR-0032's presence-guard pattern generalizes to TS. **Action**: needs its own ADR or design discussion before client telemetry tasks (R-19, R-24, R-25, R-26, R-27 in browser-client-join story) land. Surfaced 2026-05-06 by observability in ADR-0033 Round 1.

The story's R-59 text (line ~407) currently shows the GC env value in templated **suffixed** form (`…:4318/v1/{metrics,traces}`). **Hazard**: if INFRA-OTEL populates the GC configmap with the suffixed form AND GC also appends, every forward becomes `…/v1/metrics/v1/metrics` → collector 404 → **502 on every telemetry request** — silent in unit tests, breaks only in the live cluster. **Action for #28**: populate the GC `OTEL_COLLECTOR_ENDPOINT` configmap value with the BARE base (no `/v1/...`), and reconcile the story's R-59 text to match. All six reintroduced atop the new `mc_participant_mh_status_total{state}` metric: (a) the metric itself (`observability/metrics.rs` `record_participant_mh_status`); (b) `MCMediaConnectionAllFailed` alert in `infra/docker/prometheus/rules/mc-alerts.yaml` — observability-specced failed-share ratio (`sum(rate(…{state="failed"}[5m])) / sum(rate(…{state=~"connected|failed"}[5m])) > 0.80` + `> 0.1/s` volume floor, `for: 5m`, `severity: page`), replacing the deleted flat all-fail counter; (c) `mc-incident-response.md` Scenario 11 (Media Connection Failures) + TOC + Version History (operations-authored); (d) `mc-overview.json` "Client-Reported MH Status by State" + "Dropped Client MH Status" panels; (e) post-deploy acceptance gates in `mc-deployment.md` + `mh-deployment.md` (operations-authored; `mh-deployment.md` cross-boundary, `Approved-Cross-Boundary: operations` trailer); (f) catalog entry in `docs/observability/metrics/mc-service.md`. **Deviation (owner-approved):** the (e) gate is a **failed-share ratio `< 0.20`** (with `clamp_min` no-traffic guard) over 30m/2h/24h, NOT the literal `{state="failed"} increase == 0` this breadcrumb first specified — that text predated the redesign and would false-fail every deploy (the per-MH counter increments `failed` on any single client hiccup); observability (metric/alert taxonomy owner) reformulated it, consistent with the alert's `> 0.80` paging line.
- [ ] **R-26 bounded client join LOGS — emission deferred from Task #14 (browser-client-join, PATH B)**: Task #14 wired the five `dt_client_*` join METRICS (R-24/R-25) at their `MeetingSession.join` / `MediaTransport.connectAll` emission sites, but the R-26 bounded join LOGS (`logJoinEvent`/`JoinEvent`/`JoinLogRecord`, scaffolded in `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/logger.ts`) are NOT emitted yet. **Root cause (@observability §3, corrected framing)**: the 7 R-26 events span the WHOLE join lifecycle — `join.started`/`signup_complete`/`gc_token_received` all fire inside `MeetingSession.join` BEFORE `SignalingClient` even exists — but the only `dt_client.join` span is created INSIDE `SignalingClient.join`, so it covers only the signaling sub-portion. There is therefore NO single consistent `trace_id` across all 7 events. A read-only getter on SignalingClient does NOT fix this (the early MeetingSession events still have no span, and correlating some events but not others is worse than a clean defer). The only correct fix is the DESIGN DECISION to relocate the `dt_client.join` ROOT span to `MeetingSession.join` (signaling span becomes a child), which touches task #13 span semantics and warrants its own planning. The metric labels' `meeting_id_hash` (SHA-256 via `crypto.subtle.digest`) computed in THIS task is reusable for the log record, so the follow-up is span/trace_id wiring only. **Action**: (a) relocate the `dt_client.join` root span to `MeetingSession.join` so one trace_id roots the whole join; (b) make SignalingClient's signaling span a child; (c) emit `logJoinEvent` across all 7 `JoinEvent` states from `MeetingSession.join`. Owner: client (with observability). Surfaced 2026-06-29 (task #14); deferral accepted by @observability, scope split locked by @team-lead at Gate-1 (PATH B).
