  - `scripts/lang/_changed_helpers.sh:54` — `diff_touches_path()`'s `__changed_files | awk 'index($0,p)==1 { found=1; exit }'`. **THE WORST SITE IN THE REPO, and it was missed by the first two passes of this entry** (found by @test): `awk … exit` is an early-exit consumer exactly like `grep -q`, and it reproduces where the others do not — 20/20 false negatives from ~72 KB up, versus `grep -qxF`'s 1/20 at the same size. **The composite consequence is worse than either predicate alone, and it has THREE BANDS — do not flatten it in either direction.** `scripts/lang/rust/changed.sh:6` is `diff_touches_path "crates/" || diff_touches_root_files "Cargo.toml" "Cargo.lock" "rust-toolchain.toml"`, so the `||` reads as a safety net. Measured on a faithful replica and confirmed by @test against the real script with a real cache file, 20 runs/point, needle at git-realistic position:
    - **< ~1500 changed files (~72 KB)** — both branches fine (pure-`crates/` N=100 and N=1000: 0/20).
    - **~1500–2500 files** — `diff_touches_path` fails **20/20**, and the fallback rescues the run **only if a root manifest is in the changeset**. A pure-`crates/` changeset here is already **skipped 20/20 at N=2000 (92 KB)** — and that is the common shape of a large Rust change. With `Cargo.toml` also touched, N=2000 recovers 20/20 (`diff_touches_root_files` 0/20 at that size).
    - **> ~3000 files (~138 KB)** — **both branches fail 20/20 regardless**; N=3000 and N=4000 classify Rust as untouched even with `Cargo.toml` in the diff. At this size no arrangement of the gate holds.
    So the `||` is not a degraded safety net — it is a **second independent instance of the same bug** that merely fails later, and @test's `Cargo.lock`/`rust-toolchain.toml` finding below removes the only operands that could have sorted late enough to survive. (An earlier draft of this bullet said the fallback simply "returns 1 too"; @test then measured it failing at N=4000 and generalised to "no arrangement holds". Both overshoot: the middle band is a real, conditional rescue. Recorded precisely because a band structure nobody would guess is exactly what makes this gate's behaviour untrustworthy — it is size-dependent, and the dependence is invisible.)
    The trap is self-reinforcing: the changeset must be large to fire, and a large Rust changeset is precisely a pure-`crates/` one, so **the bigger the Rust change, the more certainly its validation is skipped**. **Severity in the language of the thing protected: on a large Rust changeset, compile / fmt / clippy / test AND the Rust dependency audit are all skipped, silently, with the pipeline green.** That is "fail loudly, never mask" inverted, measured rather than argued. Fix `:54` first.
  - `scripts/lang/_changed_helpers.sh:66` — `diff_touches_root_files()`'s `__changed_files | grep -qxF -- "$f"`. Same class, less exposed than `:54` (intermittent from ~72 KB, deterministic only ~144 KB ≈ 3000 changed files), but the blast radius is the widest (@test): the predicate feeds `scripts/lang/_audit_gate.sh:101,116` — the **dependency audit gate** — as well as `scripts/lang/rust/changed.sh:6` and `scripts/lang/ts/changed.sh:7`. A false negative on the Rust needle therefore **silently skips the security audit**, with a green pipeline. Exposure is asymmetric and measurable: `Cargo.toml` sorts at position **54 of 1395** tracked paths (uppercase sorts before lowercase, so it is in the first 4% of any changeset containing it — maximum SIGPIPE exposure), while `pnpm-lock.yaml` sorts at **1274 of 1395**, so the TS half is far less exposed. **`diff_touches_root_files` has ZERO direct self-test coverage** — `scripts/lang/_changed_helpers.test.sh`'s own header says it is "focused on `diff_touches_glob`" and calls that sibling "security-load-bearing — a wrong match/skip = wrong audit run/skip", while leaving this one untested. Scope the regression test to the **audit gate**, not merely to layer skipping.
  - **Compounding, and it makes the position-54 point decisive** (@test's separate finding, verified): **`Cargo.lock` is gitignored** (`.gitignore:5`; `git ls-files --error-unmatch Cargo.lock` → not tracked) and **`rust-toolchain.toml` does not exist** in this repo. So of `diff_touches_root_files "Cargo.toml" "Cargo.lock" "rust-toolchain.toml"`, **exactly one operand can ever match — `Cargo.toml`, the one at sort position 54 of 1395, i.e. worst-case needle position.** There is no second operand to fall back on. Worse, `scripts/lang/_audit_gate.test.sh:39` asserts `"rust: Cargo.lock changed -> run"`, i.e. it certifies coverage on an input the pipeline **cannot produce** — a test that passes on an impossible input rather than one that cannot fail. Different species from the vacuous-pass bugs this devloop fixed, same family. Include in the operations devloop's scope: either track `Cargo.lock` (it is a binary/application workspace, so committing it is the conventional call and would also make the audit gate mean what its test claims) or drop the dead operands and fix the test.
  - `scripts/workflow/run-story.sh:1212` — `git show HEAD --format= --name-only | grep -q '^audit-suppressions\.toml$'`. `audit-suppressions.toml` sorts very early in git's path order (only dotfile dirs precede it), so the needle is near line 1 — the worst position. Reproduction rate rises with commit size per the race numbers above (0/30 at 80 files, 29/30 at 400); it never becomes a clean threshold.
  **Lower-risk siblings, assessed rather than assumed** (owner `infrastructure`, mine; enumerated so nobody re-derives the list): `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:355,1365` and `infra/devloop/devloop.sh:653` (`kind get clusters | grep -q` — a handful of lines, one write; and a false negative leads to a loud `kind create cluster` failure, not a silent pass); `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:393` (`kubectl get pods -l k8s-app=calico-node | grep -q calico-node` — small label-selected output, match is NOT on line 1, and it sits in a retry loop so a spurious miss costs one iteration); `infra/devloop/devloop.sh:357,406` and `scripts/lang/rust/test.sh:65` (`podman ps --format | grep -q '^name$'` — reachable at ~100+ running containers, consequence is starting a duplicate container, i.e. visibly wrong rather than silently green). Also in the sweep: `scripts/lang/ts/audit.sh:107`, `scripts/dev-web.sh:269,271`, `scripts/test-oauth-integration.sh:41,43,54,112` (`echo "$KUBECTL_LOGS" | grep -q` — a builtin producer with a potentially large log variable, same class).
  **Why not fixed in the R-7 diff**: cross-owner (all three sharp sites are `operations`-owned validation-pipeline gate logic → Domain-judgment under ADR-0024 §6.3, routes to owner-implements), and each site needs its own reachability measurement plus a regression test, which is its own test surface. Fixing them blind by mechanical rewrite is exactly the change-pattern §6.2 forbids classifying as Mechanical.
  **Fix shape — THE PRECEDENT ALREADY SHIPS IN THE SAME FILE, three functions down** (@test). `diff_touches_glob()` at `_changed_helpers.sh:100` measures **0/20 at every size tested, up to 144 KB**, because it reads via **process substitution** — `while IFS= read -r line; do … return 0; done < <(__changed_files)` — so there is no pipe, and an early `return` cannot become a producer SIGPIPE. The operations devloop does not need to invent a shape: **make `:54` and `:66` look like `:100`.** That is more convincing than a recipe because it already ships and already passes, and it keeps the three predicates in one idiom instead of two. Where process substitution does not fit, read into a variable and match with a herestring (`x="$(producer)" || x=""; grep -qxF "$needle" <<<"$x"` — the shape used for the `setup.sh` fix); only where streaming genuinely matters, `producer | grep -qxF … || rc=$?` with the 141 case handled explicitly.
  **REGRESSION-TEST SHAPE IS PRESCRIBED, and a count-based repro MUST NOT SHIP** (@test). An earlier draft of this entry said "drive a producer past its buffer"; per the race numbers above that yields a test which fails to reproduce the bug ~1 run in 30 even at N=400 — a test that cannot be trusted to have caught anything, under ADR-0028's zero-retry policy. That is this devloop's own vacuous-pass class one level up. Use a producer that **blocks after emitting the match**, which forces the SIGPIPE window open with certainty and needs no size tuning: `{ printf 'needle\n'; sleep 0.2; printf 'more\n'; } | grep -q '^needle$'` — verified **30/30** rc=141, versus 2/30–29/30 for every count-based driver. Use a blocking read rather than `sleep` to keep it at ~0s. **Schedule as an operations devloop**; the `infrastructure`-owned siblings above ride along or are explicitly declined with the reasoning already recorded here. Owner: operations (lead), infrastructure (siblings).

- [ ] **Deploy-step validation**: Devloops that modify K8s manifests, kustomization files, setup.sh, or other deploy infrastructure currently have no way to validate their changes actually work — issues like Kustomize path restrictions, postgres security context incompatibilities, and selector mutation only surface when running `setup.sh` against a real cluster. Need to figure out how to incorporate deploy validation into the devloop workflow (e.g., `kustomize build` dry-run, Kind cluster in CI, or a lightweight deploy-test step).

- [ ] **absorb-devloop.sh: detect duplicate tracking-table rows after cherry-pick** (surfaced 2026-06-22 after many manual dedup commits during the post-PR-58 debt-repayment wave): `.gitattributes` sets `docs/user-stories/*.md merge=union` so parallel devloops appending different tracking rows don't conflict. But when a devloop modifies an existing row IN-PLACE (the Step 8 Pending→Completed flip) AND the host has made any intervening edit to user-story.md between clone-creation and absorb, the cherry-pick context drifts and `union-merge` silently keeps BOTH the original Pending row AND the new Completed row instead of replacing. The result is a duplicate row that the operator has to spot and manually remove every absorb. Observed at #46/#47/#48/#49/#52/#9/#10 (every absorb that landed after an intervening host edit) but NOT at #50/#54/#55 (no intervening host edit between clone-create and absorb). Fix: post-cherry-pick scan in `infra/devloop/absorb-devloop.sh` for duplicate task-ID rows in the affected user-story file (e.g., `awk -F'|' '/^\| [0-9]+ \|/ {print $2}' | sort | uniq -d`); if any appear, exit non-zero with a clear message naming the duplicated IDs so the operator can dedup before the absorb completes — same "refuse to silently apply a bad resolution" discipline the script already has for other conflict classes. Strictly an after-the-fact detector, not a preventer, but removes the silent-bad-resolution failure mode. Same dupe class probably lurks for `docs/TODO.md` (also `merge=union`) — we just haven't seen the host edit TODO.md between clone-create and absorb yet. **Update 2026-08-17**: the tracking table this was filed against was retired by the story-runner-hardening story, but the hazard moved rather than closed — the `dt-story` manifest now lives in the same `merge=union` file with the same in-place flip (`status: pending` → `status: completed`, plus a new `slug:` line). It should now fail *loudly* rather than silently, since duplicate keys in one YAML mapping do not parse, and the story-runner-hardening absorb came through clean (4 `status:`, 4 `slug:`); neither is a tested guarantee, so the post-absorb check is `dt-story validate` on the affected story file. Owner: infrastructure.

- [ ] **`absorb-devloop.sh` cannot resume, and its own hand-off trips the Gate-2 pre-commit hook** (both surfaced 2026-08-17 absorbing the story-runner-hardening story; they compound, so they are filed together). (a) **No resume**: the script has no in-progress detection — no `sequencer`/`CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` check — and `:168` starts a fresh `git cherry-pick`, which git refuses while one is active. Its documented contract (`:32` exit 3, `:171`) is to hand off to plain git on conflict, so re-running after resolving does nothing useful. (b) **The hand-off then blocks**: a clean `git cherry-pick` does not run `pre-commit`, but `git cherry-pick --continue` completes through the normal commit path and does — so the Gate-2 binding refuses every devloop commit in the queue for want of a verdict artifact, which lives in the run dir and does not travel with the patch. Its advice ("run `./scripts/layer-all.sh`") is unfollowable on the host, where layer 7 has no cluster. Net effect: the first conflict in any absorb strands the operator. Fix: run the script's own picks with hooks disabled (`git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null cherry-pick …`) and say so in the summary — legitimate because the content was validated on the source branch and the hook documents itself as "LOCAL-ONLY / ADVISORY … not a security boundary", with CI's from-scratch re-run as the real enforcement. Add in-progress detection that continues the existing queue instead of starting a new one. Note (a) has a second trigger independent of conflicts: `git cherry` compares patch-ids, so a planning commit **amended on the target after the clone branched** is not recognised as already-absorbed and is queued as an empty pick the operator must `--skip` — which is exactly how this absorb began. Owner: infrastructure.

- [ ] **Devloops wiring up new code-quality tooling don't auto-fix the existing surface** (surfaced 2026-06-24 during task #28's Gate 2): when a devloop introduces a new lint/format/type-check rule that applies to an existing language surface, the rule starts producing violations on code that was authored before the rule existed. The devloop introducing the rule typically ships green (its own code conforms; the rule has nothing to compare against in-diff). But every subsequent devloop touching the same surface inherits the violations as "pre-existing branch debt" and blocks at Gate 2/3 — surfacing as "I want to do my work but X unrelated files violate a rule I didn't introduce." Two concrete cases on this branch: (1) task #17 wired up Prettier + ESLint config over `packages/sdk-core/`, but #11's TS code (authored before #17 was wired) never saw prettier; the formatting violations surfaced first during #12's planning and then again during #28's Gate 2, with both implementers stalled on cross-boundary cleanup that isn't their lane. (2) Same shape would apply to a future devloop wiring up a new clippy lint, a new dt-guard check, or a stricter tsc flag — the rule lands clean against an empty diff, then bites every subsequent devloop on the same surface. Whatever the fix shape is (rule-introducing-devloop runs auto-fix as part of its scope / Lead-side checklist when reviewing tooling-introducing plans / a separate "branch-health" devloop variant that lands the auto-fix sweep alongside the rule / something else), the principle is: a devloop introducing a rule should leave the existing surface compliant with the rule by the time it commits, so subsequent devloops never inherit "pre-existing rule violations" as a Gate-2 blocker. Solution open — needs scoping. Owner: operations (devloop workflow design) + infrastructure (any tooling support).

- [ ] **`validate-env-config` silently skips MC + MH (per-instance deployment filenames) — false-confidence STATUS=OK** (surfaced 2026-07-06 by @operations at task #6 Gate-3 review; accepted spin-out, @team-lead-endorsed). `dt-guard env-config`'s workload discovery `find_workload()` (`crates/dt-guard/src/env_config.rs:53-58`) only recognizes the literal filenames `deployment.yaml` / `statefulset.yaml`. AC (`statefulset.yaml`) and GC (`deployment.yaml`) match and are validated; **MC and MH use per-instance names** (`mc-0-deployment.yaml` / `mc-1-deployment.yaml`, `mh-0-deployment.yaml` / `mh-1-deployment.yaml`) so `find_workload` returns `None` → the guard emits `WARN dt-guard auxiliary skip (no workload manifest)` and moves on, yet still exits `STATUS=OK REASON=env-config-clean-4-services`. Net effect: the config↔deployment cross-check (Checks 1-3: required env vars present in workload; `configMapKeyRef` keys exist in configmap; configmap keys referenced by ≥1 workload) provides **zero coverage for two of four services**, and the `-4-services` REASON overcounts (only AC+GC were actually validated). This is the same false-confidence / silent-skip class task #50 addressed — a green guard that isn't checking what the reader assumes. Concretely bit task #6: the 4 OTel keys had to be surfaced in BOTH `mc-0` and `mc-1` deployments (the "easy to half-do" case), and that invariant shipped correct ONLY because @operations hand-verified it — the guard could not. **Fix (three parts):** (a) `find_workload` must discover multiple per-instance workloads — glob `*-deployment.yaml` / `*-statefulset.yaml` in the service dir (falling back to the canonical singular names), returning a set rather than an `Option`; (b) decide **union-vs-intersection semantics** across the discovered instance workloads — a key present in `mc-0` but missing in `mc-1` is precisely the drift to catch, so Checks 1 & 3 want **all-instances (intersection of presence)** not any-instance, while Check 2 (`configMapKeyRef` → configmap key exists) is per-workload; (c) covers **MH identically** (same per-instance topology). Add multi-workload test fixtures (a service dir with two `-N-deployment.yaml` files, one missing a key → must FAIL). Consider hardening the no-workload skip to a hard FAIL (not WARN) when the dir contains `*-deployment.yaml`/`*-statefulset.yaml` but no recognized workload, so a future naming scheme can't silently reopen the hole. Non-trivial (>10 LoC + design decision on semantics + new test surface + touches two services) → correctly outside task #6's changeset. Cross-ref: §Polyglot Pipeline Follow-ups (dt-guard / ADR-0034 guard pipeline is the same binary). **Schedule as a focused infrastructure dt-guard devloop.** Owner: infrastructure (dt-guard maintainer). Provenance: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-07-05-mc-otel-wiring-task6/main.md`.

- [x] **CLOSED 2026-08-15 — `grep`: flags placed after a `--` end-of-options terminator are parsed as operands** (surfaced 2026-08-15 during story task #3 / R-7; **entry substantially corrected on the same day — see below**). The durable one-liner: **when you pass `--` to grep, every later argument is an operand — put option flags before the `--`, and reserve `--` for when the pattern may itself begin with `-`.** Measured on a synthetic tree with `/usr/bin/grep`:

  ```
  grep -rn -F     NEEDLE /tmp/pt --exclude-dir=docs   -> exclusion honored,  rc 0
  grep -rn -F --  NEEDLE /tmp/pt --exclude-dir=docs   -> exclusion IGNORED,  rc 2
  grep -rn --exclude-dir=docs -F -- NEEDLE /tmp/pt    -> exclusion honored,  rc 0
  ```

  **Sweep DONE, result: zero other sites. This is closed, not open.** Every `--exclude*`/`--include*` on a `grep` invocation in the repo was the one in `scripts/guards/simple/validate-subdomain-regex-sync.sh`, fixed in that same commit. The only other `--exclude` hits are `git ls-files --exclude-standard`, which is a git flag and unrelated.

  **PREMISE CORRECTED 2026-08-15 (@operations), same day, before anyone acted on it.** This entry originally claimed: (1) "`grep` in the devloop container is `ugrep` 7.5.0"; (2) "ugrep does NOT permute options after operands", unlike GNU grep; (3) the failure is silent "with no warning and exit status unchanged"; (4) the blast radius is "every post-operand `--exclude*`/`--include*` in the repo", marked **NOT SWEPT** with an instruction to treat prior clean checks as unverified, owned by `infrastructure`. **All four were wrong**, and the root cause of the error is worth more than the finding was:

  - `/usr/bin/grep` is **GNU grep 3.8**, and that is what every guard, every layer script and CI actually execute. `grep --version` reports ugrep **only inside a Claude Code agent's own Bash-tool shell**, where the harness defines `grep` as a shell *function* (`type grep` shows it) that shims the `claude` binary. That function is not exported and does not exist under `bash script.sh`. The original measurement was taken in the agent shell and attributed to the container.
  - **Both greps permute options after operands identically.** The real mechanism is the `--` terminator, which happened to be present in the reproduction command.
  - It is **not silent**: both emit `grep: --exclude-dir=docs: No such file or directory` and exit **2**.
  - The sweep is one command; its answer is complete; ownership was never `infrastructure`'s (it is shell idiom in the validation pipeline) and there is nothing left to own.

  Corrected in place rather than deleted, per this story's own precedent for corrected premises: the original text asserted a **fail-open defect in validation tooling** with an owner and an explicit "prior clean checks are unverified" instruction, so deleting it would leave the claim citable while removing the correction. **Generalisable lesson, and the reason this is kept: a measurement of the *environment* taken from inside an agent's tool shell is not a measurement of the environment scripts run in.** Verify with `bash -c` or a script file, not the interactive tool shell. Provenance: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-15-layer7-per-run-org/main.md` §Issues Encountered #2.

- [ ] **`layer7.sh`'s cluster-teardown path has no busy-tolerance while its setup path does — a benign helper collision reports as `cluster-rebuild-failed`** (surfaced 2026-08-15 during story R-7 task #3 Gate 2; deferred by Lead ruling with justification, not silently). **(a) The asymmetry**: `__dev_cluster_setup()` in `scripts/layer7.sh` IS busy-tolerant — it captures the client's output, greps it for `(busy)` / `in-flight write` / `another write`, calls `__poll_setup_idle` and retries once. The `"$DEV_CLUSTER" teardown` call in the `infra/kind/` rebuild branch of the same file is **bare**: `|| precondition_fail cluster-rebuild-failed`, with no busy detection and no retry. The retry helper already exists, in the same file, a few dozen lines away. **(b) The consequence**: a collision that the helper's write mutex handled correctly becomes `PRECONDITION_FAILURE / cluster-rebuild-failed`, whose runbook row tells the operator "the cluster may be in a partially-torn-down state" and sends them to `/tmp/devloop/helper.log`. The cluster is fine; the other writer simply got there first. That is R-4's misattribution class — an environment condition reported as a specific fault requiring inspection — inside the pipeline built to prevent it. **(c) Reachability is MEASURED, not theoretical** (so the next person need not re-derive it): `/tmp/devloop/helper.log` recorded `{"cmd":"rejected_busy","args":["rejected=teardown","in_flight=teardown"],"exit_code":-1,"outcome":"rejected"}` — two `teardown` verbs **500ms apart**, from two `layer-all.sh` runs against one helper. Every line in that run's `layer-7.stderr.log` was duplicated, which is the fingerprint to look for. Layer 7 died at 3 seconds, in Phase 1e, so Phase 1h never ran. Trigger condition: **two devloops sharing one helper**, which is the normal state of a container running an Agent-Teams loop. **(d) Premise correction, recorded per this story's convention**: the Gate-1 ruling that stood down busy-tolerance work said *"with no socket call there is no write slot to contend, so generalizing `__dev_cluster_setup` would churn working code for nothing."* That is true of **Phase 1h**, which makes no socket call, and **false of the teardown path**, which makes one and was observed contending. The ruling's conclusion still holds for task #3 (the teardown line is byte-identical to its pre-task version, so this is new work on a pre-existing path, not "fix, don't defer" on the task's own diff); its reasoning does not extend here. Corrected in place so the original sentence is not later citable as evidence that the whole class was assessed and dismissed. **Fix shape**: reuse the existing busy-detect + `__poll_setup_idle` + retry-once logic for the teardown call — either by extracting the shared shape or by calling it directly. Behaviour change to the operator-lane boundary, so it wants @operations' design input rather than a drive-by. **(e) Owner: operations.** Provenance: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-15-layer7-per-run-org/main.md` §Accepted Deferrals.

- [ ] **The repo's only `bash -n` sweep is wired into nothing — it runs only when invoked by hand** (surfaced 2026-08-15 during story R-7 task #3). `scripts/lang/_get_base_ref.behavior-equivalence.test.sh` case 7a syntax-checks every `*.sh` under `scripts/` and `infra/` — **117 files** after task #3 widened it from `guards/simple` + `common.sh` (~30). It is the guard for the exact defect class that task #3 hit: a stray `fi` made `scripts/layer7.sh` entirely unparseable, and an unparseable layer script exits from the shell itself with **no STATUS line, no REASON and no lane**, so `layer-all` records `UNKNOWN`. That defect was caught incidentally, by a self-test that happens to execute the script, rather than by design. **The problem**: grepping `scripts/`, `.github/` and the whole repo finds **zero** references to this test file outside itself. It is not in `layer3.sh`'s `run_and_emit` list, not in any workflow, not in `run-guards.sh`'s discovery glob (it is not under `guards/simple/`). So the sweep exists and does not run. **Why it was not simply wired in**: the file currently has **2 pre-existing failures** (`ci-push-guardrail-skip` and one other), verified independently — by @test and by task #3's implementer — to be identical with and without task #3's edit. Wiring it into Layer 3 as-is would red the gate on unrelated debt, which trains people to ignore Layer 3 — "fail loudly, never mask" violated in the other direction. **Fix shape**: fix or quarantine the 2 failing cases, then add one `run_and_emit` line to `scripts/layer3.sh`. Consider whether the 7a sweep deserves to be its own always-run check rather than a case inside a base-ref equivalence test, since its subject matter is unrelated to that file's stated purpose and its value is much broader. Owner: operations. Provenance: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-15-layer7-per-run-org/main.md` §Accepted Deferrals.

## Developer Experience

- [ ] **[ADR-0035 · Group 3 — live-read hazard; run last, then re-run the substrate probe]** **Make headless the only described mode in `/devloop`**: once the run-story experiment (Run #1: `browser-client-join` pending tasks; Run #2: `create-meeting` end-to-end) validates the headless runner, rewrite `.claude/skills/devloop/SKILL.md` so Headless Mode is not a section layered on top of an interactive default but the *only* mode of operation — every escalate-to-user path (planning timeout options, validation-attempts prompt, disambiguation ask, Gate 3 override question) becomes the escalation-file contract unconditionally, and the interactive-session assumptions (user answering mid-run, "which would you prefer?" blocks) are deleted rather than overridden. Rationale: two described modes with override rules is exactly the mirrored-prose drift risk the repo's SSoT convention warns about; after the runner is the normal execution path, interactive prose is dead weight that a Lead can still wander into. Interactive use remains possible (a human can always run `/devloop` in a session and react to the escalation file themselves) — it just stops being a separately documented protocol. Do NOT do this before the experiment concludes; the current two-mode state is deliberate during transition. Owner: operations. Surfaced 2026-08-01 while drafting the run-story deliverables.

- [ ] **[next-up, infrastructure] Fix stale `scripts/dev/iterate.sh` path in setup.sh output.** `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:780,905` and `infra/kind/scripts/iterate.sh:8` reference `./scripts/dev/iterate.sh`, which does not exist; the script is at `infra/kind/scripts/iterate.sh` (correct form already documented in `scripts/README.md:26`). `:905` is inside the post-setup access-info block, so a first-time developer's **first instruction is an unresolvable path**. Spun out of task #20 to avoid growing a diff already under verdict. Owner: infrastructure. **Not backlog.**

  Blast radius verified and bounded by @infrastructure, independently re-checked at task #20: **exactly 3 actionable lines across 2 files**, the file confirmed absent at `scripts/dev/` and present at `infra/kind/scripts/`, and the target path is not an inference — `scripts/README.md:26` already documents the correct form. So this is landable without re-deriving anything.

  **One site that must NOT be swept**: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-01-13-env-tests-security.md:200` also contains the stale path. That is a historical devloop record, not prescriptive prose — under the artifact-kind rule applied throughout task #20, records keep their original text. A naive `grep -rl | xargs sed` would rewrite history; scope the fix to the two live files.

- [ ] **Resumable setup.sh**: Add a `--resume` flag to `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh` that brings the cluster up to date without destroying it. Skip cluster creation if cluster exists, skip namespace creation if namespaces exist, skip image build+load if image tag unchanged, let `kubectl apply -k` handle idempotent infra updates. Currently any infra change requires a full teardown+rebuild (~5 min), when most steps could be skipped.
- [x] **Pre-load third-party images into Kind**: Third-party images (postgres, redis, prometheus, grafana, loki, promtail, kube-state-metrics, node-exporter) are pulled from the internet by the Kind node on every cluster creation. Pull to host Docker/Podman cache first, then `kind load` into the cluster. Makes subsequent cluster recreations faster and offline-capable. Location: `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:preload_third_party_images()`.
- [ ] **Skip unchanged service image builds**: `build_image` runs `docker build` for all 4 services on every `setup.sh` invocation, even when source code hasn't changed. The `COPY . .` planner stage uses the entire project root as build context, so changing any file invalidates all services' Docker caches. Two improvements: (a) add `.dockerignore` or narrow build context per service so changing GC doesn't invalidate AC's cache, (b) content-hash the relevant source files (`crates/{service}/`, `crates/common/`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`) and skip `build_image` + `kind load` entirely if the hash matches the previously-built image. Location: `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:build_image()`.

- [ ] **Rotate `helper.log` for the devloop helper**: `crates/devloop-helper/src/logging.rs` appends one JSONL entry per request to `/tmp/devloop-{slug}/helper.log` for the helper's lifetime with no rotation, truncation, or size cap. A long-lived devloop session that does many `status` polls (cheap, frequent — the new concurrency model encourages this) plus a few `setup`/`rebuild` cycles can grow the log to tens of MB before the next teardown. The log is the only post-hoc record of the new `outcome`/`busy`/`cancel` audit shapes (ADR-0030 §"Audit logging"), so straight truncation is wrong. Likely fix: rotate at a size threshold (e.g. 10 MB → `helper.log.1`) keeping one rolled file, or cap retention to last N entries by reading-then-rewriting on startup. Surfaced 2026-05-03 during the helper concurrency rework; not blocking because `/tmp/devloop-{slug}/` is wiped on teardown so disk pressure is bounded by session length.
- [ ] **Slim devloop image: drop ffmpeg only (NOT headless-shell)**: `npx playwright install chromium` pulls three blobs into `/opt/ms-playwright/`: `chromium-<rev>/` (~280 MB, used), `chromium_headless_shell-<rev>/` (~280 MB — **LOAD-BEARING, do not remove**), and `ffmpeg-<rev>/` (~50 MB, unused — only video-recording tests need it). **Correction (2026-07-07):** the original entry claimed the headless-shell was "unused — only some Playwright headless modes consume it." That is FALSE. Vitest 4 Browser Mode (the R-43 `test:component` tier in packages/sdk-svelte + web-app) launches the headless shell directly — the failing path is literally `.../chromium_headless_shell-<rev>/chrome-linux/headless_shell`. Removing it would break every client component test even with the version pin correct. Only `ffmpeg-*` is safe to strip. Revised action: `RUN rm -rf /opt/ms-playwright/ffmpeg-*` after the `playwright install chromium` step (recovers ~50 MB, not ~330 MB); document in the Dockerfile comment that ffmpeg is stripped intentionally and headless-shell is kept. Locations: `infra/devloop/Dockerfile` pre-cache step. Surfaced 2026-05-03 during R-35 image validation; headless-shell premise corrected 2026-07-07 after it caused the 1.49.0/1.55.1 skew debugging.

- [x] **Bump R-35 devloop-image playwright pin to 1.55.1 (Chromium browser build 1193)** — *resolved 2026-07-07 (image rebaked)*: task #15 (`2026-07-05-task15-sdk-svelte-web-app`) pinned `playwright` to **1.55.1** in `packages/sdk-svelte` + `packages/web-app` (clears **GHSA-7mvr-c777-76hp**), but the `infra/devloop/Dockerfile` `ARG PLAYWRIGHT_VERSION` was left at **1.49.0** (build 1148) — the ARG edit is in-tree and should have shipped in commit `16fb5e6` alongside the package bump; it was deferred to "the next image bake" (a passive trigger that never fired), so every client-touching devloop since either hit `Executable doesn't exist … chromium_headless_shell-1193` or relied on a manual `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/home/dev/.cache/ms-playwright` override. **Resolved:** ARG bumped 1.49.0 → 1.55.1 and the devloop image was rebaked with Chromium build 1193 (2026-07-07). Story tasks #4 and #5 both independently re-confirmed the drift as the root cause during their (pre-rebake) Gate-2 runs. `ci-client.yml` was never affected (its `npx playwright install` is keyed on `pnpm-lock.yaml`). Lesson: an in-tree source edit must not be parked behind a host-side deployment step. Owner: operations + infrastructure.

- [ ] **Guard: devloop-image Playwright pin must match the package pin** (drift forcing-function for the entry above): the 1.49.0/1.55.1 skew survived for weeks because *nothing failed validation when the two drifted* — the fix sat behind a passive "next image bake" trigger. Add a guard asserting `ARG PLAYWRIGHT_VERSION` in `infra/devloop/Dockerfile` equals the `playwright` devDep pin in `packages/sdk-svelte/package.json` and `packages/web-app/package.json` (all three must agree; lockfile authoritative). On mismatch → `STATUS=FAIL` naming both versions, so the *edit* is forced in the same commit as the package bump instead of deferred. Per ADR-0034 §3 this is a `dt-guard` subcommand (new match-arm in `crates/dt-guard/src/main.rs` + typed struct + `check()` + `*.test.sh` self-test + ~7-LoC wrapper under `scripts/guards/simple/`) — genuinely task-sized (Rust + test + wiring), which is why it is tracked rather than done inline. NOTE the caught mismatch would have been at *build/validation* time, not test-run time — that is the whole point. Surfaced 2026-07-07 while debugging the skew. Owner: infrastructure + code-reviewer.

- [ ] **Guard: Node pin must agree across `.nvmrc`, the devloop-image Node pin, the lockfile engines floor, and root `package.json engines.node`** (drift forcing-function; motivated by the 2026-08-05 host dev-env failure — the `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-06-toolchain-pin-cleanup` cleanup that added runbook F11): that failure was a THREE-way pin drift — `.nvmrc` pinned `22.11.0`, the devloop image installed a floating "latest 22.x", and the vite 8 / rolldown 1.2.1 bump raised the workspace lockfile engines floor to `>=22.12.0` — and *nothing failed validation while the pins drifted* because pnpm SILENTLY skips the engines-mismatched optional native binding (`@rolldown/binding-linux-x64-gnu`); vite crashed only at launch with "cannot find native binding". The cleanup reconciled the pins by hand (`.nvmrc`→`22.23.1`, Dockerfile `ARG NODE_VERSION` derived from `.nvmrc` and plumbed by `devloop.sh`, root `engines.node`→`>=22.13.0 <23`) and added `.npmrc engine-strict=true`; this guard is the forcing-function so they cannot silently drift apart again. Add a `dt-guard` subcommand asserting a **four-member agreement set** across **two distinct invariants** (the two invariants are NOT the same test — do not collapse them to one "all equal" check):
  - **(a) exact equality:** the version in `.nvmrc` == the Dockerfile Node pin (`ARG NODE_VERSION` in `infra/devloop/Dockerfile`, the value `devloop.sh` passes as `--build-arg`).
  - **(b) floor-match:** the **lockfile engines floor** == root `package.json engines.node` lower bound; AND `.nvmrc` satisfies (`>=`) that floor. Define "lockfile engines floor" precisely as the **max lower-bound (on the active major line) across packages that ACTUALLY install on the target platform** — i.e. apply pnpm's own os/cpu/libc optional-skip filter, or the guard will mis-derive the floor. Today the true max is `22.13.0` (from `eslint@10.8.0`, non-optional), NOT rolldown's `22.12.0`, and NOT the optional WASM fallbacks' `22.13.0` (`@rolldown/binding-wasm32-wasi`, `@napi-rs/wasm-runtime` — os/cpu-skipped on linux-x64-gnu).
  On mismatch → `STATUS=FAIL` naming every disagreeing member with its value, so the edit is forced in the same commit as whichever pin moved. **Out of the (a) equality set — named so a future author neither forgets it nor mis-flags it as drift:** CI's `.github/workflows/*.yml` `setup-node` `node-version: '22'` is a DELIBERATELY-unpinned floating major, safe-by-construction (always resolves ≥ floor). Suggested clean resolution that deletes the site entirely: migrate CI to `node-version-file: .nvmrc` (derives from the SSoT). Per ADR-0034 §3 this is a `dt-guard` subcommand (new match-arm in `crates/dt-guard/src/main.rs` + typed struct + `check()` + `*.test.sh` self-test + ~7-LoC wrapper under `scripts/guards/simple/`) — genuinely task-sized (Rust + test + wiring), which is why it is tracked rather than done inline. NOTE the caught mismatch would be at *build/validation* time, not vite-launch time — that is the whole point, exactly as the Playwright-pin guard above. Surfaced 2026-08-06 by the toolchain-pin-cleanup (run-story task #61). Owner: infrastructure + code-reviewer.
- [ ] **Real `podman image inspect` byte-delta for ADR-0034 Wave 3 `python3-yaml` removal**: The 2026-05-19 Wave 1 dt-guard devloop removed `python3-yaml` from `infra/devloop/Dockerfile` apt list (the YAML parsing migrated to `serde_norway` inside `dt-guard`). The 493 KB apt-cache estimate was derived from `apt-cache show python3-yaml` size + a default `Installed-Size` ratio; the actual on-image byte delta is unmeasured because the devloop ran inside the existing image (the next image bake happens host-side, not inside the devloop). Owner: operations. **Action**: at the next `infra/devloop` image bake, capture `podman image inspect localhost/devloop:<old-tag>` and `localhost/devloop:<new-tag>` and record the `Size` delta in this entry as the falsifiable byte-level measurement. Defer trigger: image bake happens (host-side; not blocking on the devloop tree). Surfaced 2026-05-19 by ADR-0034 Wave 1 Gate-3 operations verdict.
- [x] **`path_matches_glob` should auto-expand bare directory paths**: `scripts/guards/common.sh:454` `path_matches_glob()` handles `prefix/**` and literals but treats a bare trailing-slash directory path (e.g. `fixtures/alert-rules/`) as literal-or-extglob, so it does not match files inside that directory. Plan authors writing Layer-A diff entries naturally write `fixtures/<name>/` for "this whole directory" and discover the gap only when Gate 2 fails, then have to rewrite the row to `fixtures/<name>/**`. Fix: in `path_matches_glob`, if `glob` ends in `/` (and isn't already `/**`), treat it as `glob**` before falling through to the existing trailing-`/**` branch. Single-line change; both consumers (`validate-cross-boundary-classification.sh`, `validate-cross-boundary-scope.sh`) get the ergonomics improvement. Surfaced 2026-05-08 during the guard self-test cleanup devloop when implementer hit it on three plan rows for fixture-tree deletions and explicitly out-of-scoped the helper change. (resolved 2026-05-08 via devloop-outputs/2026-05-08-glob-trailing-slash-and-template-wording)

## Devloop Container Resource Hygiene & Build Isolation

Surfaced 2026-06-29 during task #10's (GC telemetry env-test backfill) Gate 2: the
host-side cluster build exhausted disk mid-`COPY` ("no space left on device"), and
`podman system prune -af` reclaimed **~2 TB**. Investigation found three distinct,
operations/infrastructure-owned problems. None are env-test scope; captured here as
the operations brief. Related: see "Skip unchanged service image builds" under
§Developer Experience (the `.dockerignore` / build-context-narrowing half).

- [ ] **(B — CORRECTNESS, highest priority) Concurrent same-host devloops share `localhost/{ac,gc,mc,mh}-service:latest` → wrong-code-in-cluster race.** Every other per-devloop resource is namespaced by slug — clone (`devloop.sh:79`), dev/db containers (`:80-81`), network (`:132`), helper dir (`:95`), Kind cluster `devloop-${TASK_SLUG}`, ports — but the image build/load layer is **global**: `build_image localhost/gc-service:latest` (`infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:531/552/590/655`), the manifests hard-pin that tag (`infra/services/*/deployment.yaml:34` etc.), and `load_image_to_kind` does `podman save localhost/gc-service:latest` → `kind load --name $CLUSTER_NAME` (`setup.sh:160-168`), saving whatever the **host** `:latest` points to at that instant. There is **no lock/serialization** (`grep flock|lock|mutex` → 0 hits). Race with two slugs A,B building concurrently: (1) A builds → host `:latest`=A; (2) B builds → host `:latest`=B and B's `build_image` `rmi`s A's image id (`setup.sh:289-290`); (3) A's `podman save` now saves **B's binary** and loads it into **A's** cluster → A silently runs B's code. Masked today only because (i) once `kind load`ed each cluster has its own copy (race window = build→save→load) and (ii) devloops may not actually overlap on one host — but "runs the wrong code when they do" is a latent data-corruption bug, not a disk issue. **Fix:** per-slug image tags `localhost/gc-service:${TASK_SLUG}` across `setup.sh` build+load + a kustomize `images:` transformer in `overlays/kind/` to rewrite `:latest`→`:${slug}` at deploy (manifests hard-pin `:latest`). This also makes cleanup reclaim precise (next item). Likely warrants a short ADR (changes the build-isolation contract). Owner: operations + infrastructure.

- [ ] **(A — the ~2 TB leak) Service images + build cache are never garbage-collected.** The only GC in the flow is `build_image` removing the *previous same-tag* image (`setup.sh:289-290`; twin at `devloop.sh:59-62/419-422`) — it never touches build cache, and a *failed* build (the `rmi` runs only after success) leaves partial layers + cache with zero cleanup (we saw 2 failed setups in `helper.log` 02:44/02:52). `cleanup()` (`devloop.sh:126-167`) tears down containers + network + Kind cluster + clone but **not** the host service images or `COPY`-context build cache — the one resource class it builds but never reclaims. With `COPY . .` tarring the 27 GB `target/` per build × 4 services × every rebuild × every slug, that's the ~2 TB. **Fix (the natural home is `cleanup()`, per the build-isolation fix above):** once tags are per-slug (item B), add after the Kind delete (~`devloop.sh:153`) `podman rmi localhost/{ac,gc,mc,mh}-service:${TASK_SLUG}` (precise, complete, zero cross-slug risk) + `podman builder prune -f` (unused cache). Before item B lands, only dangling/unused prune (`-f`, never `-af`) is cross-slug-safe — which is *why* item B should land first. Owner: operations + infrastructure. **Partially landed 2026-06-30 (envtest-infra-reliability devloop):** the cross-slug-safe half is now in `cleanup()` (`devloop.sh` — `podman image prune -f` + `podman builder prune -f` after the Kind delete). The precise per-slug `rmi` remains blocked on item B's per-slug tags.

- [ ] **(C — resilience) No pre-build disk guard.** Nothing checks free space before a rebuild (`grep disk|no space|df` in setup.sh/layer7.sh → 0 hits), so it runs straight into "no space left on device" mid-`COPY` — the worst failure mode (partial debris + operator-lane stall). `docs/runbooks/devloop-validation.md:192` already names "disk-space" as an anticipated `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` precondition check; it was never built. **Fix:** a Phase-1 disk-space precondition in `layer7.sh` (or `setup.sh`) emitting `PRECONDITION_FAILURE REASON=insufficient-disk` below a threshold, ideally triggering the item-A prune before failing. Owner: operations. **Landed 2026-06-30 (envtest-infra-reliability devloop):** `check_build_disk_space` (`infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh`, run-once at the top of `build_image`) measures podman's graphroot and emits the `REASON=insufficient-disk` banner + `exit 2` below `DEVLOOP_MIN_DISK_GB` (default 15). Self-tested by `scripts/setup.test.sh` (wired in `layer3.sh`); wired into the runbook §6.7 sub-cause rows + §4. **Remaining gap → see (C-followup) below.**

- [ ] **(C-followup — per-run rebuild build path is unguarded) The disk guard fires ONLY on the cold `dev-cluster setup` path.** `layer7.sh` runs `dev-cluster rebuild-all` on *every* devloop (Phase 1c, `layer7.sh:340`), and `rebuild-all` → `cmd_rebuild` (`crates/devloop-helper/src/commands.rs:1084`) builds via a **direct `podman build`** that does NOT route through `setup.sh`/`build_image`, so `check_build_disk_space` never runs on the steady-state per-run rebuild. That path is currently protected only by the `.dockerignore` keystone (tiny context — removes the 27 GB×4 blow-up) + the `cleanup()` prune. **Fix:** add a host-side disk precondition inside `cmd_rebuild` before its `podman build` (Rust). Bundle with item B (both edit `cmd_rebuild`), or track standalone. Owner: operations + infrastructure.

- [x] **(D — RESOLVED 2026-06-30) `layer7.sh` Phase-1f Prometheus gate failed in-run — IFS word-split bug.** Root cause (found via a concurrent in-container probe-loop + instrumented run): `layer7.sh:35` sets `IFS=$'\n\t'` (no space); `__wait_http_ready` ran `until $HTTP_PROBE "$url"` with UNQUOTED `$HTTP_PROBE` ("curl -fsS …") → under that IFS it does not word-split → the whole string is one command name → **exit 127** every probe → 300s timeout → phantom `observability-prometheus-not-ready` against a healthy Prometheus. Proven: concurrent identical `curl` returned 200 (93/93) during the failing window; instrumented `verbose-curl rc=0` vs `$HTTP_PROBE rc=127`. Latent because `layer7.test.sh` injected a single-token fake probe and the real-curl path never ran live until item C's `.dockerignore`. **Fix:** array-split in `__wait_http_ready` (`local -a probe; IFS=' ' read -r -a probe <<<"$HTTP_PROBE"; until "${probe[@]}" "$url"`, mirrors `layer7.sh:409`) + a `layer7.test.sh` regression case (multi-word probe under strict IFS). Proven: Layer 7 flipped from `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` to reaching the suite.
- [x] **(D.1 — RESOLVED 2026-06-30, surfaced once Phase-1f was fixed) GC telemetry forward → 502: GC egress NetworkPolicy missing the otel-collector allow.** With Phase-1f fixed, `31_gc_telemetry` ran live for the first time → 6/11 failed with 502 (GC→collector forward failed). The collector INGRESS netpol admits `gc-service:4318`, but `infra/services/gc-service/network-policy.yaml` egress had AC/MC/Postgres/DNS and **no `→ otel-collector:4318` rule** — task #10 added the forward + collector-ingress allow but missed the symmetric GC-egress allow (zero-trust needs both). **Fix:** added the egress rule (ns `dark-tower`, `app: otel-collector`, TCP 4318). Proven: **11/11** `31_gc_telemetry` tests pass live. Owner: infrastructure (task #10 deliverable completion).
- [ ] ~~(D — original investigation, superseded by the two RESOLVED entries above; retained for provenance)~~ Surfaced 2026-06-30 once the `.dockerignore` keystone (item C / the envtest-infra-reliability devloop) finally let the cluster rebuild SUCCEED and reach Phase-1f for the first time against a real cluster — `layer7.sh`'s real-Prometheus poll path had never been exercised (`layer7.test.sh` uses fake `dev-cluster` stubs; prior disk failures always died before Phase-1f). Symptom: `STATUS=PRECONDITION_FAILURE REASON=observability-prometheus-not-ready` on EVERY run (≥3 across the telemetry + infra devloops), **even with `DEVLOOP_HEALTH_BUDGET=900`** (15 min) — so it is NOT a warmup-timeout. Yet Prometheus is stably `/-/ready`→200 from inside the devloop container *right after* the run, the poll command is correct (`curl -fsS -o /dev/null --max-time 5 http://host.containers.internal:20300/-/ready`, `layer7.sh:60/194`), and `ports.json.container_urls.prometheus` is correct (`http://host.containers.internal:20300`). `cmd_rebuild_all` does NOT restart Prometheus (only the 4 services), so it isn't rebuild-disruption. **Root cause UNKNOWN — needs in-run host observation unavailable from the devloop sandbox:** during/just-after a `dev-cluster setup` (before teardown), capture (a) `kubectl get pods -A | grep prom` + the prometheus pod `describe`/readiness-probe status, and (b) from INSIDE the devloop container at that moment `curl -fsS -o /dev/null --max-time 5 http://host.containers.internal:20300/-/ready; echo $?`. That distinguishes: Prometheus genuinely not-ready in-run / container→NodePort route not wired yet at poll time / a `__wait_http_ready` interaction. This blocks the paused 2026-06-29 GC-telemetry devloop's Gate 2 (its suite is gated by the same Phase-1f) AND any future cluster-dependent devloop. Owner: operations + infrastructure. (Deliberately NOT fixed in the 2026-06-30 envtest-infra-reliability devloop — that devloop's proven disk fixes were committed via a documented Gate-2 bypass; this is the next investigation.)

## Multi-Cluster Networking (Production)

- [ ] **Per-pod externally-routable addresses for MC/MH**: MC and MH register per-pod advertise addresses with GC (`_ADVERTISE_ADDRESS` config fields, added in `8266acc`). In Kind dev, these use pod IPs via downward API, which works single-cluster. In production, GC/MC/MH may be in different clusters and clients connect directly to MC/MH — pod IPs won't be routable. Needs a `/debate` to choose an infrastructure pattern (headless service + ExternalDNS, per-pod ingress, service mesh, etc.) and design TLS/DNS strategy. Affects: deployment model (StatefulSet vs Deployment), DNS, TLS certificates (wildcard vs per-pod), GC routing logic, client SDK connection. Depends on cloud provider selection. Locations: `crates/mc-service/src/config.rs` (grpc/webtransport_advertise_address), `crates/mh-service/src/config.rs` (same), `infra/services/mc-service/deployment.yaml`, `infra/services/mh-service/deployment.yaml`.

- [ ] **Web-serving tier has no local cluster representation (production-parity gap)**: `setup.sh` faithfully mirrors production for AC/GC/MC/MH (K8s services, so a port to AWS/GCP "just works" via the common K8s layer), but the web app is served ONLY by the host-side Vite dev server (`dev-web.sh`) — there is no ingress and no web-app deploy manifest anywhere in `infra/`. Host-Vite is correct for dev iteration (HMR, the `__E2E_HOOKS__` E2E bus, on-the-fly TS/Svelte compile) and should stay, but it means the production web-serving path — static-bundle serving, ingress/subdomain routing, and critically the COOP/COEP/CSP/Permissions-Policy headers + NetworkPolicy — is never exercised locally and will be built + debugged fresh at production-deploy time (the reinvent-the-wheel risk). **Load-bearing, not cosmetic**: COOP/COEP (cross-origin isolation) is REQUIRED for `SharedArrayBuffer`/WASM threads, which the deferred client features (MLS WASM E2EE, WebCodecs media pipeline) depend on — those features cannot be exercised locally at all without this tier, so the parity gap compounds with the feature gap. Needs a `/debate` to choose the production web-serving architecture: **(a) K8s static-serving pod** (nginx/similar serving the `vite build` bundle behind the same ingress as the API) — fully mirrorable in Kind, the setup.sh-mirrors-prod principle holds; vs **(b) object-store + CDN** (S3+CloudFront / GCS+Cloud CDN) — inherently OUTSIDE the K8s-parity model (Kind can't mirror CloudFront), though a local nginx-static pod still exercises the headers/routing behavior. The choice determines how faithfully `setup.sh` can mirror the web tier. Either way the resolution shape is a **cluster-resident static-serving pod behind the ingress with production headers**, ALONGSIDE (not replacing) `dev-web.sh`. Peer to the MC/MH production-routing item above — same "depends on cloud provider selection" character. Currently only recorded as an out-of-scope line in the browser-client-join story ("Production CDN serving, COOP/COEP/CSP/Permissions-Policy headers, NetworkPolicy productionization" — deferred), which loses it as a tracked parity concern. Surfaced 2026-08-08 during manual-test setup. Owner: infrastructure + client. Depends on cloud provider selection.

## Supply Chain

- [ ] **Pre-existing cargo audit findings on `wtransport` / `rustls-pemfile` dependency tree** (surfaced 2026-05-03 during devloop `2026-05-03-bootstrap-pnpm-nx-workspace` Gate 2 audit; confirmed unchanged from start commit `11942361`, NOT introduced by that devloop). `cargo audit` reports 6 vulnerabilities + 3 warnings. The headline finding is **RUSTSEC-2025-0134 — `rustls-pemfile 2.2.0` unmaintained** (dated 2025-11-28); transitive via `wtransport 0.1.14` and `rustls-native-certs 0.7.3`. Five additional findings on the same dependency tree. Resolution paths to consider: (a) wait for upstream `wtransport` to bump past `rustls-pemfile`; (b) pin `rustls-pemfile` via workspace `[patch]`; (c) evaluate replacement WebTransport crate. Touches Guarded Shared Area (`crates/common/src/webtransport/**`) so any swap routes to a security + protocol + MC + MH co-signed devloop. Owner: security + media-handler + meeting-controller. Document context: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-03-bootstrap-pnpm-nx-workspace/main.md` § Devloop Verification Steps Layer 6.
  - **RUSTSEC-2026-0204 — `crossbeam-epoch 0.9.18` invalid-pointer-dereference** (advisory published 2026-07-06; surfaced 2026-07-07 at task #6 Gate-3 Layer-6 by @team-lead — pre-existing, fails on `main` too, unrelated to task #6). Transitive via `metrics-exporter-prometheus` → `rayon` → `crossbeam-epoch`; low-severity deref on a fmt/collector path our code never triggers. **Decision (operations + @team-lead + security, 2026-07-07 — self-contained close, no follow-up):** (a) 2026-0204 auto-resolves to the published fix `crossbeam-epoch 0.9.20` on a fresh `cargo` resolve — the immediate lockfile refresh already flipped Layer 6 back to `cargo-audit-passed`; (b) it is **not suppressed**, so TWO standing nets cover it — the live Layer-6 `cargo audit` gate (fails a devloop's Gate 2/3; it is what caught this) AND the weekly force-run `.github/workflows/audit-scheduled.yml` (`DEVLOOP_AUDIT_FORCE_RUN=1`, Mon 06:00 UTC, task #47 / ADR-0033 §3), which opens/updates a rolling `audit-drift` GitHub issue and auto-closes it on a clean run. That scheduled run is the **diff-less drift-catcher** that resolves the systemic @security concern (a fresh advisory against an *unchanged* dep set would otherwise slip past the dep-change-gated per-devloop audit), so no new follow-up is needed; (c) **no version pin** — `Cargo.lock` is gitignored, and a workspace `[patch]`/minimum-version floor would rot (standing maintenance surface for a deref our workload never reaches). Owner: operations + security. Priority: P3 (low severity; not in our reachable path; double-audit-gated).

- [x] **Pre-existing unsuppressed `pnpm audit` reds — Layer 6 is currently FAILING on `main`** (surfaced 2026-07-30 at task #58 Gate 2; **RESOLVED 2026-08-08 via devloop-outputs/2026-08-08-clear-ts-audit-advisories, task #66**). The advisory set was cleared by real transitive fixes (the `pnpm.overrides` block + intervening dependency bumps), with the final `nanoid` high advisory closed by a bounded `"nanoid": ">=3.3.17 <4"` override — no suppression. `DEVLOOP_AUDIT_FORCE_RUN=1 scripts/lang/ts/audit.sh` now emits `STATUS=OK`. The full resolution narrative (including the nanoid detail and verification evidence) lives in the canonical §Dependency Vulnerabilities (pnpm audit) entry above — not duplicated here. The TRIGGER below is satisfied: task #66 worked this condition rather than proceeding on "#58 already did." Original entry retained for the audit trail.

- [ ] ~~**Pre-existing unsuppressed `pnpm audit` reds — Layer 6 is currently FAILING on `main`** (surfaced 2026-07-30 at task #58 Gate 2).~~ `STATUS=FAIL REASON=pnpm-audit-failed`: **23 advisories — 1 critical, 12 high** — above the high/critical gate, and none of them covered by the three entries in §Suppressed Advisories. **Evidence it is pre-existing, not task #58's**: the entire #58 changeset was `git stash`ed and `pnpm audit` re-run against the resulting base tree — identical advisory set. #58 adds no dependencies (one Rust module, one 4-line shell wrapper, TypeScript source edits). Recorded here rather than left in a devloop message because otherwise the next loop inherits *"Layer 6 was already red"* as precedent with nobody owning it — which is how a masked failure becomes permanent (CLAUDE.md §Fail loudly). **This entry is a pointer, NOT an adjudication**: each advisory still needs the ADR-0033 §11 reviewed-PR path — fix, or suppress in `audit-suppressions.toml` with reason + expiry + ticket. One known member of the set: `fast-uri` ≥3.0.0 <3.1.3 (GHSA-4c8g-83qw-93j6), transitive via `vite-plugin-dts` → `@microsoft/api-extractor` → `@microsoft/tsdoc-config` → `ajv`, i.e. build-tooling-only. **Until this is worked, every devloop's Layer 6 reds on a condition its author did not cause** — the reason it is P1 rather than P3 despite the advisories themselves being mostly dev-tooling. Owner: infrastructure + security (the task #47/#48 pairing that owns the suppression machinery). Priority: P1 (a permanently-red always-run gate trains reviewers to ignore it). **TRIGGER (added by team-lead 2026-07-30 on @operations' Gate-3 recommendation): the next devloop that reds on Layer 6 for this same condition MUST either work this entry or escalate it to the user — it may not proceed on "#58 already did."** Rationale: a P1 with an owner but no deadline decays into exactly the normalization this entry exists to prevent — the entry's own argument ("a permanently-red always-run gate trains reviewers to ignore it") applies to the entry as much as to the gate. #58 proceeded on a first-instance adjudication with stash-and-reproduce evidence; that adjudication is explicitly **not** precedent for a second.

- [ ] **SHA-pin all third-party GitHub Actions in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`** (2026-05-11, security Gate-1 follow-up from `2026-05-11-ts-wrappers-task36`). Currently all 7 third-party actions are tag-pinned (`actions/checkout@v4`, `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`, `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2`, `pnpm/action-setup@v4`, `actions/setup-node@v4`, `taiki-e/install-action@cargo-llvm-cov`, `codecov-action@v4`) — including one channel-pinned (`dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`). Industry best practice (OpenSSF Scorecard, GitHub Security Hardening) recommends SHA-pinning third-party actions. Bundle the conversion in a single PR per `pinact`/`stepsecurity` automation, ideally with renovate config for automated SHA-bumps. Priority: P2 (current tag-pinning matches widely-used convention; no known active threat; SHA-pinning new actions while leaving 5 existing tag-pinned would be a no-op improvement). Highest-priority target if the pass happens: `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable` (channel-pinned, not even tag-pinned). Owner: security + infrastructure.

## Polyglot Pipeline Follow-ups (ADR-0033 Wave 1 #1)

- [ ] **Run CI inside the devloop image (dev↔CI environment parity) — the durable fix for recurring CI drift** (surfaced 2026-08-09 on PR #59). ADR-0033 shares the pipeline *logic* between local and CI (both call `layer-all.sh`), but the *environment* is duplicated and drifts: the devloop image (`infra/devloop/Dockerfile`) bakes in Rust/Node/pnpm/buf/Chromium/cargo-audit/postgres, while `.github/workflows/{ci,ci-client}.yml` re-provision a subset imperatively. PR #59 hit **three failures, all environment/invocation drift (no product bugs)**: (a) `ci.yml` Layer-4 vitest browser component tests failed — no Chromium provisioned; (b) `ci-client.yml` hand-rolled `buf breaking` with `#branch=main` (a PR checkout has no local `main`) and `npx playwright` at repo root (not hoisted under pnpm); (c) `validate-story-manifest` failed — `dt-story` not built. All three were **interim-patched** (build dt-story in compile.sh; base-ref merge-base for buf; add a Chromium-install step to ci.yml + `pnpm --filter … exec`), but the ROOT is that CI re-implements what the devloop image + pipeline already provide. **Proper fix**: run CI jobs *inside the devloop image* via GitHub Actions `container:`, then just call `./scripts/layer-all.sh` — CI == dev by construction, zero drift. Scope: publish the devloop image (or a slim CI variant) to GHCR + pin; rewrite `ci.yml`/`ci-client.yml` to `container:`; postgres via `services:`; dev-user/permissions; **retire `ci-client.yml`'s hand-rolled steps** (it is a hand-rolled duplicate of `layer-all.sh`'s buf-lint/breaking + ts-lint + ts-guards + ts-test + ts-audit). Landing this removes the `INTERIM PATCH (see TODO)` blocks now marked in both workflows, and **subsumes** the "ADR-0033 §3 Layer-7 Playwright lane" item below. Peer to the production-parity debate items (§Multi-Cluster Networking: MC/MH routing; web-serving tier) — same "environment must be consistent across contexts" axis; good candidate for the same infra `/debate`. Owner: infrastructure + operations.

- [ ] **`ts_pii` has no secret-identifier limb** (2026-07-30, surfaced during task #58 Gate 1 by @paired-client + @code-reviewer). `ts_pii` consumes only `PII_TOKENS_CATEGORY_B` (`ts_pii.rs:19,80,96,105`), which is user-PII exclusively (`email`, `phone`, `ssn`, `credit_card`, …) with no secret identifiers. Consequence: **no secret identifier is detectable at a TS log site** — `console.log('t', userToken)` is caught by neither `ts_pii` (no secret vocabulary) nor `ts-no-secrets` (runtime variable, not a literal). **Task #58's `ts-no-retained-credentials` does NOT close this**: it is a *type-declaration* guard, not a *log-site* guard — though it is the first TS-scoped consumer of CATEGORY_A. Verified consumer→category map: CATEGORY_A only → `rust_log_secrets.rs:24`, `instrument_skip_all.rs:27`; CATEGORY_B only → `rust_pii.rs:19`, `ts_pii.rs:19`; **both** → `metric_labels.rs:37-40`. **Related defect**: the inline comment at `pii_vocabulary.rs` justifying the `accessToken` CATEGORY_A entry claimed it was "needed for `ts_pii` detection" — false, since `ts_pii` reads CATEGORY_B, and false on the day it landed despite a 2026-06-23 security sign-off. (Task #58 corrected the comment in place; the underlying limb is still missing.) Three resolutions: comment wrong / entry miscategorised / `ts_pii` meant to read both. **"Fix the comment" is the one resolution that is certainly wrong** — it preserves a sign-off for a mechanism that does not exist. **Severity: coverage gap, NOT a live leak** (`MeetingSession.ts:16-23` documents R-23 hygiene; a test asserts no join logs are emitted) — do not triage as a credential leak. Owner: security + infrastructure. Task-sized — adding a secret limb to `ts_pii` is not a one-liner.

- [ ] **CATEGORY_A vocabulary: promote `credential` / `credentials` / `creds`** (2026-07-30, task #58, @paired-infrastructure + @dry-reviewer). `\bcred\b` cannot match `credential` in the three Rust consumers (`rust_log_secrets`, `instrument_skip_all`, `metric_labels`), which read `PII_TOKENS_CATEGORY_A` directly and never see task #58's `STEM_EXPANSIONS` — that table fixed the *new* TS guard's matcher, not the catalog. Measured: the terms appear as identifiers in **15 Rust production files**, concentrated in `crates/ac-service/src/handlers/admin_handler.rs` (service-credential management — precisely where a credential-leak guard should have reach and has none). **Known FP before you start**: `admin_handler.rs:754` logs `credential_id = %credential.credential_id`, which `rust_log_secrets`'s `[%?]\s*WORD\b` branch would match on `%credential` — a line emitting a credential's *identifier*, not its secret, in a file with many sibling call sites. So the work is: add terms → work the FP surface (allowlist entries, or a shape distinguishing `%credential.field` from `%credential`) → before/after runs on all three consumers → security sign-off per the file header. Task-sized on verification surface and pipeline-blocking risk, not LoC. Owner: security + infrastructure.

- [ ] **Vocabulary reachability: THREE failure modes, and closing one is not closing the others** (2026-07-30, task #58, @dry-reviewer + @paired-infrastructure). Recorded as three because a follow-up scoped from the original "one root cause" framing would close Mode A, leave B and C open, and *look* closed. **Mode A — referential integrity under rename**: a structure references a string that must exist elsewhere; rename the target and the reference silently orphans (`HYGIENE_SOURCE_SCAN_SUBSET`, `STEM_EXPANSIONS` keys). Remedy: membership test — **both closed by task #58**. **Mode B — matcher-capability mismatch**: the entry is well-formed and correctly referenced, but the consumer's matcher cannot reach it. `accessToken` is unreachable in `metric_labels` because it lowercases before matching, so that consumer cannot see *any* camelCase catalog entry and the next one dies there on arrival too. A membership test passes and reveals nothing. Remedy: a per-consumer **self-match reachability** test — feed every CATEGORY_A entry to each consumer's matcher and assert it matches itself. **NOT closed.** **Mode C — absence masked by naming**: the term is not in the catalog at all while the vocabulary's naming implies it is (`cred` reads as covering credential-ish identifiers and covers exactly one spelling). Neither orphaning nor unreachability. **NOT closed** (see the promotion entry above). Root observation: the catalog is consumed by matchers that cannot do morphology, so entries chosen as stems or camelCase spellings silently match nothing — and task #58's guard is the first consumer that *normalizes*, which is why several surfaced at once rather than as unrelated bugs.

- [ ] **`is_ts_build_artifact()` extraction** (2026-07-30, task #58, @dry-reviewer ADR-0019 extraction opportunity, NOT a blocker). `ts_secrets.rs` and `ts_pii.rs` carry byte-identical `TEST_PATH_PATTERNS` (8) + `TEST_FILE_SUFFIXES` (5) + `is_excluded_path()`; ~54% of those entries are already shadowed by the shared `is_scan_exempt`, leaving 6 genuinely additive build-artifact entries. Task #58's `ts_retained_credentials` is the 3rd caller and correctly layers on `is_scan_exempt` rather than reimplementing it, so this stays an extraction opportunity rather than a BLOCKER. Both existing files' comments defer to a 4th caller; that threshold is now one away. Net LoC goes down.

- [ ] **Comment/string-aware line lexer duplicated 3× in `dt-guard`** (2026-07-30, task #58 Gate 2, @dry-reviewer ADR-0019 extraction opportunity, NOT a blocker). Three implementations now answer "walk a source line, ignoring comments and string-literal contents, so braces/parens inside them do not move a depth counter": `common/test_code_filter.rs::strip_comments` (private), `metric_labels.rs:320-345` (inline in a byte loop), and task #58's `ts_retained_credentials.rs::strip_noise` (private). **Why this is an extraction opportunity and not a BLOCKER**: `strip_comments` is not `pub`, so it could not have been imported — no shared code was bypassed, which is the ADR-0019 BLOCKER criterion. Third instance, so the extract-on-third-use threshold is reached. **Standardize on the `strip_noise` shape, not `strip_comments`**: `strip_comments` *preserves* string contents, which forces its caller (`DepthState::process_line`) to re-track string state on a second pass — the same lexing done twice, and the two passes can disagree. `strip_noise` drops string contents in one pass, so a single traversal is sufficient. Candidate home: `common/source_lexer.rs` (a named cross-cutting concern per ADR-0034 §Neutral — NOT `common/util.rs`). Constraint: `compute_test_block_ranges` is Rust-flavoured (`r#"…"#` raw strings) while `strip_noise` is TS-flavoured (backtick templates), so the extracted helper needs a flavour parameter or two thin callers over one core — do not collapse them into a single string-quote set. Owner: infrastructure.

- [ ] **Semantic-check enumeration drift guard** (2026-07-30, task #58, credited to @semantic-guard). Task #58 dereferenced all 7 copies of the semantic-check list to `scripts/guards/semantic/checks.md`, taking CLAUDE.md §Single source of truth's *derive-one-from-the-other* branch. The *guard-on-drift* branch — a subcommand that parses `## Check:` headings out of `checks.md` and asserts each enumeration site matches, modelled on `validate-gsa-sync.sh` — was deliberately NOT built (a second new subcommand in a loop that already added one, and lower-value once only one list remains). Revisit if inline example lists reappear. **Scope it to live governance/operational docs**: run unscoped it would fail on `docs/devloop-outputs/**`, where historical records legitimately cite paths that were correct when written. Note the load-bearing pointer is `.claude/agents/semantic-guard.md`'s Review Procedure step 1 — if that path rots, the agent executes **zero** checks and reports CLEAR.

- [ ] **Guard binaries are produced by a skip-gated verb and consumed by always-run guards — collapse three sites to one, and give the failure a lane** (2026-08-13, `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-13-run-story-seams-and-hermetic-tests/`, @paired-infrastructure with @operations + @code-reviewer + @test). **Read the framing before triaging: this is a collapse, not an addition, and the interim patches do NOT close it.** Two halves, and closing either alone leaves the other live.

  **Half A — producer/consumer skew.** `scripts/lang/rust/compile.sh:17-18` builds `dt-guard` + `dt-story` inside the *skip-gated* compile verb (`lang/_dispatch.sh` short-circuits on `lang/rust/changed.sh`, which triggers only on `crates/` + root `Cargo.*`/`rust-toolchain.toml`). Both are consumed by *always-run* Layer-3 guards: ~15 wrappers via `scripts/guards/simple/_dt_guard_wrapper.sh:74-77`, `validate-story-manifest.sh:16`, and (since this devloop) `scripts/workflow/run-story.test.sh`. So a diff touching only `packages/**` or docs skips the build and reds Layer 3 with a failure unrelated to the change. **Reproduced live** at this devloop's Gate 1 by @team-lead: `STATUS=FAIL REASON=dt-guard-binary-missing`, exit 1. Commit `225462a` hit the same symptom on PR #59 and fixed it *inside* the skip-gated verb, closing only the Rust-also-changed case.

  **This devloop added two interim assert/build sites and deliberately did not fix the root**, so there are now **three**: `lang/rust/compile.sh` (produces, skip-gated), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (produces, unconditional — **CI only**), `scripts/workflow/preflight-story.sh` (asserts, **story runner only**). Per @code-reviewer's framing, one invariant with three consumers: CI covered, story runner covered, **plain local devloop NOT covered**. The `ci.yml` step is also a second build site for the same two binaries, i.e. the CLAUDE.md §Single-source-of-truth hazard, accepted knowingly as interim. **Proper fix: move guard-binary production onto an always-run path**, which then *deletes* both the `ci.yml` step and the preflight binary assertion as a consequence. Three sites → one.

  **Half B — correct detection, wrong lane** (@operations' M4 class; the one M4 instance not closed by this devloop). A missing build artifact is a wiring fault, i.e. the operator class by `docs/runbooks/devloop-validation.md` §4's own convention — but `_dt_guard_wrapper.sh:74-77` and `validate-story-manifest.sh:16` both `exit 1`, `run_and_emit` (`lang/_common.sh:138-147`) collapses **any** non-zero to `STATUS=FAIL`, `layer3.sh` flattens a guard-level `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` the same way, and `FAIL` maps to exit 1. Consequence, now live: `run-story.sh` routes a gate exit 1 to a **task escalation**, so a wiring fault is recorded against whatever task was running — the exact misattribution this devloop's R-4 split exists to prevent, arriving through the one door R-4 structurally cannot close. **Half A does not fix Half B**: however the binaries come to be built, any other way they go missing still lands on the implementer lane.

  **Reproduced live, on a green diff, 2026-08-13** (@code-reviewer CQ-16, twice during Gate 3 of this devloop): concurrent teammate edits made `run-story.test.sh`'s containment proof exit 2 (a could-not-verify state), `run_and_emit` collapsed it to `STATUS=FAIL`, `status_to_exit_code` mapped it to 1, and the runner's R-4 split recorded an environment condition as a **task escalation against a clean diff**. This is stronger evidence than the missing-binary case and should be the entry's headline: the collapse does not merely mislabel a broken environment, it misfiles a correct one.

  **Scope — NO new enum value** (@operations, correcting an earlier framing of mine that would have made this follow-up both expensive and insufficient). `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` already means "the ENVIRONMENT the gate needs was unavailable" (`lang/_common.sh:240-242`; the Layer-7 cluster is an example, not the definition) and already selects the operator lane. A new value would resolve to the same lane — `status_to_exit_code`'s fail-closed `*` backstop sends any unmapped enum to 2 — so it buys nothing the `REASON` token does not already carry, at the cost of `__status_rank`, `status_to_exit_code`, `__layer_lifecycle_end`, the runbook STATUS ladder and layer-all's summary cells. The enum's job is to select a lane; two values selecting the same lane is expressiveness, not a fix, and should be priced as such if ever argued.

  The fix is two steps, in this order:
  1. **`run_and_emit` preserves the child's rc→enum mapping** (`lang/_common.sh:138-147` is `if "$@"` — it never reads `$?`, only zero-vs-non-zero). This is load-bearing **specifically for children that emit no STATUS line of their own**, because `tee_collect_statuses` worst-wins already carries the enum for children that do. That split is the whole justification: `_dt_guard_wrapper.sh:75` emits its own `STATUS=` line and needs only an enum change; `validate-story-manifest.sh:16` (bare `echo` + `exit 1`) and `run-story.test.sh` emit none, so their exit code is the only signal and `run_and_emit` discards it. Both cases that bit us are in the second class.
  2. **The two guard emitters use `PRECONDITION_FAILURE`** rather than `FAIL`/exit 1.

  Carry with it the `report_results` prefix assertion's sibling property, since whoever changes `run_and_emit` is standing next to it: `tee_collect_statuses:421` collects **line-start** `^STATUS=` from **stdout**, and the only thing keeping ten `_test_helpers.sh` consumers from voting captured status text into their enclosing layer's verdict is that `report_results` prefixes every line it prints. That is now asserted in `layer-all.test.sh` (case `h`, both halves); it was unasserted until this devloop.

  Operator-facing symptoms + the lane caveat are documented at `docs/runbooks/devloop-validation.md` §8 (three rows + "a missing guard binary reaches the implementer lane"). Owner: infrastructure (+ operations for the emitter contract). Task-sized: Half A touches the dispatcher's skip contract (ADR-0033 §3/§5 layering); Half B touches `run_and_emit`, which every layer script routes through.

- [ ] **Layer 1 proto-first stub is comment-only — ordering relies on bash glob alphabet** (2026-05-08, ADR-0033 Wave 1 #1, paired-operations finding). `scripts/layer1.sh:19-24` has the right comment ("Stage 1: proto first") and an `if [[ -d ".../lang/proto" ]]` guard, but both branches call `scripts/build.sh` identically. The dispatcher iterates `lang/<X>/` via bash globbing (alphabetical: `proto < rust < ts`), so proto-first happens by alphabet not intent. Wave 2 #4 implementer must either (a) add a `--lang-only proto` / `--lang-not proto` filter to the dispatcher and use it in layer1.sh, or (b) rely on alphabet plus prevent any future lang sorting before "proto". Wave 1 has only rust so ordering is moot in practice. Owner: protocol (Wave 2 #4).
