- [ ] **`scripts/lang/_dispatch.sh:106` `_ignored_rc=$rc` lint-suppressor variable** (2026-05-08, code-reviewer cleanup). The variable `rc` captures the verb wrapper's exit code but the dispatcher uses the parsed STATUS line as source of truth, ignoring `rc`. Either (a) actually use `rc` to validate STATUS-vs-exit-code consistency (fail loud if they disagree), or (b) drop the capture entirely with `"$verb_sh" "$@" >"$tmp_out" 2>&1 || true`. Cleanup; does not affect correctness. Owner: infrastructure. **Note (2026-06-08, task #50):** the EXIT trap added in task #50 makes a crashed wrapper self-emit `STATUS=FAIL`, so parsed STATUS is now authoritative even on crash — option (a) (STATUS-vs-rc consistency check) is now a natural fit, but was left untouched to keep task #50's diff scoped to the silent-skip region (per DRY-reviewer guidance). Still open.
- [x] **Cross-lang-masking residual: a missing verb-wrapper in ONE lang masked by a sibling's non-bug status** (2026-06-08, task #50, documented residual) — **CLOSED 2026-06-19, task #52.** Closed GENERALLY across every verb (compile/fmt/lint/test/audit) by splitting the wiring-fault meaning into its own STATUS enum `FAIL-MISSING-VERB` and ranking it at rung 5 — above `OK`/`FAIL`, below `UNKNOWN` — so a missing wrapper wins the aggregate over any sibling's clean run and the layer reds at exit 2 by construction (no reason-tiebreak, no per-slice post-processor). #50's audit-slice workaround in `scripts/audit.sh` (the grep-the-dispatch-output + emit-synthetic-`STATUS=FAIL` block) was retired; `status_to_exit_code` reverted to pure `f(enum)`; intentional gaps now register as placeholder `<verb>.sh` wrappers emitting `N/A` (replacing the `__intentional_missing_verbs` allowlist). NOTE — behavior change: a missing audit wrapper now reds at **exit 2** (`FAIL-MISSING-VERB`), where #50's post-processor emitted `STATUS=FAIL`/exit 1; exit 2 is the honest wiring-fault code (the gate never ran). See `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-06-19-polyglot-ladder-cleanup/` and ADR-0033 §6 (2026-06-19 amendment).
- [ ] **Signal-induced (SIGTERM/SIGINT) pre-emit abort trap coverage** (2026-06-08, task #50, test-reviewer deferral). The wrapper EXIT trap (`__wrapper_early_exit_trap`) fires on most signal-induced exits too (bash runs EXIT traps after a signal handler unless the signal is trapped separately), so the trap mechanism is already exercised by the explicit-exit + `set -e` abort cases in `_wrapper_trap.test.sh`. A dedicated `kill -TERM` + wait test adds a CI timing race for marginal incremental coverage. Add if a real signal-abort silent-skip is ever observed. Owner: test.
- [ ] **`_get_base_ref.behavior-equivalence.test.sh [7b]` fails: 11 dt-guard-migrated guards report no-callsite** (surfaced 2026-06-09 during task #50 Gate 3; verified by Lead to be PRE-EXISTING and orthogonal to task #50). The `[7b]` assertion checks `scripts/guards/simple/**` call-site coverage (ADR-0034 guard pipeline); the 11 guards reporting "no-callsite" are ones ported to `dt-guard` subcommands, so it's task #45 (guard→dt-guard migration) drift — the behavior-equivalence test needs updating for the dt-guard wrapper shape. Task #50 changed zero `_get_base_ref`/`guards/simple` files. Owner: infrastructure (task #45 surface) — needs its own review; do NOT fold into task #50.

- [ ] **`_get_base_ref.sh` CI-PR tip-SHA vs merge-base-SHA divergence** (2026-05-11, ADR-0033 Wave 2 #5, surfaced by `2026-05-11-ts-wrappers-task36`). `scripts/lang/_get_base_ref.sh` in CI-PR mode (lines 74-89) sets `base="origin/${GITHUB_BASE_REF}"` and resolves to that **tip** SHA on stdout. The changed-files cache (line 121) correctly uses three-dot diff (`base...HEAD`), but the SHA emitted on stdout is the base tip, not the merge-base. Downstream consumers passing `--base=$SHA` to a diff-aware tool (e.g. `pnpm exec nx affected --base=$SHA` without `--head`) get effectively two-dot semantics — over-including commits on `origin/${GITHUB_BASE_REF}` that arrived after the PR branched off. Conservative (false-positive on affected, not false-negative), so perf nit not correctness bug. Adds CI runtime cost on PRs against fast-moving branches. **Motivating example**: the four TS wrappers landed by task #36 (`scripts/lang/ts/{compile,fmt,lint,test}.sh`) consume `_get_base_ref.sh`'s stdout SHA. On a PR against a fast-moving `main`, Nx's `affected` set includes commits not in the PR. **Desired output**: emit `git merge-base origin/$GITHUB_BASE_REF HEAD` SHA in CI-PR mode instead of the tip. Local-mergebase branch is already correct (explicitly resolves merge-base). **Constraints**: cross-language change (Rust wrappers don't consume the SHA today but might in future); touches `_get_base_ref.test.sh` matrix; the asymmetry is documented at the source with an inline TODO comment at `scripts/lang/_get_base_ref.sh:86-90`. Owner: infrastructure (canonical resolver) + test (regression coverage). Priority: P3 (performance only; correctness preserved).

- [ ] **`_get_base_ref.test.sh` should explicitly assert rc=0-iff-non-empty-stdout** (2026-05-11, test-reviewer Gate-1 follow-up from `2026-05-11-ts-wrappers-task36`). The four new TS wrappers rely on `_get_base_ref.sh` either printing a SHA or exiting non-zero (per the empty-guard drop during Gate 1 — `set -e` propagation through top-level command-substitution is the contract). The existing test matrix asserts SHA *shape* and the `BASE_REF=` stderr-token presence, but the rc=0-with-empty-stdout regression case isn't explicitly defended. A future refactor masking an error inside a `|| true` would slip past the suite. Fortify with an `assert_nonempty_stdout` check across all 8 scenarios in `_get_base_ref.test.sh`. Bundle with the tip-SHA→merge-base-SHA fix above for coherent coverage. Owner: test (test specialist; not a #36 blocker per test reviewer's call).

- [ ] **`guard:ignore` is inert for 32 of 37 `dt-guard` modules — a suppression marker that silently suppresses nothing (2026-08-14)**: Surfaced at Gate 2 of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/main.md` by @implementer, verified independently by @code-reviewer; **pre-existing, not introduced by that diff**, which deliberately did not use the mechanism. `crates/dt-guard/src/ignore.rs` provides the `guard:ignore(REASON)` marker, but only five modules import `crate::ignore`: `alert_rules.rs`, `cite_extract.rs`, `dashboard_panels.rs`, `metric_labels.rs`, `ts_metric_naming.rs`. The other 32 — including `rust_log_secrets.rs`, which is where the surfacing devloop would have reached for it — never consult it.

  **Why it is worse than "the annotation does nothing".** The immediate behaviour is fail-loud and therefore safe: write `guard:ignore` in a file checked by a non-consuming guard and the guard still fails, so nobody ships on a false pass. Two slower failure modes are the real cost. (a) **The annotation reads as a review artifact.** `.claude/skills/devloop/review-protocol.md` §guard:ignore Annotations makes an unjustified marker a reviewer finding, which trains readers to treat a *justified* one as a consciously-waived check. An inert marker asserts that a guard was waived on purpose when it was never evaluated — the code carries a false claim about its own review status, and the reviewer who accepted the justification is the one it misleads. (b) **Dormant markers activate silently.** The day any of the 32 modules adds `crate::ignore` support — a one-line import, exactly the kind of change that reads as plumbing — every pre-existing marker in its file set becomes a live suppression at once, with no review of the guard hits each one now hides. That is a latent-suppression bomb whose blast radius grows with every marker written in the meantime.

  **Shape of the fix, in preference order**: (1) make the marker fail loudly where unsupported — `ignore.rs` exposes the supported-module set, and a `guard:ignore` found in a file checked by a non-consuming guard is itself a guard error ("guard:ignore is not honoured by <rule>"). That converts a silent no-op into a diagnosable one and closes both failure modes without touching 32 modules. (2) Wire `crate::ignore` into the remaining modules — larger, and each addition needs its own review of what becomes suppressible, so it should follow (1) rather than precede it. Do **not** do (2) alone: it is precisely failure mode (b) executed deliberately. A guard-level test asserting every module either consumes `ignore` or rejects the marker would keep the invariant from decaying. Owner: operations (guard pipeline) + code-reviewer (suppression-surface policy, ADR-0034). Defer trigger: the next devloop that needs a suppression in a non-consuming guard, or any change adding `crate::ignore` to a module.

- [ ] **`scripts/guards/common.sh::get_*_files` extension filter is regex-interpreted, not literal-string** (2026-05-12, ADR-0033 Wave 2 #6). `get_modified_files` / `get_added_files` / `get_deleted_files` / `get_all_changed_files` / `has_changed_files` all filter via `grep "${ext}$"` where `${ext}` is a regex. A pattern like `.test.ts` literally matches `featureXtest.ts` because `.` is any-char. Surfaced via @test Gate-3 Finding 1 on `no-test-removal-ts.sh`; a local collector rewrite in that guard bypasses the helper. Other guards remain latently exposed where downstream processing does not re-filter by literal-suffix. **Fix**: in `common.sh`, change `grep "${ext}$"` to `grep -F -- "${ext}"` plus a literal suffix re-check, OR escape regex metachars in `${ext}` when building the pattern. Cross-guard behavior change — needs its own devloop with infrastructure + operations + all guard owners present. Owner: infrastructure (canonical helper). Surfaced during ADR-0033 Wave 2 #6.

- [ ] **Layer A scope-drift guard `--continue`-mode parser upgrade** (2026-05-18, ADR-0033 Wave 1 §scope-drift guard, surfaced at Iter-2 Gate 2 attempt 1 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-14-semantic-guard-relocation-task40/`). `scripts/guards/simple/validate-cross-boundary-scope.sh` parses the first `## Cross-Boundary Classification` H2 heading in a devloop `main.md` to derive the planned-file set; for `--continue` mode this mismatches the active iteration's scope because the original top-of-document table is Iter 1's. Workaround applied at Iter 2 Gate 2: top-of-document table replaced with the active iteration's rows, earlier iterations' tables archived under §Implementation Summary as historical reference. **Proper fix**: parse the §Cross-Boundary Classification table nearest the most-recent `### Iteration N Scope` subsection (or alternatively the table inside the most-recent `## Human Review (Iteration N)` block); fall back to the only table when no iteration markers are present. Owner: operations (devloop scope-drift guard). Priority: P3 (workaround clean; matters at the next `--continue` devloop and would prevent the re-organize-main.md friction repeating).

- [ ] **RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (rsa 0.9.10 Marvin timing sidechannel) — pre-existing supply-chain debt** (surfaced 2026-05-15 at L6 Gate 2 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-14-semantic-guard-relocation-task40/`; not present at task #38's Gate 2 PASS on 2026-05-12, so advisory database update in the intervening window). The `rsa` crate (0.9.10) is pulled transitively via `sqlx-mysql 0.8.6`. **No fixed upgrade available upstream** as of 2026-05-15; the `rsa` crate has not released a patched version. - [ ] **`async-std` unmaintained warning from `opentelemetry_sdk 0.24.1` transitive** (surfaced 2026-05-25 at L6 Gate 2 of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-23-common-otel-helper-grpc-interceptor-task24/`; introduced as a side-effect of bringing the OTel SDK into the workspace via R-54). Cross-cutting because all four service `Cargo.toml`s consume `common`. Owner: observability + operations. Priority: P3 (warning only; not in runtime path). Re-verified 2026-06-07 (task #48): workspace-wide `cargo tree -i async-std -e normal` returns empty. Suppression status: SUPPRESSED since 2026-06-07 (task #48, security-approved noise-only suppression of the non-gating warning), expires 2026-09-05 — entry in `audit-suppressions.toml` (the file formerly anticipated here as `audit-config.toml` landed under that name in task #47); renewal playbook in the manifest comment block. Operations deferred 2026-05-25 (review of devloop `2026-05-23-common-otel-helper-grpc-interceptor-task24`).

- [ ] **`filter_test_code` rustc-span precision upgrade (Wave 3)** (2026-05-21, surfaced during Wave 2 #45 in-loop). Wave 2 landed `crates/dt-guard/src/common/test_code_filter.rs` as a brace-counter detector for `#[cfg(test)]` / `#[cfg(any(test, ...))]` / `#[cfg(all(test, ...))]` blocks — promoted from Wave 3 to Wave 2 in-loop after the path-based heuristic had a 92% false-positive rate on the Wave-2 corpus. The brace counter has known precision limitations: `#[cfg_attr(test, ...)]` is NOT detected (strict `cfg(test)` only); multi-line `#[cfg(...)]` attributes are not lexed across lines. A `syn::parse_file` AST walk would report exact `#[cfg(test)]` attribute spans (byte ranges) for surgical filtering. No nightly dep needed (unlike bash today's `strip-test-code.sh`). Owner: infrastructure. Pointer back: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-20-wave2-bash-guards-dt-guard/main.md` §Decisions item 1 + §Accepted Deferrals.
  - Sub-case: `#[cfg(test)] use ...;` / `#[cfg(test)] pub const ...;` / `#[cfg(test)] fn ...;` non-block attributes cause the brace counter to over-exempt the NEXT item's block. Test fixture at `crates/dt-guard/src/common/test_code_filter.rs:491-507` documents the case. `syn`-based port closes it via item-aware attribute attachment. Surfaced by @security Gate-2 review 2026-05-22 (F-Wave2-S1, LOW, RESOLVED-DEFERRED; current in-tree usage is overwhelmingly `#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {` — survey of 171 sites found no current FN).

Wave 2's `dt-guard test-coverage` subcommand implements quick-mode only (warning-only, exits 0 — matches bash default). Rationale for deferral: full-mode is an external-tool dispatch (llvm-cov) with long runtime and its own exit-code semantics; layer-4 already runs `cargo test`; coverage-threshold enforcement is better as a Layer-7 separate concern. Owner: test. Pointer back: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-20-wave2-bash-guards-dt-guard/main.md` §Accepted Deferrals.

- [ ] **Python in the guard pipeline — language strategy + toolchain plumbing (debate-worthy)**. Surfaced 2026-05-14 during task #39-followup (`docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-14-doc-citation-durability-guards/`) Gate-3 close-out when the user asked whether the new `.py` module was a fresh dependency. Audit found six pre-existing simple guards already use inline Python heredocs (`validate-alert-rules.sh`, `validate-infrastructure-metrics.sh`, `validate-dashboard-panels.sh`, `validate-application-metrics.sh`, `validate-metric-labels.sh`, `grafana-datasources.sh`) — multi-month convention, never formalized. Task #39-followup added the first **committed `.py` module** at `scripts/guards/lib/doc_cite_extract.py`, consumed via `sys.path.insert(0, "<repo_root>/scripts/guards/lib")` by three guards. Current gaps: no `requirements.txt` / `pyproject.toml` / version manifest anywhere; no Python interpreter version pin (devloop image relies on Debian-bookworm's system `python3` = 3.11, would drift silently on image bumps); no Python lint or type-check guard (Rust has clippy, TS has eslint, proto has buf lint — Python has nothing); no documented import convention; `__pycache__/` `.gitignore` entry added without a rationale comment. Three plausible end states for the debate to converge on: **(A) Standardize on Python** — formal `scripts/guards/lib/` package + `requirements.txt` + Python lint guard (e.g. ruff) + version pin in a shared shell helper; rewrite simple bash guards where Python would be cleaner. **(B) Standardize on bash** — rewrite the 6 existing heredoc guards in pure bash (or jq/yq for structured data); abandon the committed `.py` module + revert task #39-followup's Python module to bash. **(C) Formalized polyglot** — keep both with a written contract for choosing each (e.g., "structured data parsing → Python; pattern matching → bash") and add the missing Python toolchain plumbing. Suggested `/debate "Python in the guard pipeline — formalize, consolidate, or back away?"` with participants infrastructure (devloop image + scripts/) + operations (guards + runbooks) + security (Python ReDoS / supply chain) + test (guard validation surface) + code-reviewer (style + consistency + ADR compliance) + dry-reviewer (cross-guard duplication patterns). Likely produces an ADR. Out of scope: rewriting individual guard logic; this is about language strategy + toolchain plumbing only. Owner: operations (debate convener); resolution likely ADR-track.

- [ ] **Layer-table row duplicated between `SKILL.md` and ADR-0033 has now intentionally diverged, with nothing recording why** (surfaced 2026-07-29 by @test at task #20 Gate 2). The Always-Run/Skip-If-Untouched Layer 7 row exists verbatim in two places: `.claude/skills/devloop/SKILL.md` (§Always-Run vs Skip-If-Untouched matrix) and `docs/decisions/adr-0033-polyglot-validation-pipeline.md` (§3 canonical layer table). Task #20 marked the SKILL.md copy "Playwright @smoke: pending #18/#19" and left the ADR copy reading "+ Playwright @smoke". **That divergence is correct, not a bug** — the two documents have different genres: SKILL.md is an operational instruction read present-tense by every devloop, so an unbuilt tier must be marked; the ADR is a design record of what was decided, and adding status markers to ADRs runs directly against the tracked decision in §Documentation Hygiene ("Remove status/tracking content from all ADRs — ADRs are now design records only"). The problem is that **the justification is written down nowhere**, so a future reader diffing the two rows sees a mismatch and may "correct" it in either direction — re-introducing the present-tense claim into SKILL.md, or pushing status content into the ADR. Fix options: (a) one inline note on the SKILL.md row stating it deliberately carries build-status the ADR does not, and why; (b) have the SKILL.md row cite the ADR as the design target rather than restating its text, so there is one copy. (b) is the CLAUDE.md single-source-of-truth answer and is the smaller surface. **Do NOT implement the guard shape originally proposed for this** (asserting the SKILL.md row against `scripts/layer7.sh`'s actual command): @test proposed it at Gate 1 and then withdrew it — such a guard would flag every legitimate forward reference as drift, which is exactly the pattern ADR-0033 §3 and the #56 Meta-note say to *mark pending*, not delete. A guard here would have to compare the two doc rows to each other, not either row to the script. Natural pickup is story task #19, which already opens `SKILL.md` `--paired-with=operations`. Owner: test (SKILL.md Layer-7 text) + operations (SKILL.md file). Provenance: task #20 Gate-1 row-7 exchange, @team-lead ruling 2026-07-29.

## Code Quality

- [ ] **The org concurrent-meeting cap is advisory, not enforced, under concurrent statements (2026-08-14)**: Spin-out accepted at Gate 1 of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/main.md` (@security S-6, owner @database, co-signed @security; Lead ruled it out of that diff's scope). `MeetingsRepository::create_meeting_with_limit_check` executes its count and its INSERT in one statement, which removes the *application-level* check-then-insert window — there is no separate `SELECT` round trip during which the count can change. It does **not** serialise concurrent executions of that statement: under READ COMMITTED the `current_count` CTE takes no row locks and no constraint enforces the cap, so N concurrent creates against a cap of M can overshoot to M+N-1. The module doc comment previously claimed the CTE "prevents TOCTOU race on concurrent meeting limit"; that claim has been corrected in-tree to state exactly the above, so this entry tracks the behaviour, not a lie in the source.

  **Scope the fix as the pattern, not this call site.** `crates/gc-service/src/repositories/participants.rs:38` and `:84` both name `create_meeting_with_limit_check` as the model for the future join-capacity CTE, so that CTE will inherit the same race for **participant** capacity — where an overshoot matters materially more than a soft meeting-count limit (a room over its participant cap is a media-plane problem, not a billing one).

  **Why it was not fixed opportunistically**: every option serialises meeting creation per-org and carries throughput/deadlock consequences that want measuring rather than guessing — `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the org row, `SERIALIZABLE` isolation with retry, or a per-org advisory lock. That is a database-domain decision with a load profile attached.

  **Note for whoever picks this up**: the cap test cluster in `crates/gc-service/tests/meeting_create_tests.rs` (cap exhaustion, `'active'`-status counting, cross-tenant scoping) drives **sequentially** and therefore exercises the cap *value*, never its enforcement under concurrency. A green cap suite is not evidence the cap holds under load; the source carries the same warning at the cluster. Owner: database (co-signed security). Defer trigger: the join-capacity CTE landing, or any report of an org exceeding `max_concurrent_meetings`.

- [ ] **175 bare `#[allow(...)]` suppressions workspace-wide carry no `reason` attribute — ADR-0002's justification requirement is unenforced (2026-08-14)**: Surfaced at Gate 3 of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/main.md` by @code-reviewer; **pre-existing, not introduced by that diff**. ADR-0002 §"Suppressing Lints with `#[expect]`" requires suppressions to explain themselves at the suppression (checklist: "Each `#[expect]` has a `reason` attribute explaining why"). `grep -rn '#\[allow(' crates/*/src --include=*.rs | grep -v reason` returns **175** hits; that devloop's `errors.rs` fix is the **only** use of `reason =` with `allow` anywhere in `crates/`.

  **`reason` works on `allow`, not just `expect`** — stabilised with `lint_reasons` in Rust 1.81, and this workspace is on 1.95 (verified by compiling `#[allow(dead_code, reason = "…")]` under `#![deny(dead_code)]`). So the ADR's intent is achievable everywhere `#[expect]` is unavailable, and "we had to use `allow`" is not a reason to drop the justification. `#[expect]` genuinely cannot be used in several of these: a service crate whose `main.rs` re-declares its modules privately gets the lint firing for the bin target but not the lib, so an enum-wide `#[expect]` is unfulfilled in the lib build and warns there instead — that is the situation the GC `errors.rs` fix documents, and it will recur.

  **Why it is worth closing rather than knowing.** A bare `#[allow]` is textually identical whether it was reasoned about or pasted, so a `grep` for unjustified suppressions cannot rank them and a reviewer must reconstruct each argument from scratch. Roughly **11 of GC's 27** have no justification in *any* form — not even a trailing `//` — including `services/mc_assignment.rs`, `repositories/meeting_assignments.rs` (×2), `repositories/media_handlers.rs`, `services/mc_client.rs`, `models/mod.rs`, and the `#[allow(unused_imports)]` pairs in `repositories/mod.rs` (×3) and `services/mod.rs` (×2). Of those that do carry a comment, several assert a future that has since arrived or been abandoned — `// Will be used in Phase 2+`, `// Will be used in Phase 3 for scope checking`, `// Will be used in future phases` (×3) — which is the same decay the GC fix found on the `GcError` enum, where a blanket `#[allow(dead_code)]` labelled "Variants will be used in Phase 2+" had been suppressing nothing for the library for an unknown length of time while masking all fifteen variants.

  **Shape of the fix**: mechanical pass adding `reason = "…"` to every `#[allow]`, converting to `#[expect]` wherever the lint genuinely fires for all targets (which makes it self-retiring), and deleting the ones whose stated future has arrived — the last category is the valuable one and is only discoverable by attempting the conversion, since an `#[expect]` that turns out unfulfilled is the compiler telling you the suppression is dead. Best done per-crate to keep review tractable; GC and AC are the densest. A follow-on guard asserting every `#[allow]` in `crates/*/src` carries a `reason` would keep it from re-accumulating, and belongs with that work rather than before it. Owner: code-reviewer (ADR-0002 policy) + each service specialist for their crate. Defer trigger: the `clippy::pedantic` workspace consolidation below, which will surface a fresh backlog of suppressions and is the natural moment to set the convention.

Wire them up or drop them from the arg parser + usage. - [ ] **Consolidate `clippy::pedantic` to workspace lints**: 6 crates declare `#![warn(clippy::pedantic)]` at the crate root (`crates/common/src/lib.rs`, `crates/media-protocol/src/lib.rs`, `crates/mh-service/src/lib.rs`, `crates/proto-gen/src/lib.rs`, `crates/mc-service/src/main.rs`, `crates/mh-service/src/main.rs`); the other production crates (`ac-service`, `gc-service`, `mc-service` lib) do not. Move `pedantic = "warn"` to `[workspace.lints.clippy]` in the root `Cargo.toml` and remove the per-crate declarations. Why follow up: (a) eliminates the manifest-vs-attribute precedence confusion that bit `duration_suboptimal_units` (2026-04-27 — workspace `allow` was overridden by crate-level `#![warn(clippy::pedantic)]`); (b) makes lint policy a single source of truth; (c) extends pedantic enforcement to crates that don't currently get it. Cost: cleanup of however many pedantic lints are currently latent in `ac-service` + `gc-service`.

- [ ] **MH WebTransport server never sets `max_idle_timeout`/`keep_alive_interval` — same unbounded disconnect-detection gap MC closed in task #64**: `crates/mh-service/src/webtransport/server.rs` (~`bind()`, near line 123) builds its `ServerConfig` with no QUIC idle timeout or keep-alive, so a crashed / network-lost media client is only detected via the quinn library default — not config-driven (a per-project-conventions violation) and with an undocumented bound. MC just fixed the analogous gap (config `MC_QUIC_MAX_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, fail-loud parse rejecting 0, keep-alive derived as idle/3). Fix: mirror the MC pattern in MH with an `MH_QUIC_MAX_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` config threaded into `bind()`, and document the media-path worst-case crash-detection bound. Surfaced 2026-08-06 by @dry-reviewer's non-DRY heads-up during task #64 (roster-leave-latency) Gate 3 — NOT a shared extraction (MC's `keep_alive_for` is N=1), a parallel MH-owned implementation. - [ ] **Distinct `ambiguous-basename` REASON token in Guard C** — `resolve_basename_match()` returns `None` on BOTH "zero matches" and ">=2 matches" cases; the caller currently reports both as `file-missing` in the VIOLATION line. A 3am operator hitting an ambiguous case sees `file-missing` and goes hunting for a typo when the real fix is to disambiguate via full path. Fix: change `resolve_basename_match` to return a sentinel (e.g., a `BASENAME_AMBIGUOUS` constant) for the ≥2-matches case and have the caller emit `ambiguous-basename` instead. ~10-line change in `scripts/guards/lib/doc_cite_extract.py` + both guard scripts. Owner: operations. Surfaced 2026-05-14 by @operations Gate-3 nit (non-blocking, post-merge).

- [x] **`shopt -s extglob` non-restoration in `path_matches_glob` (cross-script visibility)**: `scripts/guards/common.sh::path_matches_glob` enables extglob without restoring (pre-existing posture, carried verbatim from `validate-cross-boundary-classification.sh` during the 2026-05-08 Pattern C promotion). Now that the function lives in `common.sh` and is consumed by both Layer A (`validate-cross-boundary-scope.sh`) and Layer B (`validate-cross-boundary-classification.sh`), any guard that sources `common.sh` AND calls `path_matches_glob` leaves extglob enabled for the rest of that script. Operations surveyed all 20 guards sourcing `common.sh` and found none rely on extglob being off, so status-quo carry is operationally safe today. Future hardening: save prior state via `local prev_extglob; shopt -q extglob && prev_extglob=on || prev_extglob=off`, then restore conditionally before return. Single-line change, low risk. Owner: operations + dry-reviewer. Source: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-08-layer-a-scope-drift-parser-fix/main.md` Tech Debt References. (resolved 2026-05-08 via devloop-outputs/2026-05-08-extglob-restore-and-deadcode-cleanup)

Owner: global-controller. Related pre-existing GC wire-shape debt already tracked above: env-tests `gc_client.rs` mirror divergence (§Cross-Service Duplication entries 2026-05-23) and `gc-deployment.md` `mcUrl` staleness (§Documentation Hygiene 2026-05-23).

- [x] **dt-guard binary-surface STATUS-line assertion deferred to Wave 1.5 (Wave 1 #11 semantic-guard tech-debt pointer)**: Resolved 2026-05-19 in `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-19-wave1-python-guards-dt-guard/` in-loop fix-pass (landed early, not deferred). New `crates/dt-guard/tests/binary_status_surface.rs` carries 2 `assert_cmd` tests: (a) `nonzero_exit_emits_kebab_case_status_fail_reason` builds tempdir with malformed `infra/docker/prometheus/rules/bad.yaml`, invokes `dt-guard alert-rules-policy --root <tempdir>`, asserts first stdout line starts `STATUS=FAIL REASON=` + REASON is `[a-z0-9-]+` + ≤60 chars; (b) `unknown_subcommand_exits_nonzero_with_clap_diagnostic` documentary smoke for clap branch. Pins ADR-0033 watch-point #2 by binary surface.

- [x] **`buf breaking` oracle blind to package-rename-via-file-move** (surfaced 2026-05-20 during task #31 R-61 part 3 close-out; resolved 2026-05-20 same day): Root cause was the `WIRE_JSON` ruleset's deliberate scope — it covers binary-wire compatibility only, and package-rename-via-file-move is a symbol-set break rather than a wire-encoding break (same field tags, types preserved; protobuf wire format doesn't include the package name on the wire). The harness (`scripts/lang/proto/breaking.sh:61`) was doing the right thing; `WIRE_JSON` was the wrong default. **Fix landed**: `proto/buf.yaml` `breaking.use:` switched from `[WIRE_JSON]` to `[FILE]`. FILE catches symbol-set breaks (`PACKAGE_NO_DELETE`, `FILE_NO_DELETE`, message removal); verified against task #31's commit, FILE correctly emits "previously present file deleted" findings. The v2-migration concern (CONVENTIONS.md §2 additive-sibling pattern) is unaffected by FILE since additive siblings don't remove anything. **Override pattern for intentional wire-breaks** (no mechanism, accepted convention): the human initiating a wire-break devloop names the expected Layer 6 fire in the `/devloop` prompt (e.g. "EXPECTED LAYER-6 STATE: buf breaking will fire on the package rename, accepted per R-NN precedent"); Lead accepts at Gate 2 as the documented exclusive-window state, same pattern used for task #29's Layer 5 expected-fail. Revisit if intentional wire-breaks become common enough that a mechanical override (commit trailer / annotation) earns its keep.

- [ ] **GC telemetry F1 byte-bucket test silently no-ops on global-recorder install race (task #10 follow-up, 2026-06-21)**: `crates/gc-service/tests/telemetry_metrics_integration.rs::telemetry_payload_bytes_lands_in_byte_bucket_not_overflow` proves the F1 distinct byte-bucket matcher by installing the global Prometheus recorder via `init_metrics_recorder()` and asserting a 50 KiB sample renders into `le="65536"`. Only ONE install wins per test binary, so on `Err(_)` (a sibling test already installed) the test does `return` and silently no-ops. The metric NAME coverage is independently guaranteed (in-src `metrics_module_emits_telemetry_payload_bytes` + green coverage guard), so the byte-scale proof only needs to win the install race once — acceptable, and the test comment documents it honestly. **Future nicety** (non-blocking, NOT changed in task #10 per @observability code review): make it deterministic via a `#[serial]`-style gate OR by asserting on the in-process histogram snapshot's bucket boundaries (`MetricAssertion`) rather than the rendered global handle, removing the install-race skip. Defer trigger: a future change to the telemetry metrics or a flake in the F1 proof. Surfaced 2026-06-21 by @observability Gate-2 code review of task #10 (GC telemetry proxy). Owner: global-controller / observability.

- [ ] **Env-test Prometheus poll helpers mask query/transport errors to 0.0 (fail-loud gap, 2026-08-04)**: `crates/env-tests/tests/26_mh_quic.rs` `mc_participant_mh_status_counter` (~line 735) and sibling `mh_notification_counter` (~line 334) map `Err(_)` from the Prometheus query to `0.0`. During the 60s poll a real transport/query failure therefore surfaces as a misleading "counter did not rise above baseline" panic instead of the underlying error — the exact masking `docs/CLAUDE.md` fail-loud conventions forbid. The new TS counterpart (`packages/web-app/e2e/mcMetrics.ts`, task #18) deliberately THROWS on query/transport failure and treats only an empty instant vector as 0 — port that distinction (empty-vector⇒0, Err⇒propagate) to the Rust helpers. Surfaced by @observability during task #18 review (pre-existing, outside that changeset — not routed through fix-or-defer there). Owner: test (env-tests). To be scheduled: fold into the next env-tests devloop.

- [ ] **10 of 16 `scripts/**/*.test.sh` have no invocation site — and a grep cannot tell you which (2026-08-13)**: Accepted deferral recorded by @test at Gate 3 of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-13-run-story-seams-and-hermetic-tests/main.md` (M3). There is no `*.test.sh` auto-runner, so an unwired self-test passes by never executing. Verified 2026-08-13: `find scripts -name '*.test.sh'` → 16; filtering to real execution sites → **6**, all in `scripts/layer3.sh`. The other ten: `scripts/lang/_common.test.sh`, `_dispatch.test.sh`, `_get_base_ref.test.sh`, `_get_base_ref.behavior-equivalence.test.sh`, `_layer_skeleton.test.sh`, `_wrapper_trap.test.sh`, `scripts/lang/proto/changed.test.sh`, `scripts/lang/rust/changed.test.sh`, `scripts/lang/rust/behavior-equivalence.test.sh`, `scripts/lang/ts/changed.test.sh`.

  **Why a convention cannot close this**: a mention is indistinguishable from an invocation to a grep. All ten are name-dropped in this file, ADR-0033 and the `docs/specialist-knowledge/*/INDEX.md` files, so the naive check a maintainer would actually reach for returns "referenced, looks fine" for every one of them. That is what makes the gap durable rather than merely present, and it is the argument for a guard over a convention.

  **Home is already built**: `crates/dt-guard/src/test_registration.rs` solves the identical failure class in the other language — its doc comment is this entry's sentence with `crates/*/tests/<subdir>/*.rs` swapped for `scripts/**/*.test.sh` ("Catches the common mistake of adding a test file in a subdirectory without registering it — those tests silently don't run"). Rule-id plumbing, `Finding`/`explain` output and guard-runner wiring all exist. Scope this as **"add a second scope to an existing guard"**, not "design a new guard".

  **Decision the follow-up must make, not default**: full triage of all ten (each is plausibly superseded rather than forgotten, and wiring them all has a runtime cost against ADR-0033 §4's 90s always-run budget) versus a frozen-baseline ratchet that reds only on new arrivals. The ratchet delivers the "don't add an 11th" mechanism immediately but institutionalizes the ten, which sits against CLAUDE.md §Fail loudly; never mask. @test leans ratchet-with-expiry on the `audit-suppressions.toml` precedent — this repo's existing answer to exactly that tension — but it is a real decision.

  **Generalize the acceptance criterion beyond "has an invocation site."** The devloop that produced this entry found a *wired, executing* case that was still vacuous, and named the class better than "unrun test" does: **a case whose expected outcome is also the outcome of the mechanism never running.** Its `h3` asserted `assert_absent '"decision":"block"'` against `devloop-stop-hook.sh` — satisfied equally by the hook allowing the stop, by the hook erroring, and by the hook not existing. Measured: with the hook repointed at a nonexistent path, `h3` PASSED while its sibling `h2` caught the deletion. The repair was to assert **both halves of the contract** the hook documents at `devloop-stop-hook.sh:11` ("Exit 0 with no output = allow"), which is what distinguishes "allowed" from "never ran". Two consequences for this entry: (a) the sweep that finds these must be **mechanical, not argued** — `h3` was outside the predicted-vacuous subset, which is the whole case for sweeping; (b) **`scripts/layer7.test.sh` is the known instance to check next** — it installs a fake `dev-cluster` at `:57-77` that drops no ran-marker, so across 469 lines no case can distinguish "the path I meant to test ran" from "another path produced the same status".

  **Base rate, measured in one gate.** Four defects in the devloop that produced this entry were found only by *executing* a lane, never by reading it, and all four had been read and approved by multiple reviewers: OPS-1 (removing `|| true` unmasked a write failure, but a bare `jq >"$rec"` under `set -e` then aborted before the loud message could print — the repair had made the report unreachable); the `find`-exit silent lane; case `h`'s blind assertion; and OPS-4 (`--stop-after` falsely refusing any task with deps — verified against this repo's own story manifest, where `--stop-after=3` and `=4` both refused tasks the runner reaches normally). **A fifth was found by a case that did not exist while reading as though it did** — the suite's `E4` was a `# E4:` heading followed by a fixture restore and no `e4-` label anywhere, which is what let OPS-4 through a suite built specifically to catch untested lanes. Hence the companion rule now in `scripts/workflow/run-story.test.sh:46-52`: **a comment is not a case** — every `# En:` heading must be followed by assertions carrying that label, with the grep to check it. Any guard built for this entry should enforce that too; it is the same predicate one layer up.

  **Stated limit of the obvious technique**, so the follow-up does not over-trust it: mutation testing proves the tests you *have* are load-bearing and says nothing about the lanes you never wrote. Catching the never-written class requires enumerating lanes against the code, not reading the suite. Owner: test. To be scheduled.

## From ADR-0024 §6 Amendment (2026-04-18)

- [ ] **Plan-template ⟶ Layer A + Layer B guards + Gate 1 invocation + Gate 1 reviewer-checklist**: template extension already shipped. Author `validate-cross-boundary-scope.sh` (Layer A: diff vs. plan file list, runs at Gate 2) and `validate-cross-boundary-classification.sh` (Layer B: GSA-not-Mechanical + Owner-field-filled, runs at Gate 1 primarily and Gate 2 as safety net); build ownership manifest mapping GSA paths to required specialists; add Gate 1 invocation step to SKILL.md Step 5 (already drafted in amendment); add Gate 1 checklist item to `.claude/skills/devloop/review-protocol.md`. See ADR-0024 §6.8 item 1. Owners: operations + test + security + code-reviewer. Medium devloop.
- [x] **GSA sync guard** (resolved 2026-04-20): implemented as `scripts/guards/simple/validate-gsa-sync.sh`. The guard became the 5th mirror by design — canonical GSA list + INTERSECTION_SUBPATHS allowlist hardcoded in the guard; bidirectional subset checks against all 4 existing mirrors (ADR §6.4, SKILL.md, review-protocol.md, cross-boundary-ownership.yaml). Catches: canon-missing-from-mirror, mirror-count-mismatch, typo'd-stray-YAML-key, canon-ahead-of-mirrors. Only silent case is triple coordination failure (anchor-of-truth comments + human review mitigate).
- [ ] **The Cross-Boundary Classification table cannot express "deliberately not touched" (2026-08-14)**: Surfaced at Gate 2 of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/main.md`. `validate-cross-boundary-scope.sh` parses **every** table row as a planned file and flags plan entries the diff does not touch, so a row reading "NOT TOUCHED" is mechanically indistinguishable from a plan entry the implementer failed to fulfil. @database had specifically asked for two such rows (`migrations/**`, `.sqlx/`) so the untouched-ness was explicit rather than an omission — a reader six months out cannot distinguish "considered and deliberately excluded" from "never thought about it" when the entry simply is not there. Interim resolution: moved to a prose **"Deliberately NOT touched"** section beneath the table.

  **Durable fix: the scope guard grows a `NOT-TOUCHED` assertion.** A structured token, *not* prose. The assertion is the exact inverse of what the guard already runs — "no diff path matches this glob" versus "every table path is touched" — i.e. a set-membership test over `git diff --name-only` with **no intent-parsing anywhere**, and cheaper than the positive case. (Recorded because the first justification offered for the prose section was that a marker would force the guard to parse intent; @database corrected it, and a recommendation resting on a wrong premise gets re-litigated the first time someone checks it.)

  **Why it is worth building rather than living with the section**: prose under a heading is unparsed, so nothing stops a future devloop asserting "deliberately not touched: `migrations/**`" while its diff touches `migrations/`. That matters most precisely where it is most tempting to skip — `migrations/**` is a **Guarded Shared Area**, and untouched-ness is exactly the claim that ought to be machine-verified rather than asserted.

  **Explicit limit, so it is not overclaimed**: a `NOT-TOUCHED` assertion is **vacuously true for a glob that matches nothing**, so it would *not* catch the ADR-0024 §6.4 `db/migrations/**` dead-glob problem (that path matches zero tracked files in this repo, which uses `migrations/`). That needs the separate ≥1-tracked-file check @operations is tracking. Two different holes; this closes one. Owner: operations + database. Defer trigger: the next devloop that needs a deliberate-absence row, or the dead-glob check landing (build both together — they are the same guard).

- [ ] **ADR-0024 §6.4's schema-evolution GSA glob matches zero tracked files — the control is inert repo-wide (2026-08-14)**: Surfaced at Gate 1 of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/main.md` by @security and @database independently; Lead ruled it out of that diff's scope (five mirrored edits plus a guard change from inside an unrelated devloop would put a change to the *definition* of a security control into a diff nobody is reviewing for that). All five mirrors declare the path as `db/migrations/**` — ADR-0024:404, `.claude/skills/devloop/SKILL.md:134`, `.claude/skills/devloop/review-protocol.md:30`, `scripts/guards/simple/cross-boundary-ownership.yaml:44`, and the CANON array at `crates/dt-guard/src/gsa_sync.rs:40`. Ground truth: `git ls-files | grep -c '^db/migrations/'` → **0**; `'^migrations/'` → **8**. `db/migrations/` has never existed in this repo.

  **Operational consequence.** `cross-boundary-ownership.yaml:44` is what `validate-cross-boundary-classification.sh` reads to enforce the two rules in review-protocol.md:52 — GSA paths cannot be `Mechanical`, and GSA paths with a non-`Mine` classification require an Owner in the manifest. Because the glob matches nothing, **both rules are currently inert for schema evolution**: a migration can be classified `Mechanical` and land with no `Approved-Cross-Boundary: database` trailer, with every automated gate green. Schema evolution is a GSA precisely because migrations quietly change security-relevant structure — CHECK constraints, unique indexes, `ON DELETE CASCADE` behaviour — so this is a live gap in a security control, not a documentation typo.

  **Three parts to the fix, and the third is the durable one.** (1) Correct the string in all five mirrors together. (2) Add an existence assertion to the sync guard: fail if any CANON path matches zero tracked files — without it the next mirrored path typo reproduces this exactly, and the string correction alone just resets the clock. (3) Record the generalization, because this is the *third* instance found in one devloop of the same shape.

  **Why it evaded detection, which is the reusable lesson.** `validate-gsa-sync` **passes**. It compares the five mirrors against each other, and they are perfectly consistent — it has no notion of whether the path being synchronised corresponds to anything on disk. A drift guard that checks internal consistency rather than correspondence to reality is structurally blind to exactly one failure mode: every mirror being wrong in the same way. That is also the failure mode people most trust it to have excluded, which is what makes it dangerous rather than merely incomplete. Two sibling instances found in the same devloop share the shape — `guard:ignore` is silently **inert** for `no-secrets-in-logs` because `rust_log_secrets.rs` never imports `crate::ignore`, so an annotation reads as a justified suppression while suppressing nothing; and `no-secrets-in-logs` Check 4's "Category-A word anywhere on the line" test scored four false positives on message prose in `crates/gc-service/src/handlers/meetings.rs` while being blind to a real credential reachable through a derived `Debug` in the same crate (fixed under that devloop's finding S-7). **The missing predicate in all three is the same: does this control's target actually exist?** No amount of mirror-consistency, annotation-syntax, or pattern-match checking supplies it. A control that passes its own self-check while protecting nothing is worse than an absent control, because it consumes the attention that would otherwise go to building a real one. Co-signed from the semantic side by @semantic-guard, who is recording the `Debug`-reachability half as a Rust extension to Credential Leak in `scripts/guards/semantic/checks.md`.

  **On whether this needs a micro-debate**: @security's **recommendation** — recorded as a recommendation, not a decision, per Lead's Gate 1 ruling — is that it does not. ADR-0024 has already decided schema evolution is guarded; correcting a path string so an already-accepted control actually fires is repair, not extension, and the mirror comment gates *extending* the GSA list. Reading it to also gate *repairing* a broken entry leaves a dead control dead pending a debate, which inverts the purpose. The owning specialists get the final call in the devloop that does the work. Owner: operations (owns ADR-0024 §6), co-sign database (`migrations/**` is theirs). Defer trigger: the next devloop touching `migrations/`, or the `NOT-TOUCHED` assertion above landing — build both together, they are the same guard.

- [ ] **Light-mode reviewer-specialist coverage gap** (observed 2026-04-20 during GSA sync guard devloop): light mode spawns implementer + security + one context reviewer. If the work touches files outside both reviewer slots' domains (e.g., this devloop touched 3 code-reviewer-owned anchor-comment files with no code-reviewer in the team), those files land without owner-specialist sign-off. Operations+security covered via adjacent concerns, but strictly the cross-boundary review wasn't complete. Options: (a) upgrade to full when files-outside-reviewers condition detected; (b) auto-add code-reviewer as 4th light-mode teammate when non-light-reviewer-owned files are in scope. Not blocking; observe in 2-3 more devloops before fixing. Owner: operations + code-reviewer.
- [ ] **Cross-boundary ownership coverage beyond GSA — broader file-type ownership map** (surfaced 2026-04-20 during /close-story devloop): ADR-0024 §6 and the `cross-boundary-ownership.yaml` manifest today cover only Guarded Shared Areas (Tier 1: auth/crypto/wire/audit/migrations — narrow, high-risk). Non-GSA files touched during cross-boundary work (skills, infra, docs, agent defs, runbooks, CI, observability conventions) have no ownership map — Layer A catches scope drift, but the Owner field in the plan's Cross-Boundary Classification table is free-text with no mechanical validation.
  
  Tension observed while designing a fix: coarse ownership maps (e.g., `infra/** → infrastructure`) are cheap to maintain but misclassify service-specialist-owned artifacts (e.g., `infra/docker/prometheus/rules/mc-alerts.yaml` is MC-owned per ADR-0031, not infrastructure-owned; `.claude/agents/{name}.md` is self-owned per convention). Fine-grained maps capture correct ownership but grow maintenance cost substantially. Applying GSA rule (a) "Mechanical disallowed" to Tier 2 paths also creates friction on common legitimate service-specialist work.
  
  Options considered (not resolved):
  - Option 1/2: Two-section manifest (GSA vs Tier 2), rule (a) only for GSA. Requires Tier 2 map.
  - Option 4: Single flat manifest, rule (a) everywhere. Either high friction (coarse map) or high maintenance (fine map).
  - Option B (earlier): docs-only — rejected because guard failures would be ignored.
  
  Revisit when someone has bandwidth to think through ownership granularity + rule scope carefully. Owner: code-reviewer + operations + dry-reviewer.
- [ ] **§6.2's auto-ESCALATE clause does not distinguish an accepted upgrade from a contested one — four devloops have now had to resolve it by hand** (filed 2026-08-13 by @paired-infrastructure at @team-lead's direction, `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-13-run-story-seams-and-hermetic-tests/`). ADR-0024 §6.2 says reviewers may upgrade a classification "at Gate 1 or during Gate 3 review" and, in the next sentence, that "Classification challenges auto-route to ESCALATE"; `review-protocol.md` §Classification Monotonicity repeats the second half without the first. Read literally, raising an upgrade **at the gate the ADR itself prefers** forces an ESCALATED verdict even when the implementer amends on first ask — which penalises the cheap behaviour (Gate 1, before code exists) and rewards holding the concern until Gate 3, where amending is expensive.

  **Not a novel misreading — it recurs, and it has already been arbitrated once.** `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-23-camelcase-wire-migration/main.md:63` recorded the same ruling in-flight ("consensual classification refinement stays in normal Gate-1 flow — this is NOT a disputed challenge, so no ESCALATE route"), and `:464` filed it as a lesson learned, noting the implementer "initially over-interpreted this as ESCALATE-class". `2026-05-06-test-utils-package/main.md:82,88` carries two owner-requested upgrades handled in normal Gate-1 flow; `2026-07-29-client-credential-lifetime-guard-task58/main.md:1043` carries a @code-reviewer upgrade (*"no guard covers wrapper-registration"*) likewise. This devloop is the fourth, with five upgrades across three reviewers, and required a fresh @team-lead arbitration to reach the conclusion already recorded in 2026-05-23. **A rule that must be re-arbitrated every time it is exercised is a defect in the text, not in its readers** — the cost is paid in Lead turns and in reviewers acquiring a mild disincentive to upgrade at all.

  **Interim rule (@team-lead, 2026-08-13, binding until the text is fixed)**: ESCALATED is reserved for an upgrade that **survives to Gate 3 contested**. An upgrade raised at Gate 1 and accepted without contest is recorded in the Gate-3 Ownership Lens — raised, by whom, on what grounds, honoured at Gate 1 — and does not force the verdict. Grounds: the §Verdict Format taxonomy defines ESCALATED as "unresolved disagreement", which is the narrower and therefore governing instrument; and §Classification Monotonicity's own stated purpose is to stop owners "being argued down during fix-or-defer triage", a Gate-3 activity.

  **Fix**: one clause in ADR-0024 §6.2 distinguishing an upgrade the implementer accepts from one that survives contested, mirrored into `review-protocol.md` §Classification Monotonicity (which currently states only the auto-route). Both documents change together or the seam simply moves. ADR amendment, so it does not ride in a devloop that merely trips over it. Owner: operations + code-reviewer.

- [ ] **DRY reviewer retrospective audit on Ownership Lens verdict field**. See ADR-0024 §6.8 item 3. Owner: dry-reviewer.
- [x] **Reflection-phase INDEX drift as recurring Layer A trip** (resolved 2026-04-20): Layer A was tripping at pre-commit time on reflection-phase INDEX.md additions because `run-guards.sh` runs post-reflection via the git pre-commit hook, after the plan table was frozen at Gate 1. Original framing ("dog-fooding caught real drift") was wrong — it was a false positive from Layer A running outside Gate 2. Fixed by making Layer A symmetrically exclude `docs/specialist-knowledge/**/INDEX.md` (reflection artifacts, owner-authored, out of Layer A's drift scope) from both plan_paths and diff_paths in `check_main_md()`. See `scripts/guards/simple/validate-cross-boundary-scope.sh:186-201`.

**Reshaped or dropped from earlier drafts** (for history):

- ~~`validate-cross-boundary-approval.sh` at Gate 2~~ — reshaped into Layer A + Layer B guards above. Original framing was flawed (no commits at Gate 2).
- ~~Commit-time trailer consistency check~~ — dropped. Owner confirmation is now satisfied by Gate 1 review + Gate 3 Ownership Lens verdict. Trailers remain available as an optional audit convention (ADR-0024 §6.7) but are not enforced.
- ~~APPROVED-CROSS-BOUNDARY classification-failure fixture suite~~ — dropped. Simplified Layer B guard's narrow rules are reviewer-verifiable during authoring; no separate fixture suite needed.
- ~~Scope/claim/session-field rename guard~~ — deferred indefinitely. Compiler + reviewers cover most cases; residual risk (non-Rust string renames) is narrow.

(task #47). Suppression is ONLY ever added there via a reviewed PR (ADR-0033 §11) — never
via CLI flags, hand-edited derived files, or Dependabot alert dismissals. See
`docs/contributor/audit-suppressions.md`.

| id | ecosystem | expires | ticket | summary |

Full rationale in the manifest `#`-comment + Cluster C above. |

Test-build-graph-only via opentelemetry_sdk 0.24 `testing` feature (dev-dep of common) — verify with `cargo tree -i async-std -e normal` (must be empty); fail-closed if a normal-graph consumer appears. The always-run `scripts/audit-suppressions-check.sh` (Layer-3 guard) hard-fails any entry past
`expires`; the weekly `audit-scheduled.yml` scan catches new advisories against unchanged deps.

- [ ] **Suppression expiry early-warning (EXPIRING_SOON nudge)** — DEFERRED from #47. Optional,
  non-posture (the hard `expires` FAIL is the enforcement). Best built as a fast-follow once the §F
  scheduled scan is established, so the nudge rides the scheduled job / a digest rather than per-PR
  log noise (an EXPIRING_SOON line emitted only on ad-hoc pipeline runs misfires — the suppression
  owner rarely sees it). If built: a distinct greppable `EXPIRING_SOON=<id>:<days_left>` line, NEVER
  `STATUS=WARN` (§6 enum has no WARN member; the hard `expires` FAIL stays the only mechanical teeth).
  Owner: observability + security. **Depends-on:** the §F drift-catcher (`audit-scheduled.yml`,
  landed in #47).

- [ ] **PATH-1 fail-safe e2e acceptance test (real pnpm tree)** — DEFERRED from #47, accepted by
  @code-reviewer at Gate 2 (security concurred). The §D.1.2 PATH-1 invariant (malformed
  `.pnpm-audit-ignore.json` ⇒ ts wrapper applies ZERO suppressions and the scan still RUNS, FAILing
  on a real advisory rather than erroring-out-before-scanning = denial-of-coverage) is pinned at the
  FUNCTION level in `scripts/audit-suppressions-check.test.sh` (gate-filter malformed→rc0/zero-ids/WARN
  + valid + absent cases). The end-to-end variant — inject a known advisory into a real pnpm tree,
  malform the ignore-file, assert `lang/ts/audit.sh` reaches its scan decision and FAILs on the
  advisory — needs a real pnpm fixture + a known-advisory package, which is cross-tool fixture
  infrastructure beyond #47's changeset. Function-level floor is sufficient to prevent silent
  regression to fail-abort; the e2e adds proof the scan path itself runs under a malformed filter.
  Owner: infrastructure + test (paired security on the invariant). **Depends-on:** a reusable
  pnpm-advisory test fixture.

ADR-0033 §12 14-day MTTR tripwire applies on a per-advisory basis. No new advisories introduced; lockfile last touched in `d918343`.

Until both Devloop I + Devloop II land, every PR's CI run will Layer 6 RED on the cargo-audit half — accepted per ADR-0033 §10 explicit acceptance of always-run-failure visibility over silent bypass. Security ACKed the deferral on this devloop 2026-05-08 (relayed via team-lead). The 9 lockfile rows (6 errors + 3 unmaintained warnings) cluster along three axes:
  - **Cluster A** (5 of 9 rows): RUSTSEC-2026-0037 (quinn-proto DoS), RUSTSEC-2025-0009 (ring AES panic), RUSTSEC-2025-0010 (ring unmaintained), and both RUSTSEC-2025-0134 entries (rustls-pemfile 1.0.4 + 2.2.0 unmaintained). Closed by Devloop II.
  - **Cluster B** (3 of 9 rows): RUSTSEC-2026-0099, RUSTSEC-2026-0104, RUSTSEC-2026-0098 (all rustls-webpki). Closed by Devloop I.
  - **Cluster C** (1 of 9 rows): RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (rsa via sqlx-mysql). Closed by Devloop I.

  Document context: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-08-pnpm-audit-ts-lang/main.md` § Devloop Verification Steps Layer 6 — "Deferral" + "Spin-out plan — 2 devloops, A/B/C cluster split".

- [ ] **Devloop I — Cluster B + Cluster C (small, fast, ~3-4h)** (depends on `2026-05-08-pnpm-audit-ts-lang` landing first). Specialist: `infrastructure --paired-with=security`. Closes 4 of 9 rows. Two scope items:

  - **Cluster B**: `cargo update -p rustls-webpki@0.103.10` (closes RUSTSEC-2026-0098, -0099, -0104). **Security pre-condition for Gate 3 sign-off**: implementer must surface resolved `rustls` / `rustls-webpki` / `quinn` version pins in `Cargo.lock` for security review before merge — "surgical" depends on the bump not unintentionally pulling the rustls 0.22+ chain widening. If chain widening occurs, scope re-evaluates against Cluster A (this becomes part of Devloop II rather than landing alone).

  - **Cluster C**: rsa@0.9.10 ignore in audit config with rationale (closes RUSTSEC-2023-0071). **Policy text of record** per ADR-0033 §11 (security-owned threshold + ignore-list) — verbatim TOML block authored by @security 2026-05-08, to land in `audit-config.toml` exactly as written:

    ```toml
    [[ignore]]
    id = "RUSTSEC-2023-0071"
    # Crate: rsa 0.9.10 (Marvin Attack — timing side-channel on RSA decryption)
    #
    # Exposure analysis (security, 2026-05-08):
    #   - Pulled in transitively by sqlx-macros-core's compile-time query-
    #     validation scaffolding, NOT by any runtime driver we select.
    #   - Workspace sqlx config (Cargo.toml): features = ["runtime-tokio",
    #     "postgres", "uuid", "chrono", "migrate"]. No "mysql" feature; no
    #     runtime code path constructs an rsa::* primitive.
    #   - cargo tree -p rsa --invert (verified 2026-05-08) confirms only
    #     build-time consumers; no shipped binary links rsa runtime.
    #   - Marvin Attack requires attacker-observable RSA decrypt timing on a
    #     server we operate. We don't operate one — we don't ship rsa code.
    #
    # Upstream status:
    #   - No fix available in rsa 0.9.x. Constant-time RSA primitives in Rust
    #     remain an unsolved upstream problem (see rust-lang/rsa#19, et al.);
    #     waiting for an rsa fix is waiting indefinitely.
    #
    # Sunset:
    #   - Re-evaluate by 2026-08-08 (90-day default per ADR-0033 §11 audit-
    #     config policy). Re-evaluation MUST re-verify the build-time-only
    #     claim above against the then-current Cargo.lock — if any runtime
    #     driver in our deps starts pulling rsa::*, this entry FAILS-CLOSED:
    #     remove the ignore, fix, OR re-justify with new exposure analysis.
    #   - Permanence note: this entry is expected to be the longest-lived in
    #     audit-config.toml because no upstream fix is on the horizon. That
    #     does NOT make it unconditional — the build-time-only invariant is
    #     the load-bearing claim, and that invariant must be re-checked at
    #     each sunset.
    ```

    Operational substance preserved alongside the TOML block:

    - **Verification command of record**: `cargo tree -p rsa --invert` is the command for re-verifying the build-time-only invariant at each sunset/tripwire fire.
    - **90-day sunset** (re-evaluate by **2026-09-01** — re-anchored to the manifest-landing date when task #47 created `audit-suppressions.toml`; the original 2026-08-08 figure was computed from the 2026-05-08 authoring date, before the manifest existed, and is now stale): heavy active re-justification — re-run `cargo tree -p rsa --invert`, confirm runtime tree is still empty, re-affirm in-tree that the rationale still holds. The mechanical teeth are the manifest `expires = "2026-09-01"`, enforced by the always-run `scripts/audit-suppressions-check.sh`.
    - **Fail-closed condition** (also encoded in the TOML rationale): if any runtime driver starts pulling `rsa::*`, the ignore expires immediately — no extension, no grace period.
    - **ADR-0033 §12 14-day MTTR tripwire continues to apply** per security's explicit decision; no special-casing. **Tripwire vs sunset** distinction: tripwire = light periodic re-verification of "still build-time-only, still no fix" (cargo tree + advisory-db check); sunset = heavy active re-justification (full rationale review). Both fire, both add value, they do not conflict.
    - Cluster C is **expected to be the longest-lived entry** in `audit-suppressions.toml`. The rationale block anchors that expectation so the 2026-09-01 re-justification reads as routine active maintenance, not process failure.

## Layer 7 Playwright Lane (ADR-0033 §3 vs `layer7.sh` drift)

- [ ] **ADR-0033 §3 promises a Layer-7 Playwright lane that does not exist** (surfaced 2026-07-30 at task #58 Gate 1 by @test + @operations independently; **pre-existing drift, NOT introduced by #58** — recorded here rather than as a finding against that diff). ADR-0033:152 gives the Layer-7 row as "dev-cluster + Rust env-tests + Playwright `@smoke`", and :87 lists `e2e.sh # nx affected -t test:e2e (Playwright)` in the dispatcher layout. Neither exists: `scripts/layer7.sh:410` runs `cargo test -p env-tests --features all` and nothing else, `scripts/lang/ts/e2e.sh` is absent (ADR-0033:479 says it lands "when task #15 lands"), and `packages/web-app/playwright.config.ts` + `packages/web-app/e2e/` are task #18 deliverables. **Consequence at task #58**: verification item (c) ("`MeetingSession.join` succeeds against the live Kind cluster using ONLY a userToken") had no executable lane, and was re-scoped to #18 by joint @test + @operations ruling — the server half is covered by `crates/env-tests/tests/24_join_flow.rs::test_mc_webtransport_connect_and_join` (now pinned observationally via `AuthClient::call_count()`), the client half moves to #18. Between #58 and #18 the browser join path has **no live-cluster coverage at all**, and #58 is the commit that changed that path's contract. **Requirement on #18**: wiring the lane into `layer7.sh` (or `lang/ts/e2e.sh` + the Layer-7 call site) is part of the task, not a follow-up — specs that no layer invokes read as coverage and provide none, which is the same masked-coverage shape #58 spent its review budget removing. Either wire the lane or correct ADR-0033 §3 so the matrix stops promising it. Owner: test (#18) + operations (pipeline surface).

- [ ] **Browser-E2E lane fails on missing generated protobuf code — no pipeline step ever runs `buf generate`** (surfaced 2026-08-14 at Gate 2 attempt 1 of devloop `2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes`; **pre-existing pipeline defect, NOT introduced by that diff** — recorded here rather than as a finding against it). Layer 7's Rust env-tests passed (`STATUS=OK REASON=env-tests-passed`); the browser suite failed with `Cannot find module '/work/packages/sdk-core/src/proto/dark_tower/signaling/v1/signaling_pb.js'` → `No tests found` → `STATUS=FAIL REASON=browser-e2e-failed`. **Root cause, verified**: `packages/sdk-core/src/proto/**/*_pb.ts` is gitignored (`.gitignore:13`) generated output, produced by `buf generate` per `proto/buf.gen.yaml:6` (`out: ../packages/sdk-core/src/proto`) via the Nx `proto-gen:codegen` target. `grep -rn "buf generate" scripts/ .github/` returns **nothing** — no pipeline step generates it; `scripts/lang/proto/compile.sh:22` runs `buf build`, which only validates. And `scripts/layer7.sh:574` invokes the suite as `pnpm --filter @darktower/web-app test:e2e`, which selects the package and runs its script directly, **bypassing the Nx task graph** — so the `web-app → proto-gen:codegen` dependency never fires. This is exactly **F5 in `docs/runbooks/client-dev-local.md` ("Vite cannot resolve `signaling_pb.js`")** reproduced inside the validation pipeline: same idiom, same bypass, same missing artifact, in the lane whose job is catching what the other layers cannot. **Why it fires now**: the browser lane is diff-triggered and `crates/gc-service/` is in `__BROWSER_E2E_TRIGGER_PATHS` (`layer7.sh:134`), so it correctly triggered; Layer 1's proto stage is skip-if-untouched and this diff touches no `proto/`, so codegen never ran. **Any diff that triggers the browser lane without touching `proto/` hits this**, which is most of them — a diff that *does* touch `proto/` masks it, because Layer 1 then regenerates the artifacts as a side effect. That masking is why it survived this long. **The status was wrong, not just the outcome**: it reported the *implementer* lane (`FAIL`) for a missing build artifact, which is an *operator*-lane precondition. Under the skill's Layer 7 policy that consumes an implementer attempt, so an unlucky devloop could exhaust both attempts on an environment fault and escalate against a clean diff — **R-4's defect** (an environment failure recorded as an implementer failure), inside the story whose whole purpose is making those lanes trustworthy. **Fix**: `layer7.sh` already has a `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` lane with `dev-certs-missing` (`:504`) and `playwright-browser-missing` (`:510`); a third check for the generated proto artifact belongs there — cheaper and more honest than making the invocation Nx-aware. Better still is both: the precondition check makes the failure *legible*, while switching the invocation to `pnpm nx run` makes it *not happen* (Nx caches codegen, so it is ~free when the proto is unchanged — see F5's "Fix"). Whichever lands, it needs a hermetic case in `scripts/layer7.test.sh`, per that file's existing convention. **Deferred from the surfacing devloop deliberately**: `scripts/layer7.sh` is not in that diff, the fix has genuine design ambiguity (precondition-check vs Nx-aware invocation vs both) and needs its own test cycle, and changing how Gate 2 executes from inside a Gate-2-blocked devloop is not a safe trade. **Note for whoever picks this up**: the Lead cleared the precondition for that run by executing `pnpm nx run proto-gen:codegen` manually. That unblocked the run; it did **not** fix the defect, and the next devloop that triggers the browser lane in a fresh container fails identically. Owner: operations (`scripts/layer7.sh`), coordinating with test (Layer 7 lane policy) and client (`proto-gen` target wiring).

- [ ] **Layer 7 reports environment faults on the implementer lane — generalized from two distinct instances in one devloop** (surfaced 2026-08-14/15, devloop `2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes`). The bullet above is instance 1: a **missing build artifact** (`buf generate` never runs) reported as `STATUS=FAIL REASON=browser-e2e-failed`. Instance 2 is **accumulated cluster state**: on the frozen-tree re-validation the browser suite failed 4/4 with `MeetingForbiddenError: Organization meeting limit exceeded` — R-7 cap exhaustion on a shared `devtest` org (`max_concurrent_meetings DEFAULT 10`, `migrations/20250118000001_initial_schema.sql:10`), the same suite having passed 8/0 an hour earlier on the same cluster, with no production code marking meetings ended. Same misreport: `FAIL`, implementer lane. **The generalizable defect is that `layer7.sh` cannot distinguish "the environment cannot support this test" from "the code failed this test."** The two instances need different remedies — instance 1 a precondition check for the generated artifact, instance 2 owned by task #3 (R-7 per-run org provisioning) — but both need the *same* lane fix, which is why they are recorded together: fixing only the artifact case leaves the misattribution intact. **Positive discriminator available in both cases, and cheap**: on the 2026-08-15 run the Rust env-tests **passed while successfully creating meetings** (`crates/env-tests/tests/23_meeting_creation.rs`) in the same execution where the browser suite hit the cap. A create path that works for one suite and refuses another is org-state-specific by construction, not a code regression — a check `layer7.sh` could make itself rather than leaving a human to reason it out post hoc. **Note the dependency inversion this creates**: instance 2's fix (task #3) is gated behind the commit of task #2, so the story cannot resolve it without first proceeding past a red it predicted. That is a structural argument for the lane fix, not just an inconvenience — an operator-lane classification would have let this run be re-run or waived without an adjudication. Owner: operations.

## Guard Timeout vs Violation — a gate that can invent its own red

- [ ] **A guard timeout is reported in the same shape as a guard violation, so machine load is indistinguishable from a code defect** (surfaced 2026-08-14 at Gate 2 attempt 2 of devloop `2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes`; raised jointly by @operations and @implementer, with @team-lead recording that they created the contention that produced it). The pipeline was launched into the middle of a concurrent `cargo` workload; Layer 3 — 13s on a quiet machine — took **713s** and returned `STATUS=FAIL REASON=guard-timeout-validate-kustomize`. L1 went 10s→975s and L4 186s→954s in the same run. **Nothing was wrong with the code, and it consumed a real Gate 2 attempt on a clean diff.** **The defect is the REASON taxonomy, not the timeout**: `guard-timeout-*` and a genuine violation both surface as `STATUS=FAIL`, so a reader — human or the story runner's classifier — cannot distinguish "this guard found a problem in your diff" from "this guard never finished measuring." **Prior art for the fix is one layer up and already accepted**: `layer7.sh` draws exactly this line with `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` (operator, exit 2) vs `FAIL` (implementer, exit 1), documented in `docs/runbooks/devloop-validation.md` §3's STATUS ladder with `scripts/guards/common.sh:__status_rank` as the rank SSoT. A timeout is an environment fact about the machine, not a fact about the diff, so it belongs on the operator lane by the same argument R-4 makes for exit 2. **Distinct from the sibling entry above**, and worth keeping distinct: the Layer-7 codegen defect is a control that reports *about the code without observing it*; this one is a control that **fabricates a verdict out of machine load** — a gate that can invent its own red rather than one that misses a real one. A false red trains reviewers to re-run rather than read, which is how a real red gets re-run away. **Scope note**: raising the timeout is not the fix, and neither is removing it — an unbounded guard hangs the pipeline. The fix is that the *classification* be honest, so an operator-lane timeout can be re-run without spending an implementer attempt, and so an under-contention run is legible as such rather than as a diff defect. Owner: operations (`scripts/guards/common.sh` STATUS taxonomy, `docs/runbooks/devloop-validation.md` §3 ladder), coordinating with test (the story runner's failure classifier consumes these REASONs). Not work for the surfacing diff — it changes how every layer reports.
